This Booke with the others of medicins, preserves,
and Cookerye, My lady Catchmay lefte with me.
to be delivered to her Sonne Sr William Catchmay
Earnestly desiringe and Chardginge him to lett
every one of his Brothers and Sisters to haue
true Coppyes of the sayd Bookes, or such parte
therof as any of them doth desire, In witness that
this was her request, I have herevnto sett my
hand at the delivery of the sayd Bookes.

Ed Bell

Iesus:
1 3
A booke of medicens:

A prayer to be sayd at all tymes to defend thee
from thy Enemyes: 1.

In the name of Iesus, In the name of Iesus, In the name of Iesus, in the name of Iesus
Iesus, Iesus, as my trobles doe Come; lorde so lett them goe; the father  mee, the sonne
 mee, the holy ghoste  mee, three persones & one god, three in trinitye & one
in vnitie, saue,  keape & defend me from all my Enemyes this daye & evermore:
Christ is mersifull & I am sinfull, I beseache thee sweete Iesus forgive me my
sines, & graut me my bonne, that I may have the vpper hand over all my Enemyes
this daye & evermore: the spiritt of god stand betweene & me & all my Enemyes
this daye & evermore: In the name of Iesus, in the name of Iesus in the name of
Iesus: in the name of Iesus. Iesus, Iesus, in the name of the father of the sonne, &
of the holy ghoste: so be it: Amen.

The order of drawinge yor aquacomposita:

Halfe yor sinamu yor lignu allowes, the Rootes & seedes of Angelica must be reserved
out of the brasse pott & knitt in a linen bagge, & layd into the receyvor, wher
yor lycour must Rune into, & by & by yor Alkauitt must be putt into the receyver
also reserve out of the brasse pott all yor herbes & flowers vntill the last draught
come; All yor other spices, seedes, rootes & gomes must be putt wth halfe the Ale
in the brasse pott, then sett yor lymbecke & close it fast, keapinge the head of
yor limbecke alwayes wth could water, & drawinge it so longe, as it will come
good wch is tryed by castinge alittle of it into the fier, yf it burne it is good
or els nott, then take of yor limbecke charely, & power out all the stuffe that
is in the little bagges that is in the Receyver, & all the rest of yor flowere &
herbes & a gallon & a halfe of freshe Ale (beinge the other halfe) putt vnto
them, & sett on yor limbecke, & drawe it as you did before, & the sinamu &
sugar was lefte must be putt in the Receyvor agayne, when all is drawen out,
putt yor first draught & last together & keepe it for yor vse:

howe to make a perfume to preserve mankind from the Infection
of the plauge, & also to bourne it in presses for the
preservation of Clothes, beddinges or hanginges 2.

Take puer ould giniper, shave it as thine as maye be an vnce of the flower & herbe
of Saint Iohns worte halfe an vnce, of the Gummes of olibanu, of masticke, of puer
Chosen mirre, of eche of thos one vnce, yor gumes grinde greate, yor saint Iohns worte
hacke smale yor shavinges of giniper in lyke sorte , then mixte all together; & putt
them in some convenient vessell, or boxe, then add therto puer turpentine of venishe as
muche as will make it lyke a peece of paste, take the quantitiye of halfe a nvttmegg, &
laye it on a hott tyle eyther in yor Chamber or in the parlor beneathe when you
goe to bed at nighte./

The order of makinge three kindes of pepper
for expellinge of Wynde:  3

Take of puer suger fower vnces, Rose water as muche as will moysten yor suger wch is
in the posenett, then haue redye thes spices followinge, White pepper, black pepper, of
eche of thes halfe a drame, puer gallingale sliced, & fine minced, ginger pared & fine minced
of eche of thos one scrupell & a halfe, puer Chosen curbitt white & goumey cleane scraped
thine slyced & finelye minced two scrupells, yor Rose water & suger scume it as cleane
as may be & then lett them boyle wth a softe fire, then putt in all yor spices aforesaid
& sturre them diligentlye, then take it from the fier, & sturr it till it be could &
thicke, & thes putt into a glasse pott & reserve to yor vse, of this you may take the
quantitye of a peny or two pence at a tyme: -

3 4

An oynement for the heate of the reynes or els
Wher in the bodye:-

Take of the oyle of Roses sixe vnces, of cleare white waxe two vnces, as fine as may be scraped,
thes resolve together on the fier, & scume them as cleane as may bee then haue a dishe of
Rose water, & three sponefull of Rose vineger mixt together, strayne yor other stuffe vnto it, &
labour it a longe tyme in the lycour, & last of all haue redye camphion in as fine powder
as may be a drame, & worke it till it be could, the vse of this oynement is for the backe
& head as for the eyes:

The makinge of the oyle of chery otherwise called
the winter gill-flower: 2.

Take what quantitye you will of yor flowers wey them, put the like weight of puer hogges
larde not salt & a quarter of the weight more & stampe them together, & putt vnder a softe
sweete fier seethe them till the leafe be parched & then strayne them & then put so manye
flowers agayne & so serve them three tymes:

+ To make oyle of popular budds: 3.

Take of popular budds halfe a pownde cleane pickt & stampt smale ?ep of hogges greace wch
is very puer take the like weight & halfe as muche more, this stampe together in a stone
morter to one substance, then putt it into an earthen pott close covered that no ayer
come into it, lett it stand so six dayes till it be horey, then take it forthe & putt it onto a
cleane panne, & lett it boyle on a softe fier alwayes sturinge it, the herbe beinge parched
then strayne it & keepe it for yor vse. it will delay all heate about a mans body & pro-
cure sleepe: in like manor the oyle of marche mallowes is made:

To make a man or woman make water stronge from them: 4

Take of gromell seede halfe an vnce, of seene cleane pickt from the stalkes a quarter of an vnce
of ginger scraped & sliced thine, of sinamon scraped & brused, of eche of thos one drame of damask
prunes seven, the stones taken out, of white wyne a pinte, putt all the stuffe in a pott wth
the pinte of white wyne, cover it close & stoppe it wth past. sett that in an other pott
wth Whott lycour, that the one stande in the other for the space of a longe hower, that the
pott may be hott, & then take it forthe, sett it bye till it be coulde then strayne
it, & after put in sugar as you like:.

+ A powder to make teeth cleane & wthout ache: 5.

Take of drye sage, of allame, of pepper, bay salt, of eche of thos like weight, thes make
in powder, & preserve them in a boxe, & take a sage leafe wth powder, & rubbe yor gumes
wth it when you will:.-

for fayntnes at the harte: 6.

Take the flowers of sentorye, a good handfull, cleane picked from the greene, of seene leaves cleane
picked from the stalkes a drame & a halfe, of puer sinamon a quarter of an vnce, well brused
of white wyne a pinte, thes put all together in a pott & suger close as you list close covered,
& sett in a pott wth whott lycor for the space of one hower, then lett it stande all nighte
& in the morninge strayne it inot a fayer pott & putt good store of sugar, lett it simper &
simper till it come to a syroppe, & of this you may take two or three sponefulls at a
tyme in the morninge fastinge.

To make suger of roses very good for the bluddy
fluxe or laske: 7.

Take of Redd Rose buddes not all blowen a pownde the white ends cutt off, & haue redye of puer
springe water three pintes, of puer white suger three powndes, halfe the water sett to the fier
putt in yor halfe suger, sett it on a softe fier alwayes scume it as longe as scume will rise,
& then putt in yor Roses little by little alwayes sturringe it so lett it boyle till the Roses
be as tender as may be, & then take vpp yor Roses wth a little scumer out of the lycour
& lay them in a fayer dyshe by the fier side & then putt the rest of yor suger into the
lycor

licour & lett it boyle & melte, & scome it cleane & then putt in the rest of yor Roses lett it boyle
& nowe & then sturringe it wth a silver spone, so let it boyle till the lycour begine to waxe
stiffe & clamey, then sett it bye & cover it, wth some dolbe lynen clothe, nowe & then sturringe
it & when it is could have redye some galley pott, & putt it in, cast therin afterwards some

to yor vse.

To streynthen a weake backe. 1.

Take the pithe of a youge oxe or of a hefer as much pithe of the backe as a good handfull,
slyce it, & take out the stuffe that is in it, putt it in a fayer dishe, put thervunto puer dates
(the skyne taken off wh in side) minced as fine as may be, one, two, or three, then boyle them
in two or three sponefulls of Rose water, then looke what quantitie of dates, the like of Raysons
of the sonne & Redd currant, yor Reysons must be minced smale & the stones taken out, then
boyle yor currant & Reysons & Rosewater all together till they be tender, & putt in some cromes
of breade, synamon & suger, & safron smale beaten into powder, temper all thes together in a
fayer dishe, & haue redy pasti^lion?, wch is made of suger & yolkes of egges fine flower & butter
this worke alltogether into past, & rowle it as thine as you can, & make it into peaces, the fashion
of a peasecodd, & bake them slenderly, & reserve them to yor use, & when you doe eate of them, take
one of them & heate it by ?the fier side, fastinge one at morninge an other at noone.

A playster to mitigate the payne of piles & emerodes: 2.

Take the pappe of an apple putt therto the yelke of a new layd egge, worke them together, then
putt in nyne chives of saffron smale groue, of lyne seede xxtie cornes fine groude, thes boyle
allthogether wth imbers sturringe it, this make playsters on & laye it to yor griefe.

A drinke to mitigate all heate of the lyver & spleyne.

Take of liverworte, of mayden heare, of Endiffe of the flowers of winter gilloflowers cleane
picked from the stalke, of great comferey, of the herbe terrogant of eche of thos fower vnces
of springe water a pottell, of licoras cleane scraped & thine slyced one vnce thes boyle all
together now & then sturringe it, & then putt in fine white suger candey one vnce & ahalf fine
beaten into powder, & then lett it boyle it half away alwayes sturringe it, & put it in some
close glasse, & reserve it to yor vse, this you must drinke euy morninge a drafte & fast two
howers after it.

To discharge an impostume slow & all: .3.

Take powder of seene alexandria one vnce, of ginger, of sinamon, of mace, of colliander seedes, Anyseeds
of licoras, of eche the weight of eight pence, of suger two vnces, of spurge seedes ten l twelve
blauched, beate them all smale into powder, & put them into a quarte of clarett wyne, & lett
it be brued out of one pott into an other often tymes in the daye, by the space of three dayes
before you shall occupie it, & then lett it ronne out of a gelly bagge, & then take half a pinte,
of the same drinke, & when you shall have neede to occupie it you muste warme it at the fier
& drinke of it after yor firste sleepe, & so lye still till it offer to come, & then goe to a closestoole,
till it be all come away, & then make a playster of redd mintes, redd fennell, of sage, of wormewood,
of every one a handfull, & putt therto a good handfull of comin seedes, & beate them all smale
together, & putt therto a good sawcer full of vineger, & set them all on the fier in a panne, & so
sturre them till they be almost drye, & take them & putt them in a bagge, & lay this bagge to yor
stomake so that yor stomake be not full of meate.

A medicen to make a womans disease come to her right Course: 4

Take of youge sothernwoode, of byttany, of canapitis, of sentory, of the rootes of sentory salendine, of
Saint Iohns woorte wth the flower, of eche of thos herbes a brauche as bigg as yor thumbe, but
one roote of salendine, of white wine a pinte & a half, of reysons of chesonne the stones taken
out half a handfull cutt in peaces, thes boyle till the third parte be wasted, then strayne
it then putt two or three & fortie blades of  saffron s made in powder, of graines the weight
of two pence beaten, boyle it agayne, & of this drinke a draught in the morninge fastinge
wth as muche treakell as a hasell nutte, & at night a draught wthout trekell.

5 5
A very sofferant medicen for one that hath taken coulde
att the harte:

Take oyle of bitter Almons, & waxe, capons greace, & rose water, boyle all them together then take black
woole newly plucked of the sheepes necke, wett it in the licour, & put it in a quilted bagge, &
lay it very hott to the stomacke to bedwarde:

A good medicen to rien a pluresy: .2.

Take a faier Costard cutt of the croune, picke out the coare, make no hole throughe, & then put therto-
=in thes powders, of a beoares toothe the weight of fower pence, of the powder of Ruberbe fine
grated as may be, the weight of three pence, of sinamon the weight of a penny, of the powder of
white suger candy the weight of two pence, thes stopp into an the apple, & cover it agayne, & lay it
on a tyle wth imbers, & turne it often till it be as softe as may be, then cutt it in sounder, &
give the sicke partie in the morninge half of it to eate, all the other half take the next day
so that boothe the dayes be good to take medicen on, you must faste two howers after it, then have
some good broathes to eate.

=+The order of makinge the greene oynetment: 3.

Take of sage, of rue, of eche of them a powde, of wormewood, of bay leaves, of eche of them halfe a
pownde, of mellilott herbe or flower, of camamell, of the flowers of spike, of Rosemary, of redd
Rosewater leaves, of Saint Iohns worte, of dill, of eche of thos one good handfull, of marshe
mallowes two handfulls, all thes herbes chopp as smale as maye be, & stampe them as smale as
may be, & wey them what they wey, putt therto the like weigh of puer shippe shuett, chopp it
as fine as may be, mince yor herbes & it together, & stampe it in a stone morter to one subst-
ance that there be s no shuett seene, but all greene: put it in some fayer pott or panne,  put therto
sweete oyle olive as sweete as may be a pottell & a pinte, thes worke all together in a panne wth
yor handes to one substance & cover it close wth some claye or past aboute the edges that no
ayer come in nor out, lett it stande for seven dayes, the seven dayes beinge ended, undoe it & take
it foorthe & put it in a panne, sett it on a softe sweete fier, alwayes sturringe it till the  leafe
beginne to waxe parched & then strayne it into some fayer panne, then have redy thes oyles follo-
winge, oyle of Roses, of camamell, of white lillies, of spike, of violetts, of eche of these one vnce,
sturringe them together, & reserve them in a glasse for yor vse.
yf you will have it more pleasant of sent & more nurrishinge add therto the gumes of labdanu one
vnce & a halfe brused fine to powder, & of the gume called osterex cremitte thre quarters of an
vnce brused fine to powder, this mingle wth yor other stuffe & strayne it, & so reseve it to yor vse:

The Infirmities that this oyntment is good for: .4.

for stiches, for bruses, for the palsey, for the shrinkinge of sinewes, for gowtes & sciaticas, for the
ache of the backe, for lamnes & pluriseys, for the couffe the soles of the feete beinge anoynted, for
extreme payne in the head by could, make a cappe for the croune wth lynen clothe, & lay in it
the woulle of a quicke sheepe pluckt from the flankes & coddes, force of the filthines & picke out
all the moates & carde it, then scime wth this oyntment aforesayd, & so bast it, & lay it somewhat
warme to yor head, it is good also for the collicke, & for the spleine, & for the could dropsey of the liver

A playster for soare eyes: 5.

Take of the pappe of a Rosted apple two sponefulls of the yelk of a new layd egge the like qua-
ntitie of saffron thirteene chives dried & fine made into powder, thes worke you together to one
substance, adioyne therto of a womans milke of a woman child a sponefull, of Rose water the like
quantitie, of thes make your playster & lay to your eyes eveninge & morninge:
Have in redines the powders of thes stones followinge of lapisse callaminans squenced nine tymes
in whiye wyne, of alloes hepatica, of white suger candey, of tucia prepared of camphire, of
eche of thos half a drame fine made into powder, thes sowe in a fine cloute or peace of silke, &
lett them fall into yor glass of water, & of this water dropp into yor eyes morninge, noone  & at
night, & lye vpp when you doe dropp into yor eye: yor water as followeth, of Roses, of
marigouls, of plantine, of browne fenell, of wyw.bright, of sallendine, of turmentill, of
bittany, of scabious, of femitory, of oken buddes, of eche of thos two vnces, thes mixt
all together in a glasse, & putt in the powders aforesayd:

for the green sickness:

Take fastinge euy morning of one cowes milke as near as you can a pinte newly milked from the
cowe through a lynen clothe, wth a fewe mintes brused in it. & lett the partie sturr after it./

Mr farrowes salve good for all manor of aches in bones or flesh
=+ & helpinge of all soares, & good to anoynte the stomacke for agues
& also the belly & backe for the collicke & stone:.

ffirst take a pounde of Englishe waxe, & two pounds of rosen, half a pound of frankensence, & thes
boyle in a panne, & then seathe them till they be moulten, then take a handfull of mouseare, one handfull
of smallege, on handfull of sheapards purse, one handfull of mellilott, one handfull of yeaw., one
handfull of plantine, one handfull of marigould leaves, howselike the greene leaves of seven or eyght
heades therof, stampe thes leaves together smale, & streyne them in a cleane clothe, putt
therto a poud
of may butter, & wth the Iuce of all thes herbes mixte wth the may butter, sett them on the fier &
boyle them well together, & then take yor first medicen agayne, that is to say yor waxe, Rosen, & fra-
nkinsence, & relent them on the fier sturringe them wth a sticke, & when they be well relented take
them off the fier, & putt in ^all the Iuce of all the herbes & may butter & seethe them well together, a
good while, this donne, lett them coole in the same panne, yf you will you may putt it into pottes
or make rowles of it: proved many tyme.

for the Rume distillinge into the eyes or lunges:

?? Take of Rosemary, redd sage, of sweate margeram of eche a handfull, of bittony half a handfull, seethe
them in a quart of balme water vntill a pinte, straine it & make vpp the decoction wth suger, & for
the lunges you must put in some Isopp, & a fewe anyseedes & licoras before you boyle it:.

A medicin for the stone:

Take as much wild tyme as will yeld a pinte of water, as much saxifrage as will yeld an other pinte
as muche sowe thistle as will make an other pinte as much of brome blossome water, as much of
popular buddes leaves & all, asmuch of phillipendula water, asmuch of pellitory of the wale water,
asmuch of Radish water, asmuch of stervet water, asmuch of parseley root & all in water, polipodie of the
oke Cli, of mayden heare asmuch, of kneehove roots asmuch, of  holly hock asmuch, & you must gather asmuch
may dewe as will make a quarte of stilled water, & sprinkell that amonge h the herbes & rootes
as ye still them alittle at once, then lett it stand nyne dayes in the sonne, then still it altogether wth
a pinte of muskadine, & keape it for yor vse & drinke euy morninge adraught vntill you see the disease
to breake./

A good medicen for the plauge:

It taketh them like the ague wth coulde, as soone as they complayne, take five, seven, or nine seedes of
garden spurge, (nyne is wth the mast) stampe them smale in a dish, put to it warme milke or a poset Ale, & lett
the sicke body drinke it, & assoone as you can gett him to bedd, cast him into a sweate, then take agreate Inion
& take out the coare, & put into it methridatu, & lay one the capp agaiyne, rost it till it be very softe, then
putt it in adish & beat it very small, putt to it three sponefulls of white vinegar, & asmuch of suger as
will make it pleasant to take, assoone as his stomake is purged, give him the onyon asmuch as you
can for it will drive out the soare in fower & twentie howers by the grace of god, plye him wth
hott broathes & drinke, kepe him in asweat twentie fower howers, & keepe him very hott after still,
when the soare is come out; you may rost an onyon wth treakell, but no vineger & lay it to the soare
to draw it out & you must have asugrgion to lauce it. probatu:
for yor howshold give them euy day metridatu & treacle, take sentory & madder & boyle it in beare
& lett them drinke now & then of it:

for a bruse a proved medicen:

Take a handfull of henbane, asmuche violett leaves, asmuch mallowe leaves, shredd them smale, seathe them
in a pinte of milke till they be softe, put to it otemeale & a good quantitie of boores greace, & lett them
boyle till they be thicke, & so playster lay it warme on the hurte.

for a scaldinge or burninge:

Take toune houe, & the Inner pille of elme, shred them smale put to it boores greace, the reddest
hennes dounge you can gett, & some waxe, boyle all together a quarter of an hower or more, putt
in a little howsake & so dresse the soare./

7.
An oyntment for a bruse or ache:.

Take of dill, of verbine, mogeworte, henbane, camamell, the toppes of lavender of euery one a like
quantitie, then take may butter & shredd the herbes smale put them in the may butter, well stamped in
the butter, lett them stand a fortnight, then frye it well & straine it./

The makinge of aqua mirabilis & pretiosa wch is of
singuler vertue:/.

Take gallingale, cloves, quibibles, ginger, mellilott, cur^dmoney, mace nutmegges, of euy of them a
drame, beate them to powder, then take of the Iuce of sallendine half a pinte, then put all the
powders into it, & sturr them together well, wth a pinte of aquavitie, & thre pintes of good white wyne,
then put all thos thinges together into a stillatory of glasse, & lett them stand so all night, & on the
morrowe still them wth an easy fier, & when it is stilled put it into a glasse stopped very close, for
this water is of singuler vertue:
This water will preserve the lunges that is wouded or persed, it will help them wthout any greefe, &
comforteth them, & it suffereth the blud not to perish, that he shall not need almost to be lett bludd,
& it keepeth the hart from beinge burnte, it will avoyd mellancoly, fleame, runie, it is good for the
stomake, it is good for youthe to engender a good collour, it keepeth the visage & memory of man,
also it is good for the palsey, this water will revive ones spirit that is on death beadd, it
must be dronke one sponefull a weake, & in the winter tyme two sponefulls aweake, except need require for this water is the best.

The ingrediens to doctor steevens Water:

Take agallon of good gasconie wyne, then take ginger, gallingale, sinamon, nutmegges, graynes, cloves
maces, anyseedes, fenell seedes, carroway seedes, od eche of thos a drame, then take redd mint, redd
Rose leaves, tyme of the garden, pellitory of the wale, smale margeru, rosemary, penyriall, sage, wild
tyme, camamell, lavender, avens, of euery of them a handfull, then bruse them all in a morter, & beate
yor spices smale, & then put yor spices & herbes into the wyne & let it stand twelve howers, sturinge
it often tymes, then still it in a limbecke, the first pinte is the best, the second ^is not so good. /
The vertue of this water is it comforteth the spirits & procureth greatly the youthe of man, it
helpeth the inward diseases cominge of could, it helpeth the shakinge of the palsey, it comfortethe
the contraction of sinowes, & helpeth the toth ache, it comforteth the stomacke very much, it cureth
the Reynes of the backe, it cureth the canker, it cureth the could dropsey, it helpeth forthe the
stone in the bladder, it helpeth a stinkinge breathe, it helpeth the conception of women that be barren:
A sponefull of this fastinge to some is inough, to other two or thre in ten dayes sufficeth:

A speciall good tissen for a cough or could:

Take a pottell of stale ale & set it on the fier, & scume it cleane, then put to it a handfull of Isopp, a peny
worthe of licoras, & a peny worthe of Anyseedes, & bruse yor anyseedes & licoras together, then put
in a quarter of a pound of white suger candy, & let it seath from a pottell to a quarte, & so let
the patient drinke of it somewhat hott first & last:.

A speciall good medicen for a shakinge or bourninge ague
especially in the springe tyme.

Take thre burre leaves as broad as yor hand apeace, nyne leaves of yerrowe otherwise called mille foliu
nyne croppes of elder, euy croppe fouer branches, take of eche of thes in quantitie a childs handfull
stampe them & strayne them wth adraughte of good Ale or beere beinge warme, & so drinke therof
a quarter of an hower before yor fitt come, & then walke & Rune vntill you be hott, & then goe to bedd
& cast on clothes inoughe, but yf you can not walke nor Rune goe to bed presently:.

Goodwife lushers salue for a Catt: -

Take Englishe honey, English waxe, Rosen & deeres shewett, or els sheep shuet, of eche of them a like
quantitye, boyle them all together softly on the fier, & then take it off, & coole it, & make it vpp
in Roules & stricke it thine on playsters as you neede:

for the Comon plauge:

Take a bricke or tyle & heat it in the fier Redd hott, then take redd vineger of the strongest in
operation, & power vppon it, & then take the smell & odour therof wth yor head hanginge doune-
ward ouer it, this doe euy morninge & by the power of god it shall doe you good:.

A medicen to remedy the tothe ache for eu & ever  .2.

Take pellitory half an vnce, two drames of stane facie  a henbane roote, half an vnce of comfrey, thre drames
of allam, a few woodbine leaves, a pinte of vineger, a pinte of Runinge water, a sponefull of honey. seethe
thes together, till the one half be consumed, & then wash yor teeth bludd warme, when it is made could
put it into a glasse, wth some parte of euy thinge, & so preserve it at your pleasure; remember to slitt yor
henbane roote & take out the pithe, for it is nought, & scrape & washe the other deale cleane for this
medicen, & when you haue payne washe yor teethe euy day once, & after your payne is gone, then euy
weeke, & then euy month once, & thus vse it & it will help yor teethe, & also preserve them as they
be  probatu

& it will helpe yu  & haue it out or els nothinge

A medicen for the clarifiinge of a woude

ffirst take a sponefull of sallett oyle, & the quantity of a nvtt of turpentine, boyle them together, dipp
linte into it, & stopp the woude full, when the woude is clarified, droppe the oyle of eggs into
it with a fether, & fill it vpp wth linte

for prowd fleshe or dead flesh in a woude 12

Take Roche allam & beate it into fine powder, & put some into the wounde, & it will consume the dead
fleshe./

A quicke playster for an ould soare 13

Take two sponefulls of sallett oyle, an vnce of waxe, as muche as a walenutt of Rosen, half as much
turpentine, as much diaculu,twise as much sheepes shuett, the quantitie of a hasell nutt of bolarmonike
as much blacke soope, boyle all thes together & power them in a bason of water, & make it in Roules
& sticke very thine in playsters

A gentill plaster for the same 14

Take virgens waxe, rosen, sheepes shuett, English honey, of euery eche one like quantitie, boyle
all thes together & make them in Rowles as aforesayd, & sticke them in playsters

to make a Powltesse 2.

Take Rose leaves & seath them in Ale, till they be tender, then putt in Ryes meale so much as may lye vppon the soare as thicke as ons hande, & seathe them together, & lay it to the soare as hott as
you can suffer it

Mrs Clyffes medicen for the palsey 3.

Take the leaves & Root of primeroses, & cowslypps cleane pickt, drest & washt, & a little brused of bittany
of eche of thos a good handfull, of sage thre good handfulls cleane pickt & drest, of graynes a sponefull
brused, of Anyseedes asmuch brused, & one nvttmegge made into powder, then make paste wth barly
flower knodden with water & honey sodd together, with that mak you a pye a spanne broade, & five fingers
deape, then putt in all your herbes  & stuffe, & close them vp, & bake it wth cakes or half peny breadd
that it may not be overmuche baked, then haue redye a vessell of thre or fower gallons of Ale
or beare of fower dayes ould, then breake the pye, & put it therin hott, then close it & lett it stande
two nightes, & two dayes, then drawe of it, & drinke therof first & last morninge & eveninge, & at
other tymes, & this vse till all be spent, & vse to drinke no other drinke in the meane while
but only that

A medicen for the stone 4.

Take the pricked holly & dry it, then beate it to powder, & se^arce it throughe a searse, take white
wyne & butter, warme it as hott as you can suffer to drinke it, & putt in the powder of the holly
then take two handfull of chicke weede & heate it as hott as you can suffer it vppon a
hott tyle stone & lay it to the navell

A medicen for the strenigthning of the mother 5

Take one vnce of pepper, one vnce of fenell seede, one vnce of Elicampane Root, & beate them all
severall very fine, & asmuch English honey well clarified as you thinke will serve, boyle them
a little altogether, & put it in a galley pott, & eate therof morninge & eveninge, & faste
two howers after

for the Rysinge of the mother 6.

Take good malmesey, & a fewe cloves heate in a pott, & drinke therof, take redd mint & heate it
betwene two tyles, & when it is hott, power power some of the malmesey on the mint, as it is
on the tyles, & Rowle it on a Rowle, & lay it alonge on the grease, & keepe the party warme, &
it shall help; by gods grace

for the fallinge downe of the mother 7.

Take a quarte of good ould wyne, a handfull of the toppes of younge brome, putt it in the wyne
boyle it from a quarte to a pinte, put into it some suger, & drinke it off, & it will by the
grace of god helpe the patient

A medicen for a mangy horse or dogge 8

Take of Allame, greene copras, & brimstone; of eche a like quantitie, minge them wth a little creame
or freshe greace, then let yor horse be very well curried, & therwth anoynt the soare place, &
Rubb it in very well & lett him stand so tyed to the Rackes halfe the daye

A medicen for the Iche 9.

Take a good quantitie of planten & shredd it very fine, & boyle it well in fresh nutter, & strayne
it for yor vse

An other for the same 10

Take the greace of a younge hogge, & a quantitie of brimstone & worke them well together with a
knyf vppon a trencher till it be throughly wrought, & then put therto alittle aquavitye, &so mixe
them all together at yor pleasure

A medicen for the stone 11.

Take the shells of new layd egges, & washe them cleane in the out side, & beate them in very fine
powder, grate a nvtmegge fine & putt therto, & drinke therof a little in the morninge, & if need be
in the eveninge also, asmuch of the egge as the nvtmegge the drinke & all need be no more then
a sponefull or twayne in Renish or white wyne

To make a siroppe for one that is shorte winded: 1.

Take a good handfull of Isope, a handfull of harehownd, seathe them in a quarte of Runinge water to
a pinte strayne it through a fayer clothe, & put in suger to make it pleasante, vse this Evenige
& morninge wth a licoras sticke thre sponefulls at atyme:

A Sufferant medicen for them that are trobled wth wynde: 2.

Take a quarte of malmesey or white wyne, one vnces of Anyseedes, one vnce of fenell seedes one vnce of
Carroway seedes, an vnce of comyn seeds, boyle all thes from a quarte to a pinte, strayne them
If you thinke good you may put in suger, take of this in the morninge thre sponefulls,
& two howers after.
when you are disposed to make alight supper, toste a toste till it be yellow, & not burnte, & stricke
it wth suger on bothe the sides, as longe as it will soake in, then put it into a fayer porrenger
& put to five or six sponefulls of puer malmesey, eate vp the toste & drinke vpp the malmesey,
& you shall finde it to doe you greate good.

A playster for an ache: .3.

Take of smallege & stampe it & putt to it aquavity & strayne it, then put to it boores greace, sturre
them well together & anoynt the patient therwth before the fier Eveninge & morninge.

for the payne in the Eare: 4.

Take two handfulls of sage, one handfull of Isope, half ahandfull of Rosemary, put thes in a little pilowe
boyled in Rose vineger, aquavity, & Rosewater & lay this to yor Eare as hott as you can sufffer it.

A drinke to make on solible: 5.

Take thre fenell Rootes, thre parsely Rootes, & take out the pithe of them, then take thre sorell leaves
boyle them well in whey & drinke therof morninge & Eveninge.

To breake the stone

A medicen for an ache: 6.

Take greate garden wormes & slitt them & scrape away the filth that is wthin them, choppe them
small, & frye them in good sallett oyle, strayne them & anoynte yor grief wthall.

A medicen for the mother & the green sicknes: 7.

Take great garden wormes slitt them & washe out the filthines & then lay them in white wyne
half an hower, then take them out & lay them in an oven to drye & when they are through drye
beate them into fine powder & drinke it with white wyne in the morninge fasting, & faste two
howers after.

A medicen to cleanse the backe & purge the Raynes: 8.

Take one fenell Roote & two parsly rootes, & picke out the pithes of them & put therto one handfull of
pelletory of the walle, washe them all cleane & seathe them in possett Ale, & drinke therof when you
goe to bedd & yf you wake at midnight, drinke of it also.

A medicen for the payne in the backe & heate
in the backe. 9.

Take Rosewater & put therto sauders & Roseleaves, & lay them in steepe in yor Rosewater one whole
night, & washe yor backe wth the same, & it will take awaye the heate, & greatly comforte
the Raynes.

A good medicen for the goute: & to put it awaye. 10

Take of waxe two vnces, as much of oyle olyve, & one vnce of ceruce, put to thes two sponefulls of
good vineger, & boyle them together & wth a very softe fier let it seethe, till it tourne collour
somewhat blacke, then take it from the fier, & when it is somewhat cooled, dippe a
linen clothe in it & vse it in the manor of aseare clothe, havinge care that the clothe be
well oyled therwth all places alike so make it p vpp & keepe it to yor vse, & it will
be good fower or five yeares.
