A medicen for the stone proved

Take greene samphire as it growethe, & make powder of it, let him that is trobled wth the same drinke every weeke
of it, whether he feele payne or no; & in proces of tyme, it shall ridd him of the stone, the powder is best to be
dronke with white wyne, this is a very good medicen & proved.

An other proved for the stone 2

Take malmesey & bourne it, & put to it a good dele of alexander seede beaten, & drinke it as hott as you can./

A playster for a weake stomacke 3.

Take ahadfull of mellilott, ahandfull of mintes, a quarter of ahandfull of wormewoode, afewe rose leaves, asponefull
of comyn, & as muche of Anyseed, mingle them all & put them together in abagge, quilte it, & put it on
adishe vppon achaffinge dishe of coles, & sprinkell it wth malmesey, & lay it to the patient stomacke./

for the frensey & payne in the head be it never so sore 4.

Take bittany, camamell, rosemary, bruse it alittle, then lay it betweene two tyles sett, sprinkell it wth alitle
vineger, & lay it hott to the nape of the necke, at nighte a new playster  in the morninge doe ??? this
till the payne be gone this medicen is well proved./

A playster specially good for thes diseases followinge./ 5.

That is to saye spatius, the crampe ortetica, coldnes of sinowes & veynes; sciatica, the ache of the hockell bone
or the Reynes, the palsey, the shakinge palsey in the armes & handes, podagrie, the hantes in the feete, coulde
gout in the armes, handes & shoulders; for debilitie & wekenes in the handes or legges, for deadnes or
mortifiinge of the exterior partes or members, yea for all kinde of diseases if it come of could causes
& this is the makinge therof.

firste you shall take aplayster called oxicranu, aplayster called ceroniu, of eche fower vnces, aplayster
called the spice playster, mellilot playster, of eche twelve vnces, oyle of dill, of camamell, of cowslope,
of eche one vnce, oyle of peter oliu half an vnce, the powder of camamell flowers & mellilot flowers, of
eche an vnce & ahalf, melt your playster firste on asofte fier, & after put in yor oyles, & last yor powders, alwayes
sturringe it wth aslice of woode, & then cast this playster on amarble stone, anoynte the stone with oyle of  ???
dill, rowle it vppon red lether, & when you doe occupied it, you must draw it vppon scarlett

This is the makinge of oxicrariu 6
Take waxe collofine, pitche, saffron, of eche fower vnces, masticke an vnce & ahalfe, perrosen, meere, gume ar-
maniake, galbanu, turpentine, of eche two vnces, lay yor gumes in vineger two dayes, that is galbanu
& gume armaniake, firste you shall melt yor waxe & perrosen, & ^then take it from the fier, & put in your gumes
& after the powder colofine, masticke, & mere, then the powder of saffron last of all, then cast it on a
marble stone & so keepe it./

To make the spice playster 7.

Take hartes shuet, waxe, frankensence, of eche twelve vnces, olibanu, sheepes tallowe, of eche fower vnces,
a quarte of redd wyne, boyle thes vppon the fier, & after strayne it, & then set it on the fier agayne,
puttinge to it cloves two vnces, maces, saffron, of eche half an vnce, when the wyne is wasted, make
it vpp in rowles./

A medicen for the Iaudes & morphew proved 8.

Take docke root the yellowestyou can gett, let them be fayer washed & scraped, & the pithe taken out, then
slice them in thinne slices, & put to it white wyne or renishe wyne, in acuppe close covered, & put to
it six croppes of speare mintes, & let it stand so aday & a nighte ere you drinke it, & it must be
dronke in the morninge two howers ere you drinke yor brothe./

+ The great vertue that is in the hedgehogge 9

A hedghogge rosten & eaten of thos that be diseased of the dropsy healeth them in shorte tyme lickwyse
dried in an oven twise or thrice after the takinge foorthe of the breade, the skinne & the mawe wth
the intralls cast away, the body beinge dried within alinen clothe ^& not washed, & after beaten to powder,
& taken in wyne, or brothe the quantity of asponefull, healethe the dropsy, & the fluxe of seede in all
men, the liver & kidnes dried after rhat sorte, prevayleth as muche agaynst the sayd diseases

+ The vertue of the hedghogges  skinne that
pricketh .10

The skinne dried in an oven & beaten to powder, mingled with alittle sharpe vineger, takethe
away the morphewe or other diseases, or deformities in the skinne, yf the deformitie be much
in the skyne let it be rubbed twise or thrice aday, & it will take it away in shorte tyme, by
god good helpe.






A very good medicen for sore eyes that ronnethe'
of the migroine: proved: .1.

Take the whytw of an egg, wate it well, then scome it & put to the oyle of cast of ginger, finely beaten &
white sugar, then take flex & dippe it in & put of it on every temple, & take heed that no parte
of it come into yor Eyes, & doe this every night when you goe to bed, fower or five night or more, & every
night washe yor eyes with waster that cometh of he cuttinge of vines: provatu./

A souerayne medicen for aches & bruses 2.

Take of sage, of rue, of eche apounde, of wormewood, of bay leaves of eche half apounde, cut them smale, & after
beate them in a morter, then take thre pound of sweete sheepes shuett, taken from the sheepe could, mince it
smale, & so put it into your herbes inthe morter, then beate them together vntill the shuet be not seene, but the
herbes all in one collour, then take it out of the morter, & put it into a bason, & put therto a pottell of
oyle plyve, worke it wth your handes into the herbes, till it be all of like sweetenes, then put it into an earthen
pott & cover it close, & so keepe it eight dayes, then put it into abrasse pott, & seethe it wth asofte fier, vnto
the tyme the streinth of the herbes bge begonne & thus shall you prove, take asponefull of it, & put it into
a newe canvas clothe & wringe the Iuce from the herbes, yf ther come any licour like abroune water, then
is it not sodden inough, for if it be sodden Inough ther will nothinge come with wringinge but
oyntement: the la of oxford./

howe to make the blacke playster: vsed by Sr philip paris 3.

Take apownde of the finest droppes of olibanu or more so that it be apownd when it is beaten & sersed, apound
of good cleane rosen, when it is beaten & searsed, apound of pitche, then muste you take half a pound of sheeps
tallowe tryed fine, fower vnces of comin, two vnces of labden, tooe vnces of cloves & mave, half an vnce of safron,
then must you take afayer brasse panne; & sett it over the fier on a trevett, & vnder make afier f of coles,
so that it flamethe not, & first you must put in your rosen & let it moulte fayer & easily, & sturr it not
till it be moulten, then take heede it sogethe not, & wth aspone litle by litle, put in your olibanum &
ever let it molt before you put in any more, & when yor rosen & olibanu are cleane moulten then put
in yor sheepes tallowe ^& then yor labden both cut in smale peeces, but yf you will you may beat your labden & sheeps tallow together
wth ahott pestell, put them together in acleane panne, & when it is moulten, take yor comyn, cloves, mace
& safron finely beaten, mingle them together in aplatter, then take the panne wth moulten stuffe from the
fier, & wth aspone put in yor spices & strue it vppon yor other stuffes, & take good heede it rune not over into
into the fier for it will rise exceedingly, & seethe from the fier when yor spices be in, then take asticke,
& sturr it together, & after taken abrason ladell & poure of the sayd stuffe on the fleshie side of a thinne
sheepe or lammes skinne, streched out on a table, & spread it abroad with awarme rowler, yf yee list to
keepe it in arowle, yee may poure it on a table cleane scraped, washed, & anoynted wth sallett oyle, or sheeps
tallowe, & when it begineth to coole, assone as you can handle it, before it be harde, gather vpp the sides
& roule it vpp together rownde & when you will vse it, heate therof vppon al chaffinge dish of coles,
& spread it vppon lether as aforesayde,
Itt is to be vsed in this maner, whersoever the payne is, a payer of sooles of this playster layd to the sooles of
the feete, keepe it to as longe as you can & when it is moyst take them offe, wype them & put them to,
agayne, & yf your feete be to hott, you may forbeare anight or more, as you see cause, & then put them
to agayne:

for a canker in the mouthe 4.

Take a pottell of fayer water, put to it roche allam the bignes of an egge, sett the water on the fier, till the
allam be moulted, then let it coole agayne, take thre handfulls of sage, six handfull of woodbine leaves,
stampe together smale, & when the water is could, strayne the herbes therwth, put to it asponefull or two of
honey, set it into the sonne in aglasse, occupie it not while it is newe, for the older it is the bett.

for bruses and bone aches 5.

Take daysies, camfrey, broocklime, pimpernell, of eche ahandfull, stampe them together, & lett them stande two or
thre dayes close in apott, then boyle them in askillet with apinte of may butter, & asaucer full of sallett oyle till
it be all greene, strayne it & vse it./

for aches a medicen 6.

The fatt of an ould bucke cony about the kidnes, s the slyme or skinne about the kidnes taken away, mynce the
fatt, & putt it into adishe of hotte water, & melt it, then gather it & keepe it fayer, it is good to anoynte
limes that akke, rubb them well therwth, & keepe them warme;/ also the oyle of neet feete is good for aches,
& to soople asore, & it is to be gathered thus, washe the neat feet well, & put them into a pott of fayer water,
seathe them till they be tend, & even as they seeth skyme of the fatt, & put it into acleane dishe, & when
it is could, take of the fatt wth afether, & keepe it for your vse./

A medicen for wormes:1.

Chafe well the navell of the childe with oyle of worme woode before the fier, when ye???????????

for all bourninge agues: 2.

Take ahandfull of rosemary, & alesse quantitye of sage, boyle them in a quantitye of stale ale, & alittle anyseed brused,
then strayne it & boyle it agayne, put alittle suger to it, & give it the patient to drinke as hott as may be suffered
it will imediately cause hime to sleepe, it may be dronke in could or bourninge./ it is a sufferant medicen./

for a quicke thinge crept into the eare 3.

Take the Iuce of sentory, or the greace of an eele, the Iuce of rue, & put it into the eare, also put the Iuce of wilde
tansey into the eare:/

for the pyles: 4

Take asofte leafe of millefolyu & sheepes tallowe, beate them togetherin a morter smale & lay it to the place./

for a wenne./ 5.
stampe occulus christe & with the Iuce therof anoynte the wenne five or six tymes & it will waste away.

To drawe out a thorne: 6.

Take agreat blacke worme that is comonly creapinge in horsedounge or other lyke hye wayes ar not wthout, slytt it on
the belly, but kill it not, & lay it to the place wher the thorne is, it wilbe awhile paynefull, byut if any thinge
drawe out the thorne this will:/ Also Rosen moulten at acandell & so dropt on the place hath drawen out a
thorne./ & yf asinewe be pricked wth aknife nedell or thorne, take oyle of roses heate it well & lay it to
it will ease the payne.

A souerayne poultess for any swellinge/ 7.

Take agood deale of chicke weed, seath it well in Runninge water &  then drawe it from the water, & lay it to twise a day

for the mother./ 8

Pare athreshoud, wch paringe take & sprinkle well with malmesey, warme it very hott in afryinge panne, & put it in
a linen clothe, & lay it hott as neere the payne as may be endured for heate, & so as it is coolinge, lay it neere
the payne./

+ To cause one to vomite proved. 9.

Take the middle peele of the elder wch is the greenest, boyle it in milke, (havinge it to be cutt vpward, & drinke it
fastinge./

A medicen for the crampe: 10.

Take good oyle olyve, & put to it fine powder of pepper beaten, at night when you goe to bedd chafe well the place agaynst
the fier, wher the crampe is, & if it be in the legges put on a peace of alinen hose./

To make a seare clothe for a legge that swelleth & rankeleth:/ 11.

Take oyle olyve, virgens waxe, & the powder of white leade, boyle all thes together, & while it is hott, dipp the clothe
in it, & when it is could it wilbe aseare clothe, then warme lay it to the place greeved.

A very good medicen for the goute proved./ 12.

Take the toppes of an elder tree, frye it wth shuet of arame, stayne it,  & keepe it in aboxe th till the patient shall
be anoynted therwth./

A proved medicen for bourninge, or for achild that hathe any parte
if his body bournte by fier./ 13.

Take hennes donge or capons donge, & ground Ivey, stampe them alltogether, then take sheepes tallowe, & fry the herbe
& the dounge wthall, strayne it, & wher the bourninge is, anoynte it two or thre tymes adaye & keepe this same
close in aboxe for yor vse./

A souerayne medicen for the stone proved./ 14.

Take vnsett leekes, & still them, & when the fier is out of it drinke of this water fastinge in the morninge, the leekes must
be stilled the greene & the white berds and all./

A playster for all manor of sores .15.

Take of rosen, of vnwrought waxe, of sheepes shuet, of eche apounde; beat the Rosen to powder, then shredd the waxe & the
Rosen shuet, & melt them with the rosen vppon the fier, till they begine to seethe, then take it of the fier & sturr it wth
a sticke, & then put therto two vnces of blacke soope, & stirr them till they be could, & then make it vpp in
rowles & keepe it to yor vse./

A medicen for a soare side: .16.

Take apinte of malmesey, & ahandfull of herbe grace, boyle them well together, & then put therto wheaten branne
stirre it till it be dry, & put it in a bagge, & lay it y=to the griefe.

+ A proved medicen for those that hath theyer face blacke & blewe with afalle
& for swellinge that bourneth. 17.

Take hemblocke & still it, & wth this water at every tyme warmed, bathe the blueplace wth aclothe wett in the
water, an hower together warme, doe this every day till it be whole, also this is very good for
swellinge that bourneth./

A souerayne medicen for a stiche: proved./ 18.

Take a quarte of stale Ale, & clarifie it on the fier with suger, then take the croppes of greene brome, if it will not be
had the seedes, beaten into powder & let it boyle in the ale, that the streintgh of the topppes of brome
may be in it, then sturr it, & let the sicke drinke it reasonable warme, & sweate vppon it, & it will take
away the greatest stiche can be, also drinke it last to bedd twise or thrise at nightes, after the stiche is
gone, lykewise in the morninge./





32 for the rume proved: .1.

Take the rootes of cowslippes & wth a quille blow it vpp into yor nostrells, & it will cause the rume to
come out of yor head.

A medicen for the collicke in the stomacke: 2.

Take aredd onyon & cut out the pithe, fill it full of butter, stopp it agayne wth the same that was cut off, rost
it in the fier & binde it to the navell hott./

for the could dropsey: .3.

Take of the Iuce of water cresses, of the Iuce of wolworte, of the Iuce of herehounde of ech a pounde, seeth thes to-
gether, put honey to them & make a siroppe. proved./
Vse water cresses & parsely in yor porrage for the same dropsey.

An easye purgation for a man or childe. 4.

Take aquantity of seene of Alexandria, & anyseedes, seeth them together in white wyne, or posset ale, after put
suger candy therin & drinke it.

To make awater for corrupte sores full of noysome
humors:: 5.

Take a gallon of smithes water, & thre good handfulls of sage, seeth it from agallon to apottell, then take the herbes
strayne them, & cast them away, then put into the licour thre peny worth of good white copresse, half a quart
of apounde of rooche allam, & thre sponefulls of honey, let all thes seeth aprety while, scome of the fome if
ther be any, put this water into glasses, & keepe it to washe sores twyse aday therwth

To heal a cutt: 6.

Take turpentine a penyworth, the yelke of an egge, & a quantity of oyle of roses or of oyle olyve, & so muche wheat
flower as will make it thicke to be aplayster, temper thes well together, & lay a playster therof to the cutt; if
the wounde growe to fast, heat alitle honey in asaucer, & spredd it over the playster, & it will mollifie
& heale, by god help.

for a tissin: 7.

Take apottell of water of fenell rootes, parsely rootes, & succory rootes scraped cleane, then washe them & take out
the pithes of them, take the quantity tye of ahandfull of them all, then take half ahandfull of reysons of the sonne
the stones taken out, six figges shred in the middes, of anyseeds & colliander seeds, apeny worth of eche, & a handfull
of frenche barlye washed & scalded well, apeny worth of licoras, & asmuche hole mace, seeth all thes from a pottell
to a quarte, & so keepe it in aclose pott, vntill you neede drinke it.

To make oyle of mace: 8.

Take an vnce of mace beaten fine, then put the powder therof into asaucer full of sallett oyle, sett it over the fier till
it be warme, well stirred together, then take it of the fier & it is done.

A medicen for the yellow Iaundis. 9.

Take ahandfull of redd nettells croppes, seethe them in a pynte of oyle, & drinke the same beinge strayned thre or
fower dayes together, & by god grace it helpeth.

Another for the same. 10.

Take aquarte of white wyne, agreat handfull of sallendine leaves, boyle them to apynte, but in winter take the root,
& drinke therof warme Eveninge & morninge, & it helpethe

A present remedy for the collicke. 121.

Take pellitory of the wall & in winter some of the root wthall, drie it on atyle by the fier, & beate it to powder
& put it to ale luke warme & drinke it thre tymes a day: this is proved to helpe.

A suer helpe for a greene wounde 12

Take millefoyle wch is much like yerrowe, bruse it well wth acleane hand or amorter, & put that in the wound or
cutt, & wthin five or six dressinges it will heale & close, & to drinke as much bugle stampte in a morter,
& tempered wth wyne, & then give it to the sicke to drinke.

To asswage the swellinge of alegg after an eague. 13.

Take vervine, dill, night chard, & sage boyle them all together, & bathe the legg wth it & binde the herbes hott to the
legg all night, vse it divers tymes & the swellinge will asswage, proved.

A water very comfortable for the eye sight. 14.

Take of rose leaves, of redd fenell or vervine, of rue, of sallendine, of eyebright, of eche ahandfull; still them
all together, & you shall have agood water for the eye sight. / proved.

To recover eye sight. 15

Take smalledge, rue, fenell, verbine, egremony, bittany, scabeus, avens, hounds tounge, eye bright, pimpernell,
& sage, still all thes together, wth alittle vrine of aman child, & fiue graynes, of frankensence, & droppe the
water eche night into the eyes, & the sighte will recover. proved by Robert Whateley.

To cleane a wounde. 16.

Take the white of two or thre new layd egges, beate all well together, & lay it on flaxe or towe & so
apply it to the sore.

+ To heale a wounde within nyne dayes

stampe compherey, with barrowes greace, & lay it to the wounde: & it will heale very soddenly.

for rankelinge of a wounde: 2.

stampe leekes heads root & all, put therto honey, & wheate flower, mingle all well, lay aplayster therof to the wounde,
it will doe away the payne & swellinge & heale it. proved.

+ to open a wounde almost healed: 3.
seathe the croppes og grounsell, & swines greace, & lay aplayster therof hott to the wounde.

for could in the brest: 4.

Take oyle of camamell & aquacomposita mixed together, & agaynst the fier anoynt the brest ^ chafe it therwth.

An oyntment for paynes in the Ioyntes: 4 5.

seethe sothernewoode wth wyne, butter, or oyle, till the wyne be wasted, strayne it & make an oyntement therof, good for all
griefes in Ioyntes, thighes, or other partes cominge of could.

Popular is agood oyntement for all soores:/ The fatt about the kidnes of ahare is very good to anoynte the sciatica
or like payne./ Oyle of roses is good to anoynte any disease cominge of heate, & therfore they mixe it in med-
icens to delay heate.

+ the oyle of a foxe: 6.

The oyle of a foxe is good for great palseyes or like diseases, & for the Reynes of the backe: it is made thus, the body must be
boyled till the flesh fall from the bones, & every bone from the other, gather the flotinge, & that is the oyle.

for payne in the backe. 67

Take thre pyntes of water, one pynte of butter, & ahandfull of sage, seethe all thes g together well, & anoynte the backe
therwth agaynst the fier./

+ for a dead palsey proved: 78.

Take ayounge female foxe that never had younge, make her fatt wth milke & other garbage?, kill her, wipe the body
with a clothe, but washe it not, take the greace & put it in her belly the intralls taken out, then take coweslypps
primrose leaves & flowers, sage leaves, chopp them together, & stuffe the foxe belly as full as you can & her mouthe,
& sticke some vpon her backe, shoulders & legges, rost her wth asofte fier, gather thedroppinge, clarifie it & keepe
it to anoynte the dead place;/ this hathe holpen.

for a consumption, for all diseases: 89.

Take afatt sowe pigge, put her in afayre scillatory, of speere minte, of redd fenell. of redd nepe, of eche a handfull, of
liver woorte, of clary, of eche ahandfull; & nyne dates well & cleane made & the stones pulled out, ahandfull of great
reysons, picke out the stones & washe them cleane in thre or fower waters; half aquarter of an vnce of mace,
& two stickes of sinamon brused in a morter, distill it together wth an easy fier, put it in a glasse, & sett it in the sonne
nyne dayes, & so drinke of it at yor pleasure.

A powder for them that be shorte winded: 10

Take afoxe lounges washe them in fayer water, lay them in vinegar a night & a daye, drye them in an oven when the bread
is taken out, & beate them into powder, then licoras, anyseed, fenell seed of eche a like
quantitye, beate them all into powder & serse them, throughe afine searse, & mingle them wth the powder of the
foxes lounges, as longe as you can abide the taste of it, it must be at leaste half as muche of the powder of the foxes
as of all the other powders, take it in the morninge in yor meate, drinke, or porrage, & at all howers of the daye,
or as ofte as you liste, the oftener the better, put to it suger candy, & it will take away the taste of the
lounges: proved by the la of bedford.

A medicen for them that cannot sleepe in sicknes but rave. 11.

Take the Iuce of howselike agood quantitie, as muche womans milke & as muche rose vinegar, or els rose water & vineger
meddell them all together, & wett flaxe in it milke warme, & binde it to the temples of the patient, & also wett
flaxe & put it rownde together like aballe, & binde it to the palmes of the handes & yu shall finde great ease./

for those whose leggs or armes be deade. 12

The dead place put into horsedounge, that is of hottest sorte, & ther lett it be an hower wth awarme sheete then
wype it cleane, & goe to a warme bed for awhile, lest any could should ent, after beinge in the warme
dounge.

for sore eyes proved: 123.

Take the white of anewe layde egge, streyned from the yelke, beate it to an oyle, & take of the frothe, & therto
put a sponefull of stronge white wyne vineger, & asponefull of rose water, beate all well together, & wth alitle
flaxe lay to eche temple, aplayster, but take heed it touch not yor eyes, vse it thre or fower tymes to
bedward./ it hath brought them to sight that wer seven weekes blinde.

for redd eyes: 134

Take a newe layd egge or twayne roste them very harde, then take of the shells, cut them in sonder, & take away
the yelkes, & wher the yelkes wer, scrape alittle white coprasse, strayne it throughe clothe into asaucer & make
therof an oyle, wherwth annoynt yor eyes to bedward.

for the fluxe. 145.

Take the pissell of aharte drie it in an oven, till it be harde like a rase of ginger, grate asponefull therof, & put
therto asponefull of redd roser dried, & asmuche sinamon, put all this ijto red wyne, & let the sicke drinke it warme
first & last, & at other tymes of neade be,/ this is asoverayne medicen & proved.





34.
for a fistula: 1.

Take verdigreace, Russett pepper, blacke sopes & woodbine leaves, seathe all well together, & washe the soare wthall, wett the
tente therin that must be put into it.

for the bloudy fluxe: 2.

Seathe milke & put to it roche allam & ther ^will rise acurde, drinke the whey that comethe of it, & if thy stomacke will
serve, eate the courd, for it is good for the bloudy fluxe.

for a bourninge fistula: 3.

Mixte Rye meale wth honey very thicke, & harde, make tents therof, put them in, & drawe them out, when they be softened.

for the hott goute: 4.

Take the marrowe of ahorse legge, mingled wth flower of otes, over the fier, lay it vpon woulle of anewe shorne
blacke sheepe to the goute.

for the blacke Iaunders: .5. +

Take Angle wormes washe them & scoure them in salt, then slitt them alonge, & washe the bellies & salt from them,
then strayne them with malmesey or good Ale, & drinke it.

for eyes that are bloudshed or have pushes or mesells: 6.

make atoste of fine levend breade, lay it in wyne till it be softe, then put it into aclothe, & to bedward lay it to
the eyes, & it will drawe out the rednes.

An other for the blacke ^yellow Iaunders 7.

Take greate wormes, slytt & washe them, then drye them in an oven, or vppon ahott tyle stone, beate them to powder &
take it in brothe or drinke, it is speciall good for the ^yellow Iaunders.

for an eye that hathe bine hurte so that the ball was redy to come out,
& is much swollen therwth: 8.

Take a rotten apple, cut of the same from the core, beate the white of an egge well, & dropp the Iuce of the aple therin
binde it with a clothe to the eye twice aday till it be whole.
Also the galle of ahare, & clarified honey well mingled together, is speciall good for the webbe, it muste be
layd on wth afether.

for a pushe and fellon: 9.

Take wormewood & plantayne of like quantity, strayne them wth swines greace, till it be afine salve, lay aplayster
therof morninge & eveninge to the pushe or fellon, & it will drawe out & heale.
Also take the tilte of a kilderken of beere, ahandfull of grounsell, & a good peece of the sowrest leven, boyle them
till they be thicke, like asalve, then spread it on aclothe, & as hott as may be suffered lay it to the fellon, &
withn twice dressinge you shall finde ease, it will bothe breake & heale.

When a fellon is broken: 10.

make aplayster of frenche honey, the white of an egge, & wheate meale, heat it, & this is agood playster for other
sores.

To purge the stomake: 11.

Take horehownd, & scabience, of eche ahandfull, streyne it wth ale & drinke it.

To knowe whether the goute be hott or could 12

Take rye meale tempored wth vineger, make aplayster therwth, & lay it to the gouty place, if it anoyeth it is a
could goute, if it easeth it is a hott goute.

for the head ache.123
1 Take primerose leaves, washe them & bruse them, & lay them betweene two hott tyle stones, & when the herbe is
throughly hott lay it to the nape of the necke as hott as may be endured.
2. the Iuce of Ivy & powder of pepper beinge dronke, purgeth well the heade.
3. powder of pepper boyled in white wyne, & taken hott into the mouthe, & ther held a good while easeth well the head.
4. Sothernwood boyled in vineger is also good so vsed.
5. the Iuce of redd mintes put into the eares warme doth put away deaffenes.

for soundinge or tinglinge in the eare. 134

Take the Juce of howselike & womans milke of a man child heat in an egge shell & put it hott into the eare.

To knowe whether the morphew be curable or no: 145.

picke it but the thicknes of the skine, if it bleede it is curable.

Oyle of sage: 16.

Take Sage & parsely, boyle them in oyle olyve till it be thicke, & greene, it is good for payne in the Ioyntes.

for frensey like head ache. 167

Take cowslopp flower & water, drinke it morninge & eveninge & vse it in wyne, ale, or beere.

for the same or any head ache. 178

Take bittony camamell, & alittle rosemary, bruse them alittle, then lay them betweene two hott tyle stones, sprinkell
them wth alittle vineger & lay it hott to the nape of the necke.

A medicen for a bruse proved: 189
Take comfrey roots & herbe daysy roots, boyle it well in malmesey from aquarte to apinte, & drink a good drafte
fastinge, & last to bedd fower dayes together. vse it not to much.

An other for a bruse:1920

Take pitche & grate it & give it to the sicke to drinke eveninge & morninge, wth sacke malmeset, ale, or
beere till he be whole.

20 35
for a teter or risinge in the face 1.
Take peache leaves, stampe & strayne them, & wth the Iuce anoynt the tetter, for it will heale it.

for heate of the backe proved: 2.

Take of water lillis the leaves, sowe five or six of them together on vppon the other, then pricke them wth aknife, & lay it to the
backe, that is full of heate, & vse it a week together, havinge fresh leaves morninge & eveninge, it will bothe
take away the heate & ache: Mr Shaley:

A medicen to bringe sleepe, Doctr Craver: 3.

Take twentye or thirtie Almons or more, beate them wth a sponefull of poppey seede that is white, then take two handfulls
of white poppey leaves, & as muche of lettis leaves, seath them together from aquarte to apinte & wth that wat-
ter, streyne the almon milke to drinke & let them that cannot sleepe drinke it last to bedd / proved.

A medicen of doctr craver for the head ache 4.

Take anewe layd egge rosted hard, cutt it in sonder, & take comin & bruse it, lay the comyn on bothe sides of the egge, &
lay it as hott as you can suffer to the nape of yor necke, & for lacke of comyn take sage & drye it before the fier
& lay it on the egge likewyse.

An other for the head ache: 5.

Take alloes & lay it betweene two tyles hott & so take it & lay it hott to the forehead, & to the temples & about the head,
Aso puer rose water alone is good, to take aclothe & wett it in, & lay it over yor forehead & ever as the clothe driethe
wett it in rosewater agayne:

A medicen for the rume, the head ache, or for an impostume
in the head: 6.

Take the strongest vineger you can gett, pellitory of spayne, spicknard, but not half so muche spickward as pellitory, boyle
them in vineger agood while, & when it is well boyled take it off from the fier, & lett it stand till it be could, then
put to it mustard & puer honey, & every morninge take a sponefull at once, & hould it in yor mouthe agood while, &
spitt it out agayne, & doe it seven or eight tymes in amorninge the space of aweeke together, but take it in wint-
er chiefly, for then ingendrethe rumes, & it is specially good for the palsey.

A medicen for a child that hath a sore mouthe that cannott sucke: 7.

Take Collumbine leaves, & five leaved grasse, seath them in milke together & give it with aspone to the childe.

To stoppe the vomite that cometh wth apurgation: 8.

Take milke & seathe it, & when the sicke would be stopped of vomitinge, h lett him drinke the milke hott still.

A good medicen for the stone, proved 9.

Take brome flowers & pellitory of the wall, stille them & drinke it twise aweeke, & more if nead be, for it is very good.

A drinke to avoyde the plauge. 10.

Take five sponefulls of runinge water, fower sponefulls of vineger, & asmuche treacle as a walenutt, drinke this once
a week.

A very good medicen of doctr martins to preserve one from
the plauge or sicknes: 11.

Take two or thre figges & put wthin them Rue, & certayne cornes of bay salt, & of the carnell of a walenutt or of the younge
buddes of the walenutt tree, eate it fastinge & it will preserve you, it may be made of losinges, but the walenutt
carnell is the better.

An other of doctr buttes for the same 12.

Take a browne tost & lay it allnight in ^sharpe vineger, & fastinge eate of it & lay a blacke stone in the fier, & when it is hott,
sprinkell vineger on it, & gape over it to receave the smoake.

+ A medicen for the mesells & poxe for to bringe
them foorthe. 13

Take ahandfull of figges, wipe them cleane, wth aclothe, then take apinte of stale ale, & seathe yor figges therin, till
halfe be wasted, scome it cleane, then take adrafte of the same licour, & put therto alittle powder of english saffron,
& warme give it to the patient to drinke.

A medicen for them that are thicke of hearinge: 14

Take a blacke snayle & put it in a clothe, & strewe salt vppon him, & when he hathe Rubde him self well in the salt, then
pricke him, wth a needell, & let the water runne into aglasse, & then let thre or fower droppes of the Iuce come into
the eare, then take of the comon margerom, stampe it, & drinke a little of the Iuce of it, & put a little of it into yor
nostrells, that wch is taken into yor eares must be could, & assone as the snayles be gathered they must be occupied
or els they will drye / proved by the la of arrondell.

An excellent medicen for them that are taken wth the palsey
or be benomed. 15.

Take Spanish or sainct thumber onyons, & take out the coores therof, & fill it uppe wth very good aquavity, rost it
well, & when it is rosted, & softe, strayne it as it is or wth alittle more aquavity if it be dry, keepe the oyle therof in a
fayer close glasse, or earthen pott, & morninge & eveninge anoynte the patient therwth & wth gods mercy he shall
recover helthe, proved: the la darseys

A speciall medicen for astiche proved: 16.

Take the great poppey flowers (that groweth in corne that stinketh) & still them, & of the water therof take adrafte
bludd warme wth ould ale or wyne at any tyme, & if need be drinke it twise or thrise it never
fayleth.





36.
for the morphewe white or blacke: 1.

Take brimstone melt it in stronge white vineger, & take alinen clothe & wett it in it, & washe well the morphew, bothe
Eveninge & morninge. Also take wormewood, seath it in water, & washe the place thre or fower tymes aday therwth
the oftener the better, & give him of the shavinges of ivory to drinke: the la sackford.

A medicen for the flux proved: 2.

Take the moosse that groweth on an oke, boyle it in redd wyne & drinke it: & it will helpe: e la dorrells.

A medicen for the palsey & crampes: 3.

Take the greace of afoxe, & trye it as you doe bores greace, to keepe all the yeare, & therwthall anoynte the place that
is taken: proved.

To asswage the swellinge of an adder or other venimous thinges.  4.

Take the shell snayles wch in somer tyme keepeth in gardens, & in winter moost in ould howses, breake the shells & lay
the snayles in afayer dishe, pricke them wth a pinne so that the oyle may come from them, & anoynte the patient
therwth wth afether, & so vse it alwayes as the oyle drinketh, & when you may perceave the place wher the stinge
was, take one of thes snayles not pricked & lay it to the place, & alwayes beware the swellinge rise not towards
the harte, therfor let the place wher the body is stounge lye higher then the harte, & take agood quantity of
treackell & strike vppon alinen clothe of the breadth of an inche, & gird it to the place wher the stinge is towards
the body to preserve the swellinge from the harte.

+ A medicen for the palsey that taketh away speeche. 5.

Take sage leaves & primerose leaves, & if it be in winter primerose leaves roots and all, beate them together of eche like
muche, strayne it wth stale ale & give the sicke to drinke a good quantity therof: proved.
Also kernells of greate reysons, cleane wipte, & beate in a morter, put aquavyty therof to redd wyne
& drinke it warme, will helpe. proved:

A Speciall medicen for sinewes that be shronke. 6.
Take six swallowes out of their nest, when they be flushe & redy to flye; beate them in a morter fethers gutts & all
& beate wthall two handfull of mallowes that growithe in morish grounds, & two handfulls of the topps of rosmarye,
it must be beaten so longe till it be very smale, so that the feathers be not seene, wthin fower & twentie howers, frye
it wth sweete may butter & salt, till it be half fryed, then put it in astone pott, & stopp it close nyne dayes,
then frye it throughly wth more may butter. Strayne it, & keepe it in a fayer pott close, & therwth anoynte the
place greved & it will helpe: proved:

A purgation for the stomacke: 7.

Take an apple or peare & take out the coare, & then take nyne seeds of spurge, blaunce them as you doe almons, bruse
them, & put them into the apple, wth alittle ginger, & powder of sinomon, roste the apple well, & then eate
it eyther fasting or when you goe to bedd, this will purge wthout hurte: the la Sackforde.

A medicen for the pinne or webbe in the eye: 8.

Take white wyne & put it into abason that is bright, & put bay salt to it, & let it stande for nyne dayes, two
of the dayes shake the bason, & at the nyne dayes ende, put it into aglasse, & so keepe it till you haue
neede of it, it is good for redd eys a eyes also.

A good medicen for the harte that beateth or tremblethe
in the body Cominge of sorrowe or thought: 9.

Take acurrall, & braye it in amorter of brasse, smale to powder, meddell the powder wth suger Rosett, eate
of it firste & last, & you shalbe whole.

A medicen for the migrome. 10.
Take rue & stampe it, & take the Iuce & put it in one of yor eares, & stoppe it, thre droppes of rue is well, & lye doune
on that side: well proved:

A medicen for the head ache. 11.

Take oyle of camamell, warme it on the fier, & all to chafe it vppon the temples & foreheade, this is good, & as
good for any ache in arme or showlder, to chafe it therwth.

A speciall powder for the pestilence 12.

Take sentory, spignard, turmentill, betony, scabeus, pimpernell, the browne may thorne, sage, & >sonicke, gather
them before midsomer, & dry them in achamber out of the sonne, when they are well dryed, braye them to powder
searse them cleane, & eate them fastinge.

A drinke for weakenes of the stomacke: 13

Drinke mace & fenell seede, brused, & boyled in wyne, it is good at all tymes, but best in the morninge to be dronke:
proved by doctr Sheltons.

for could in the brest: 14

Take oyle of camamell & aquacomposita mixed together, & agaynst the fier anoynte & chafe the brest.

for an ache in the showlders, necke, or any cricke
be it never so evill: 15

Take hemlocke, a stinkinge herbe & comon, lay it betweene two hott tyles, & when it is throughly hott & moyste,
lay it wher the griefe is, & fume the head wth olibanu: proved

An other for the same. 16

Take hemlocke, & bake it in a pastie, & when it is well baked, lay it as hott as may be suffered to the
payne, & it will helpe: proved.

21 37
for a stiche: 1.

Take a great onyon, & pull out the coore, & wher the coore was, put baysalt, & rost it in imbers in a paper, & lay it hott
wher the payne is:
Also take bay salt dryed on the fier in a fryinge panne, put it in abagge, & quilt it & lay it wher the stiche is, as
hott as may be suffered.

A remedy for the stiche & windy stomacke: 2.

Take younge mintes, & put it into adishe, & sett it into the oven, & drye it & make make powder of it, keepe it close in
a boxe till it be occupied, & when the stiche, winde or any thinge troublethe the stomake, put asponefull of this powder
in yor porrage or egge, & you shall finde greate ease of it.

A proved medicen of doctor raydons for the stone. 3.

1. The berreys of haythorne, stilled & dronke fastinge is at all tymes very good:
2. Also elder flowers, dried & made in powder, & dronke wth ale, beare or white wyne is wondrous good.
3. Also parsely stampt & strayned, & dronke fastinge wth white wyne is very good.

A medicen for the collicke: of d: michels 4.

Take hartes tonge, sage, & parsely in the month of maye, make powder of them, & take it in yor por^rage or dr-
inke & it will help.

An other of d: cromells for the same. 5.

Take Alexand seede, parsely seede, & fenell seede, make powder of it & drinke it.

Medicens speciall good for thos that be taken wth the palsey
but not shakinge palsey: 6.

Take the bloud of afoxe warme as it comethe from the foxe, the bloud about the harte is best, but all is good, wth
the bloud chafe the place that is taken, then take the skinne of the foxe put the rawe side nexte the place
taken, & it muste lye to it at leaste twelve howers, & in the meane tyme, till you can gett afoxe, chafe the ^benomed
place wth oyle olive & aquacomposita: this medicen of the foxe is speciall good & well proved.

To stoppe vomitinge: .7.

Take adrafte of Ale or beere, & put therto a quantity of fine sinamon , & drinke of it warme, & if it will not stopp
take sponefull of water of mints, & adrafte of Ale, & likewise warme drinke it: bothe are well proved.

for Swellinge of achildes roddes: 8.

Take redd wyne, & redd rose flowers, & comyn, boyle all together half an hower, & take alinen clothe & wett in it
& lay it to the swellinge as hott as the child can suffer it.

A speiall powltesse for a broken man: 9.

Take half apinte of redd wyne, asmuche sound honey, ahandfull of wormewoode, asmuche rue well stamped together
a peny worthe of comyn well brused, thicke all in apanne wth a penyworth of meane meale well boulted,
put this into alinen pockett playster wyse, & as hott as may be edured lay to the broken morninge & eveninge,
& wthin a fewe dayes the hole ^or wounde wilbe more large, & purged, & the matter that keepethe forthe mollified therby, to
goe vpp more easily, & after it is vpp thre dayes, & so kept well trussed & firme, take this drinke that followeth
& it will suerly binde wth good ord in aquarter of ayeare.

The drinke: 10.

Take apinte of good stronge ale, a quantity of ocke ferne (called pollipodie) the rootes, & asmuche of the roots of kneehowse
called burre, that beareth aredd flower & is greene in winter & somer, take also asmuche mouseare roots & asmuche
ivante roots, called houndes foote, washe them all well, stampe them & temper them wth ale, strayne it, &
drinke therof even laste warme, & first could till you be whole.

for all diseases in the bodye. 11.

Take of sage, of sentory, of eche a handfull, wringe out the iuce of them, & drinke it in wyne Ale, or beare
bloud warme, first & last.

A medicen for bourninge or scaldinge. 12.

Take an onyon, bruse it, & lay it to yor griefe, & in shorte ^time after you shalbe whole.

A souerayne bathe for all manor of lamenes. 13.

Take ablacke sheepes head woulle & all, then take out the brayne & tonge, & wype the head cleane, then put put
it into two gallons of fayer water, & put therto apecke of culburage, otherwise called arsesmarte, cleane
picked & washed, & so seath them together, till the bones fall from the fleshe, & skyme it cleane in seathinge, then
take away the bones, & so put it in an earthen pott or panne, & cover it, & take of the same brothe wth the fleshe
& herbes, asmuche as you shall thinke good to bathe the place of the patient & as hott as the patient may
suffer it, bathe him wthall eveninge & morninge, & so lay therto a warme linen clothe, & keepe it warme,
& so vse it till you finde ease.

for acutt eyther of sinewe or veyne. 14.

Take turpentine, & beate it in fayer water till it be white, then put away the water, & take the yelke
of an egge, beate it well, & then beate them bothe together, & make therof a playster, & lay it to the
wounde, & it will cure him.





38.
A comfortable water for the liver: 1.

Take aquarte of whey clarified, of endife water, of succory water, of eche half a pinte, thre or fower sponefulls of
vergis, & the Iuce of an orange, mixe them alltogether & strayne them, sweeten the drinke wth aquantity of suger
candy, & drinke therof all one day together & so the next day if the partie will.

A very comfortable medicen for an inward griefe 2.

Take thre pintes of muskadine, & put to it cappe dates, & agreat handfull of margeram, then put in mace, cloves, nuttmeggs,
asmuche of quantity as you shall thinke good; & when you have boyled all thes together, put to it half apinte of
Rosemary water, & two or thre vnces of manus christi, then take of this eveninge & morninge.

A comfortable drinke for one that is in a greate thirste. 3.

Take fower parsely roots, as many of fenell roots & Alexander roots, two roots of borrage, afewe harts tonge leaves
of the smallest, asmuch liverworte, a peny worth of Anyseeds, asmuche of licoras beaten both, seath all thes in thre pints
of stale beere, till one pinte be consumed, then strayne it & put therto one pinte of white wyne, & so vse to drinke
as occasion shall serve, & by gods grace it will helpe & if neede be put therto alittle suger, you may vse it thre
or fower tymes aday & as ofte as you have cause, but it is specially good first & last.

A drinke comfortable to the stomake & nature. 4.

Take aquarte of clarett wyne of rosemary nyne slippes, of sweete margeram seven brauches, of mints five croppes, of
whole mace thre, asticke of sinamon whole asmuch as you may have for apeny, of dates five cutt, put all thes to-
gether in the quarte of wyne, & sett it to the fier, till it be boyled very well, when it is boyled brue it from pott to pott
but you must not burne it & in the bruinge, put some suger, & alittle redd rosewater thre sponefulls, & lett him
drinke therof at all tymes five sponefulls could, night & day.

A bagge for the stomake. 5.

Take of dried wormewood, & of sweete margeram dried of eche thre vnces, cloves two vnces brused, put it together into
a fayer linen bagge square, & lay it betweene two platters, & make it hott on achafinge dish of cooles, & when the one
platter is hott, turne the other to the fier, so shall you make it as hott as you please then lay it to the stomake
& when it growethe could, heat it agayne, & so applye it, but in no wise wett it not wth any thinge.

A medicen for an ague: 6.

Take aquarte of good Ale, of sage leaves, & bay leaves of eche a handfull, wth aquantity of longe pepper, seathe them alltogether
vntill it come to apinte, & when the fitt is felt cominge, drinke somuch therof at adrafte as you can, & goe to
bedd imediately, & sweat after it.

The makinge of smale balme water: 7.

Take apottell of white wyne, and asmuche balme (beinge finely shredd) as will thicke the wyne, half apownde of
Anyseeds picked & brused, asmuch licoras scraped & brused, all thes thinges aforesayd muste lye in steepe in yor
wyne thre dayes sturringe it thrise adaye, then still it in astill as you doe roses.
Some of this water beinge mingled wth some treacle of Iane, is good for them that hathe the poxe & mesells, &
beinge mingled wth methridatu, it is good for the plauge.
Also it is holesome drinke in a mornine fastinge, when you feele yor self inwardly not well, beinge taken wth-
 out any thinge mingled wth it: proved.

A proved medicen for the piles. 8.

Take ahandfull & ahalf of longe wo^urte, stampe it smale, put to it half apownd of may butter, or freshe butter, but it muste be
clarified, boyle them well on the fier, when it is boyled, streyne it, then put to two penyworthe of blewe copresse, one
penyworth of frankensence, sett them on the fier agayne, & let them boyle well alltogether, still sturringe them, then
streyne it, & put it into boxes it will continve fower or five yeares, you must anoynte them with this oyntement &
put them vpp wth aredd clothe: it will help them by gods grace.

A proved medicen for the piles: a consumption: 9.

Take thre pintes of newe milke from the cowe, & afewe genue graynes, & a quarter of an vnce of anyseedes, asticke of licoras,
aquarter of apownde of cappe dates, of sweete margeram, of redd sage, of settwell, of eche ahandfull, seath all
thes together in the milke, till it come to aquarte, st then strayne all throughe aclothe, & take an vnce of treakell
& two vnces of manus Christi, & six blades of English safron dried & grounde very smale, all thes put in together
& lett the milke seathe alittle, & put the same in some thinge to keepe, & let the partie drinke therof in the
morninge, at noone & at night.

A very good drinke for the greene sicknes & to scoure
the bodye. 10.

Take thre sticks of afigge tree, & thre stickes of awalenutt tree, a handfull of redd sage, a good handfull of penyriall
apownd of reysons of the sonne stones & all thre cappe dates stones and all, bruse them all toether, & put them
in apottell of white wyne, & seath them from apottell to aquarte, then take them offe, & streyne them
& put into the drinke an vnce of the best sugercandie & drinke it morninge & eveninge, & by gods helpe
it will helpe them. Probatu

22 39.
A proved medicen for an ague: 1.

Take agood handfull of cardus benedictus, boyle it well in possett ale & then drinke it an hower before the fitt dothe
come, & sweate after it. Also take the iuce of marigould leaves, & a peace of rye leaven, temper it well
together, that is may looke greene of the iuce, then make acake of it as broad as yor hand, bake on afier slice
so that it be not bourned, then sprincle on it white wyne vineger on bothe sides, & so tourne it on the fier slice
till the vineger be dried in, then lay it vppon the hole of yor stomake as hott as you cann endure it, an hower
before the fitt comethe, you muste sweate & in any wise beware of could, this halthe helpt many in once dressinge
but if you vse it in due tyme, & beware of coulde it will cuer you in thrice dressinge.  by gods helpe.

A medicen for aswellinge in arme or legge that is
harde & redd. 2.
Take the white of an newe layde egge beaten, put to it asponefull of oyle olyve, asponefull of redd rosewater, beate all
thes well together, till they come to an oyle, then anoynte & Rubbe well the greeved place and after roule it harde
wth a rowler it must be so drest twyse aday, & it will helpe by gods grace: probatu.

A proved water for atetter, ringe worme or heate in the face
called mercury water. 3.

Take half an vnce of puer mercury, aquarte of runninge water, put the water in a newe Earthen pott grinde
your mercurye well & smale, then put it into yor water, sturre it wth asticke till it be consumed, then sett it
on the imbers till it be so hott as you may hardly abide yor finger in it, alwayes sturringe it, but let it not
seathe, when it is hott take it offe, & when it is could put it in aglasse for yor vse.
It must not touche any mettall but earthe or glasse, they that vse must take a little of it in athinge of glasse,
or ea^rthe, & beware it touche not their eyes nor mouthe, & they must not in any case lay on to muche at
one tyme. probatu.

A proved medicen for the plauge. 4.

Take aquantitye of ivie berryes, drye them well that they may be beaten very finely into powder, then take of this
powder somuche as will lye vppon ashillinge, & put it on areasonable draught of the best maulmesey, then lett
the patient drinke it & be presently had to bedd, & rast into asweate & after kept very warme.
This medicen must be taken wthin twelve howers after the partie is infected, it will make some to caste,
some to scoure, others to bringe out the soore. proved.

An other for the same. 5.

Take apinte of the best maulmesey, bourne it, & put to adraught of it some treacle of Iane, & give it to the
patient to drinke, that is infected, & if he cast give him an other draught & so continve in givinge of him
malmesey & treacle till he leave castinge, or els his stomake be throughly purged, after give the
patient adraught of bournt malmesey wthout treacle, & so rast him into asweate, & let him be after kept
very warme, & by the grace of god he shall have helpe.

+  A soverayne medicen for the heate in the face proved
by many often tymes. 6.

Take brimstone beate it very fine & boult it throughe laune, way out adramme of it then take two vnces of
may butter, worke the brimstone & the butter well together, then put it in alittle earthen pott, & sett
it on the imbers, sturringe it still till it be boyled, enoughe, & so reserve it for yor vse in agalley pott.
The partie that vsethe this oyntement must drinke plantayne water fastinge five sponefulls mingled wth
suger fowerteene dayes together, & fast after it an hower, or two, duringe wch fowerteene dayes the oyntment
must never lie offe, but as it driethe in it must be new anoynted with afether or acleane finger: mr nicholas
goeges medicen

The makinge of adregge for winde in the stomake. 7.

Take of carroway & of anyseeds comfites, of fenell seeds comfites of eche two vnces, one vnce of colliand
seedes, two vnces of anyseeds, one vnce of suger candy grosely beaten, eight knotts of gallingall
cut in very thinne slyces.

for the ronninge of the reynes. 8.

Take rise & lay it in water all night, boyle it after in milke of one cowe of one collour, when it is almost
boyled, put into it Archangll flowers redd or white, redd for men white for women; season it with
suger, sinamon & nvttmeges & so eate it, & closinge the backe & rowlinge it: proved.

To make an oyntement for the palsey, crampe or any other
comon rume. .9.

Take primerose toppes with yonge speere leaves, couslippe toopes, waleflowers that be single toppes, & all rose-
mary flowers & buddes, sage budds when it is redy to blowe, camomell when it is redy to blowe, & smale
lavend when it dothe blowe, take of eche of thes flowers so many, as when they be chopped smale eche
one by him self, they will take apinte of hogges grease that is fresh, well tried & mad so like soyft dowe,
every sorte of flowers must haue apinte of greace, & so lay the one vppon the other as tyme will come
to gether them, the last of all wilbe yor lavend, & you must take the greatest quantity threof, & let them
stand.

stande afortenight all together, then take & boyle them on a softe fier till it be greene, then strayne it into
a cleane vessell, & so let it coole all night, the next day make ahole in it, & lett all the water
come out cleane, then melt it & put it in cleane potts & vse it.

A speciall approved medicine for aches, goutes,
+   sciaticars & bruses: 1.

Take the browne fatt wormes that breadeth in ould rotten trees lay them in a fayer dishe & pricke
them well that the fatt may come from them into the dishe, then gett agood quantity of the best
pitche, beate it very smale & haue in redines some fine flaxe, first take the wormes wth the
fatt that comethe from them, & chafe rubbe well the Ioynte wth the same so farr as the ptie
is grieved, & when it is quite wasted in, strewe yor beaten pitche round about it so thicke that
it may be covered all over wth it, then wrappe the flaxe very finely aboute it, & so lett it lye nyne
dayes wthout sturringe of it, & if the ache goe further follow it wth the same medicine as be-
fore, probatu

A medicine for deafeness or payne in the eares: 2.

Take cummen seede, bruse it & boyle it in good malmesey if it may be had, or sacke, this beinge
well boyled, put it in aclose pott; then take alittle tunnell, & put the lesser end into yor ^the eare,
& the other end over the mouthe of the pott, so that the steame of the lycour may ascend into
the eare, & so continue it a good tyme, then haue in present redines the thinges that followe:
first the water that droppeth from ayounge ashe beinge burned in the fier, then take gelliromana
seedes, bruse them & put them into the ashe water, then take blacke woll & deepe it into the
sayd lycour, & so put the seedes woll & water into afine peace of lawne, first lett it dropp into the
eare, then stopp it close wth the same & after lye & sleepe yf you can: proved.

+ A medicine for the stone in the bladder, & to be taken
in great extremitie. 3.

Take agreat spanish onyon, & pull out the coare very cleane, then put the best frankensence you can gett
into it, & castell soape of the best & blackest, then rost the oynion very tender, & spredd it on aclothe, &
lay it to the parties navell, that is trobled, as hott as he can suffer it, this medicine will so breake
the stone that you shall avoyd it easilye.

A medicine for the stone in the kidney: 4.

Take creyfishes picke them very well & cleane, then bruse them & distill them, & when you feale yor selfe
greaved, take thre sponefulls of the water at one tyme & no more at once, this is good to breake
the stone & also for restoritye.

The makinge of manus Christi very good for the
wynde collicke. 5.

Take parsely seede, fenell seede, carroway seede, tyme seede, longe pepper, white pepper, blacke pepper, of the
best ginger, of colliander seede wch must lye two howres in white wyne vineger, & then they must be beaten
taken out, & dried by the fier very well, you must take of every of thes seeds & spices adram, then
beate them all fine together, then take apownde of fine suger, beate it beate it & put it into a
posenett & put to it six or eight sponefulls of red rose water, then sett it on the fier vntill the suger
be moulten, then put to the sayd suger & rose water all the seades & spices, & sturre them well to-
gether, & put them over the fier & boyle them as they doe other manus Christi.

An other present remedy for the wynde collicke.  .6.

Take apinte of malmesey & ahandfull of the toppes of another tyme, boyle it well together & streyne it, &
so drinke it very warme, & it is apresent remedy for the wynd collicke. proved.

= A most excellent Iulepp to refresh or coole any body
distempered wth heate or drouthe in agues or hott diseases 7.

Take of barly water aquarte & put it into a bottle glasse & lett droppe into the same, some of excellent
oyle of vitrioyle, or oyle of sulpherd for want of the other, then shake it well alltogether,
to disperse the oyle; & when you have perfectly temperd it, then take two very goode pomegarnards
& cutt them intwo & squise all the Iuce into it through some collander or strayner, to keepe out
the kernell or huske, or asmuche surope of violetts or surope of lemons, as of the Iuce of two
pomegarnard, then take six penyworthe of white sugercandy & beate it very smale, & put it also into
the barly water, & lett it dissolve therin, & then drinke therof as nead requireth; & it is the moste
sufferant & pleasant Iulope that ever was made by the arte of man.

To breake the fleame: 1.

Take apinte of Isoppe water stilled, on quarte of good muskadine, thre good races of case ginger, pare them cleane
& cutt them in slices, & twise so much licoras, pare it lykewise, on good handfull of sugercandy grose
beaten, put all the premisses together in aglasse, & shake them together, & stopp it close & let it stand
fower & twenty howers & drinke therof at yor pleasure.

A good medicine for deafnes cominge by the payne
of the heade. 2.

Tak agood deale of fetherfewe, frye it in grey salt, & lay it to the sooles of the feete as hott as may be endured,
vse it thre dayes together & keepe the party very warme & it will helpe by the grace of god.

An other for deaffnes: 3.

Take oyle of bitter almons nve drawen, warme it alittle on the fier, & droppe once or twise aweake on dropp at on
tyme of the sayd oyle into the eares, then keepe the Eares stoppt wth fine linte, but not to harde, & it will
by gods grace helpe, if the defenes cometh by wermes arosted oynion is very good beinge put into the eare
but not farther then it may be easily gotten out.

A good medicen to make on solible. 4.

Take mallowes & boyle them in water wth a peece of veale & reysons of the sonne & so eate the meate & broth,
& it will make you sollible.

An oyntiment called flos vnguentoru very good for nve woundes
Cutts or sores; Also excellent good for the winde collicke, beinge
spred on a clothe, & layd to the navell: 5.

Take of Rosen & perrosen of eche half apounde, of olibanu fower vnces, of hartes suett fower vnces, of masticke
two vnces, of mirre one vnce, of camphire half an vnce, of good turpentine two vnces, of white waxe fower
vnces, lett yor Olibanu, mirre & masticke be made into powders, & searced eche by them selves very finely
bis then take yor waxe & suett & dissolve them vppon asofte fier, & when they be moulten, have redy yor powders
& put them therin, alwayes sturringe them vntill they be all moulten, then have redy apottle of white wyne
hott in apanne, & streyne all yor stuffe through afayer canvas clothe into it, & then put in all yor turpen-
tine, & then yor camphire finely powdred, alwayes sturringe it together, vntill it be could, then make
it vpp in a rowle & keepe it for your use.

A salve called Rupertoriu & for to make
a Corseye.  6.

Take Cantarides & powder him & asmuche of vnslaked lyme & of blacke sope as muche as all the other, mingle
all thes together, in maner of aplayster, & lay it to the soare as broad as you will have it.

A good medicine for swellinge in the stomacke. 7.

Take bole armoniake, and aquantity of red vineger, & asmuch of good Ale, putt them together, & drinke
it fastinge in the morninge, & abstayne two howers after. proved.

A good medicine for the pyles: 78

Take awhite ^an owlde harde dogges turde that is white & boyle it in sallett oyle, & anoynt the place therewth

A salve for ascaldinge or burninge: 89

Take the yellowe mosse of an ashe, & scrape it into butter never salted, & put it into apan & let boyle on
the fier till it come to a salve, & therwth anoynt the soare.

A speciall good medicine for any bruse or blowe
howesoever: 190

Take new cowe donge, & fry it in apann wth sheepe shuett & white vineger of the best, when it is well
fryed, take it off the fier & spredd it thicke on aclothe & lay it to the greefe as hott as the patient can
suffer it, & so continue it wth freshe playster till the patient be well.

A very good medicine for the Runinge of the reynes
or any stripe: 101

Take a pottle of newe milke, of on browne or redd cowe, put it into apott & put therin thre good greate pars-
neapes, let them seath well together vntill the milke be very thicke therwth, then take out the parsneapes, &
put therin half apinte of Red rosewater, aquantity of sinamon, & some fine white suger, & then lett it seethe
alittle agayne, & so lett the patient eate therof many tymes aday & it will cure them.

To heale a wounde onlye wth drinke. 112

Take the donge of ahorse continvally standinge in the stable newly made, & wth Renish wyne streyne
out the Iuce therof, & lett the patient drinke therof thre tymes adaye aprety quantitye
of that wyne, & power some into the wounde, & wett aclothe in the wyne as often, & lay it to the
wounde, & it will heale it very soone.





42.  To stoppe bloode at nose mouthe eare or wounde.  1.

Take in marche or may chiefly afayer lynen alothe, & wett it in af the spawne of frogges, nyne
dayes dryinge the clothe every day in the winde, then lay upp the clothe safe, & when you haue
neede, take apeace of the clothe, & put it into the place whe^re the blood cometh forthe, & it will
presently stopp it.

A very good medicine for the winde collicke. 2,

Take Camamill agood handfull cleane picked, & seethe it well in white wyne, & drinke a quantity therof
somewhat warm & so continve it twise or thrice, & it will helpe.

A very good medicine to make on sollible:  3.

Take of currantclene washed & well dried out pounde, stipe them in this licour following all night decoctinge in

this is well boyled, put in yor Currant, & boyle yor water to asuroppe, & when you neade take two good
sponefull at atyme with the surrope./

for the winde collicke or the after throwes: 4

A very good medicen for the heate in the face, or
any parte of the bodye: 7.

A medicen for the wormes in Children: 8

Take the Iuce of wormewoode; & allowayes beaten to powder, & mingle it wth the Iuce, & make aplayster therof
& lay it vppon the navell, if the child hath wormes it will sticke fast, & so let it lye vntill it fall of it
self, & if the child have no wormes it will not cleave at all: proved

Good vsiall wordes for the Curinge of woundes: 9.

I doe aske health of our lord & saviour Iesus Christe, by the way of Charitye for this man or woman, that
his disease may heale,  & swell no more, but heale all outwardlye, & nothinge inwardly, all downwards,
& nothinge vpwards, from the Crowne of the heade, out of the soole of the foote, then say the ten
Comaundement, & the articles of the beleef.

A medicen for the bloody flux = approved: 10.

Take areasonable quantity of Conserve of slooes on the topp of a knife thre tymes & eate it, then take a smale
quantity of plantan water, & mingle it wth asmuch of the Conserve of slooes, as will collour it, & drinke
it a quarter of an hower after you have taken the slooes, this vse thre or fower tymes & it
shall cure the patient, ./ Also preserve of blacke berreyes, or preserve of slooes is very good
of them selves for the fluxe./

An other proved medicen for the same. 11.

Take Red corrall, & the gall whch they make Inke wthall, & beate them very smale to powder, & searce them
severally, through afine peace of Lawne, take of eche like quantitye, & put therto two droppes of English
honney, & make two pilles of the bignes of tw pease, & take them thre mornings together./

An other for the same: 12.

Take good Risse, picke it & washe it very cleane, then boyle it in distilled plantan water, vntill
it be very softe & thicke, & so let the patient eate of it at all tymes/ & it will helpe him
by god grace./

A medicen for a soare mouthe: 7.

Take aquarte of white wyne & put to it alittle Runninge water, els the wyne wilbe to stronge, put into it
two handfull of honneysuckells, or yf they can not be gotten then take the leaves of sage, of Rosemarye,
of violett, of plantan, of herbe grace, of eche ahandfull, aheade of howselike, boyle all thes in the wyne
& water, & scume it cleane; after it is well boyled; putt in aquarter of apownde of Roche allam, & asmuch
honney as will sweeten it, & boyle it alittle & scume it agayne, then take it from the fier & when it is
coulde, streyne the Iuce out of the herbes; so reserve it to thy vse washinge thy mouthe therwth,
as ther is cause./

A very good water for the heate in the face, or
for the morphewe/ 8.

Take apottle of springe water, the which you must boyle, till half therof be consumed, then take on vnce of
mercury sublimatu, & put it into the water that remaynethe, then let it boyle half aquarter of an hower,
then take it off & let it coole, & put it in aglasse for your vse; you must dipp aclothe in it, & so put it on the
place that hathe the heate, once or twice in adaye; yf you feele it Itche or smarte, take yor owne spittle &
wett the places; but if it rayse blisters, take a little rawe creame & anoynte the blisters therwth, &
it will goe away wthout any more harme./

A very good medicen for the heate in the face, pimples
+ scabbes or such like: 9.

Take half apinte of red rose water, & aquart of a pint of plantan water, put them in aglasse together,
& put therto, half an vnce of mercury sublimatu, beaten into a fine powder, then take the Iuce if fower
oranges & fewer lemons, & put it likewise into the glasse; let thes be alltogether, the space of five or
six dayes before you vse it, & when you vse it the place greaved, once every day: proved.

A proved medicen for a could stomake./ 10

Take on sponefull of aqua composita, & so much capons grease as the enf of a mans thumbe, put them together, &
warme them on achaffendishe of cooles, then take afine linen clotheas broad as the palme of yor hand, &
bast it wth alittle bumbast, that beinge done, dippe the bumbast in the sayd water, & lay it warme to the
stomake, from morninge till night, & from night till morninge, apply this well to yor stomake, & you shall find ease./

To restore abody that is in Consumption: 11.

Take apinte of milke as it cometh from the Cowe, & apinte of redd rose water, & the yelkes of thre newe layde
egges, beate them well together, then put them into astillatory with an vnce of sinamon brused & so distill
them wth asofte fier, after, vse one sponefull or two in any brothe or drinke you take./

To make a quilted playster to Comforte the
stomake of aweake bodye. 12.

Take atoste of leaven wheaten bread & crome it & put it into rose leaves, speare mintes, sweete margeru, a
fewe of eche, then beate nvttmegge, cloves, & mace, & put them in malmesey, rosewater, & vineger, then
mingle thes together, & quilte it all in a linen bagge, & make it hott in adishe, & lay it vnto the
stomake, it is very good & comfortable./

A Cordiall for the tremblinge at the harte & to Comfort
the Stomake: & Iliaca passio: 1.

Take ginger, mace, cloves, nvttmegges, Cubibes, of eche aquarter of an vnce, Sinamon, gallingall, of eche
one vnce, Redd currall, amber of eche aquarter of an vnce, Aniseeds, licoras, carroway seede, dill seede, fenell
seede, of eche two vnces, make thes in fine powder & searce them, then take the valence shuger half a pounde
& so much rose water as will moysten the suger, etthe it on the fier, scume it, & let it boyle vntill it come
to some thicknes, then take it from the fier & put in all yor powders aforesayd, then stirr it wth afayer woode
sticke till it be could; then reserve it to yor use, & take therof at one tyme, the quantitye of anvttmegge,
or hasell nvtt as you shall neade.

The distillinge of water for soore eyes, by pearle, prime,
webb, lashe, or pricke. 2.

Take of sellendine, herbe grace, betonny, browne fenell, eyebright, redd rose flowers, mayden heare, or as many of
thes as you can gett, still of eche alike quantity by them selves, then of eche of the waters stilled a like qua-
ntity, & put them together in aglasse, & let the party greaved, lye on half quarter of an hower vpright
in the morninge, at noone, and at night, & dropp into his eyes at one tyme on or two droppes, & close the eye
closse afterwards, & so vse it still, & by gods grace it will helpe.

An other pretious water for soore eyes: 3.

Take smalledge, Rewe, verbine, egrimony, bittony, scabious, Aueans, houdstoug, ewfrace, pimpernell, sage,
still all thes together wth a little vrine of aman child, & fine graynes of frankensence, droppe on dropp
every night into the soore eye, & thow shalt be whole, & have sight agayne, by gods helpe.

A pretious water to Clarifie the eyes & to doe away the
pearle in the eyes. 4.

Take redd roses, smalledge, Rewe, verbine, mayden heare, ewfrace, endive, senegreene, redd fenell, hilwoorte,
& celedon half aquarter; washe them cleane & lay them in good white wyne the space of one day, & then
distill them the first water wilbe like gould, the second like silver, the third like balme, keep it in aglasse, for
it is as worthy as balme for any soore, & is pretious for ladies, in steade of balme water.

A Surrope good to cleanse the stomake & purge it of coller
salt flame & mellancholye. .5.

Take of the Iuce of borrage clarified apinte, put therto seene brused on vnce, knitt it at large in alinen clothe, also
take ginger brused two drames, of suger, of clarified honney fower vnces, of Ruberbe two drames brused, let it
seethe vntill the third parte be consumed, then streyne it & reserve it in aglasse, once aweeke or afortenight
take two sponefulls therof wth six sponefulls of this drinke followinge; take of fenell cleane washed & picked
one handfull, of Isope half ahandfull, of Ale or beare aquarte, twelve cloves brused, put into aclothe alittle
suger, boyle this to apinte, streyne it & reserve it to the vse aboue written.

A good medicen for the payne in the head & to put backe
humors from the eyes: 6.

Take redd roses, bittany of eche what proportion you will, stampe them smale then put to it the sweete suett
of ahogge purely tried wthout salt, the weight of the herbes & some whate more, then stampe them together
very smale, put them together into aclose vessell covered the space of eight dayes, then boyle them over a
softe fier, alwayes sturringe them vntill the herbes be parched, then streyne it & wth the Iuce anoynte
the foreheade, & temples, layinge after alinen clothe vnto them.

To make a woman be soone deliued if she be in greate
extremitye, the childe dead or alive. 7.

Take agood quantity of puer Amber of the best, beat it exceedinge fine to powder, then take it & searce it,
throughe a fine peece of lawne, & so drinke it in some brothe or Cadell, & it will by gods grace
presently helpe.

for the tooth ache very good medicens, & to kill the wormes,
& keepe the teethe cleane & sweete: 8.

Take aparte of aclove of garlicke, & put it onto afine peece of lawne, & put it into the Eare on the contrary side
wher the payne is. Also take a quantity of white wyne, & asmuche white wyne vineger, then gather the
toppes of broome wormewoode & seethe it in the sayd licour, & when it is well sodden take it off & put it
into aglasse, & twice or thrice at on tyme gargle yor mouthe well therwth, & it will kill the wormes
in teeth & take away the payne. Also alittle cleare honey warmed & put into the eare, of the same
side the payne is, one dropp will ease the payne.

A good medicen for the Rume distillinge downe the throate, & often
causinge payne in the teethe. 9.

Take two handfulls of Isope strippe it from the stalkes, rowle it in abrowne paper somewhat wett, then
lay it to roste vnder embers, vntill it be rosted very softe but not bourned, then take it out & laye
it vppon alinen clothe & so lay it vppon the moulde of the heade, as hott as may be suffered,
& so freshe it thre or fower tymes, lettinge it lye from eveninge till morninge.

To staye a laske or bloody fluxe: – 1.

Take afewe oulde leaues & parche them over the fier in apanne, & then beate them into power, make an Albery of stale
Ale & put some of the powder therin, seethe them well together, then lett the patient drinke therof warme &
wthin twice drinkinge it shall helpe

An other for the same: 2.

Take Almonds stampe them smale, & boyle them in honey, untill they waxe somewhat blacke, then addinge thervnto a
little sinamon eate therof morninges fastinge. Also yf you take Triacle & sinamon beaten into powder,
& mingle them together it is very good beinge in the morninge fastinge.

An oyntemente Called populeon wch is good for all heates,
inflamations, & breakinge out & for heate in the head or backe,
or any other memb of the bodye: & will cause sleape, if the
temples be anoyted therwth 3.

Take of populer leaves at the first shuttinge foorth eyghteene good handfulls, of herbe walter thre handfulls, of
petty morell, orpin, sengreene, mandrake, if any be to be gotten, of popye, mercury, beates, plantan leaves,
lettice, greate brier leaves, violett leaves, water cresses, mallowes, of each one handfull, stampe them all very smale
then put vnto them the weight of all the herbes, of puer hogges grease wthout salt, & clearely tryed, then stamp
them all together, & put it into afayer pott closely covered the space of ten dayes, then take it forthe the
& all sett vppon asofte fier stirringe it vntill the herbes begine to pearche, then take it off the fier, streyne
it, & reserve it in apott for yor vse.

To make on sleape a good medicine: 4

Tale apinte of milke seethe it & lett it coole, then take the Creame therof & the white of an egge, & alittle womans
milke, b & alittle rose water, beate them alltogether, & spread it on aclothe, or on flaxe, & so lay it to the
foreheade.

A Speciall good medicine for the avoydinge of winde 5.

Take the Iuce of Redd fenell, & make possett Ale therewth & drinke therof.

A very good medicen for the winde collicke: – 6.

Take aflinte stone & cast it into the fier vntill it be redd hott, then put it out into apottell of drinke, & ther
will arise agreate foome, let the patient drinke therof.

To make wormewoode water: 7.

Take apownde of wormewood fayer stripped & grosely shredd, put it into agallen pott, & then vnto put apottle of
good Ale, & of sacke or clarett wyne a pottle, of licoras, of Anyseeds of eche brused thre vnces, put them
all together, stirr them well, & then distill them souftlye.

A good medicen to kill wartes. 8.

Take egremony & stampe it wth salte, & lay it to the wartes & wthin fower dayes they wilbe whole gone.

A good medicen for stinkinge nostrells: 9.

Take the Iuce of blacke mintes, & the Iuce of Rewe of each like quantity, & put it into the nostrells, when you
goe to bedd, & it will helpe.

A proved good medicen for the Runinge of the Reynes: 10.

Take alittle plantan water & comfrey roots, alittle boliu beaten fine, alittle poungranett pill grated, & a
quarte of mvskadine, wth the pithe of an oxe backe, let it half boyle away, & so drinke of it morninge
& eveninge.

for the piles or emiroddes agood medicen. 11.

Take ^a very ould & hardwhite doggesturde wch wilbe on the topp of a moulehill, seathe it in sallett oyle very thike & so put them vpp therwth
& it will helpe quicklye.

A good medicen for astinkinge breathe 12.

Take two handfull of comin seede, seethe them in good wyne from apottle to apinte, drinke of it fifteene dayes
morninge & eveninge.

A pretious water of all waters good agaynste all poysons
& pestilences: 13.

= Take Turmentill, scabious, dittony, pimpernell, of each alike quantity, dsitill them all together, philosophers doe
say that it is vnpossible that any should dye of the pestilence or by poyson, that vseth to drinke of
this water.

A medicen not only to breake the stone, but so clearely to purge you
therof, that you nevere be payned therwth agayne if you vse
the same daylye:– 14.

Take the Rowes of Redd hearinges, & holly leaves that haue prickles, & dry them bothe in an oven & beate them
by them selves into powder, & then searce them, & put them together of eche like quantity, then take gromell
seede beaten to powd, half so much as the other two powders & so put them together, they beinge well mixed
together, vse dayly, agood sponefull therof in adraught of Ale beare, white wyne or remsh wyne, eveninge
or morninge, besids you may vse the sayd powders in broothes, the oftener the better, it must be kepte
dry in apott by the fier side.

The operation & effects of divers waters:– 15.

1. Burrage water is good for the stomake, agaynst the collicke & many other diseases in the body.
2. bittony water is good for hearinge, & agaynst sicknes in the body, for as mvstarde is sauce to meates, so is this
to other herbes, & it is also good for dronkennes & poyson.
3. beetewater, sage cowsloppes, & primerose, is good for the palsey.
