72 An other for the goute:  1

Take snayles & put them in a pott strawe salte theron, & let it melt well then anoynte the
place grieved therwth by the fier, & you finde ease.

For all kynde of aches: 2.

Take a pownde of Sage leaves, a pownde of rewe, halfe apownde of wormewoode, halfe a
pownde of baye leaves all this paerly picked &c.

For an ague often proved: 3.

Take one penyworthe of sacke, as muche of aquavite, a penyworth of longe pepper, as much
of sentory seedes, a penyworthe of white suger candye, sett on the fier & let it boyle
well, then let the patient drinke therof when he feeles his could Cominge, then let
him lye downe vppon his bedd & cause him to sweat, this must be druke thre tymes
& it will helpe.

To stoppe the bleedinge a cutt ^?? & to
heale it: 4.

Take vnsett leekes, & stampe them wth honey, & make aplayster therof & laye it to
the hurte.

To heale a sore that is hott:  5.

Take vnsett leekes, stampe them & strayne them wth creame, & anoynt the Sore therwth
& it will heale.

for heate in the backe: 6.

Take a quantitie of frenche barley, picke it & washe it cleane, & seathe it in morninge
water, lettinge it boyle half an hower, then strayne the barley & put vnto it
newe barley, & let it boyle asmuch then strayne the barley agayne & put vnto it more
water & let it boyle af hafe an hower, then take it & strayne out the barley & set one
the lyquor agayne, & put therto violet flowers, & planten leaves, this pappe coolethe
the heate.

for a scaldinge or bourninge: 7.

Take the yellowe mosse that grweth uppon the bowes of ashe, & shaue a good deale
of it into butter never salted vppon the fier, & let it boyle till it Come to a salve,
still stirringe it that it bourne not, then take it off & keepe it for yor use: proved.

=+ An Excelent healinge drinke be to Cure all
mannor of woundes, fistulaes, vlcers in the
bodye or owld soores.

Take of Sanicle, Bugle, Cumfrey, Ribworte, daysies rootes
& all, wood Bittony, Scabias Egremony, Spedwell, Avens,
Bramble tooles, of each of all thes one good handfull, but
of the Cumfrey & Bugle more then of any of the rest &
for want of Cumfrey in the winter take the rootes, picke
& washe all thes yearbes very Cleane & shred them very
smale, then put them into eight quartes of fayre running
water in a cleane potte & sett them on the fier & let them
boyle sowftely fowr or five howers, when it hath so wett
boyled put into it a quarte of white wine & so let it
boyle a little while, then put in a pownd of the best english
honney & let it boyle to a quarter of an hower after it, then
take it from the fier & let it stand in the potte all night
& in the morninge streyne it & keape it for your vsse.
it will in the sommer continue good a forthnight & in the
winter longer, when it waxeth sower, you must haue new

39
73
drinke in readines, for any wownd or other soore you must
take five, seaven, or nine sponfulls at one time, in the morninge
fastinge & the like quantitie at thre a clocke in the after noone
& so Continue takinge it vntill you be well, if the greafe
be aboue, the fewer sponfulld at one time is best, but if it be
belowe, then the most is best, you must washe any greafe
with the drinke that is owtward & if you see Cawse you
must tente it for a time. This drinke hath done strange
Cures on fistulaes & other inward & outward greafes, but
the time must be attended with patience so little quantitye
of drinke Cannot do wonders on a soddayne & greater
quantitye is not so good, for it will soner passe through
the bodye.

+ An approved good drinke to cleanse the stomacke &
 ? + purge the body of flemge, ,alincoly, coller, or
any other noysome humors.

Take eyght quartes of very fayre runninge water & put
it into a cleane pott & sett it on the fier, then put ther in
two vnces of Sarsapereela sliced & Cut into shorte peces,
one vnce of lignum vitae, one vnce & a Halfe of polipodium
brused, one vnce of Elicampane thyne sliced & so let them
seeth one hower & a halfe or more, now & then sturringe
it with a sticke then put therin two vnces of licoras, & let them
seeth one hower & a halfe or more, now & then sturringe
it with one vnce of Hermidactalus Cut & sliced one handfull
of Caliminte (& in steade therof take garden minte) & as many much
of the yongest nettles, then fill vpp your pott with fayre
water as full as it was at the first to eygh quartes, so let
it seeth one hower & a qter, then put in a quarte of white
wine & let it seeth one qter of an hower after, then put
in two vncres vnces of Scenae, two vnces of sweet fenell
seede brused, thre quarters of an vnce of Sticados, & halfe
an vnce of Epithamum, a quarter of an vnce of mayden heare
& as much of Adianthum, then fill vp the pott with fayre
water as at the first & so let it seeth almost halfe an
hower, then take it from the fier &* when it is cowld streyne
it, but not wringe it hard for it will be thicke, & keape
it in glasse bottles for your vsse. When you haue Cawse
to vsse it, take halfe a wine pinte therof at one time in
the mprninge fastinge & ^as much in the after none, at thre a clocke
& fast one hower after it & so vsse it vntill you haue healped
if the body be harde to worke & that this proportion doth
not giue thre or fower soles, then take a greater quantitye
at a time when it growes sower in a bo take it ^no longer
but nake new, this quantity of stuff will not cost a boue
thre shillinges at the most:

 A present Remidye for the piles

Take some spetiall good Allam & lay it on fayre runninge
water & when it is Consumed, take a browne paper & wett
therin & lay it to the place greaved & keape it to them &
it will presently driue them away.






74 = x An excelent approued medicine to driue
away any Agooe.

Take one good handfull of baye salte & beate it
very smale in a morter, then put therin as many of the
biggest & strongest Cobwebbs as the bignes of fowr
fingers, beate them well together, then put therin a good
handfull of smaledge newly gathered & cleane picked
& stampe them altogether very well, & when it is well
beaten take it out of the morter and put ther vnto two
vnces of the best venis Turpentine vnwashte & temper
them well together Two howers before the agooe fitt
Commeth devide the quantitye of stuffe & lay it equally
to both the wristes at the place wher the  powlss beateth
& with a linnen Cloth so fasten it on to the wriste, that
none of the stuffe may come out, so let it lye too fowr
and twentye howers & if it doth not driue away the
agooe at once dressinge vsse it so the second time
& it will healpe you, for it hath Cured many.
The Iuce of smaledge dronke in possett Ale first
& last is very good to remoue any agooe.

A present and espetiall good medicine for
the Blacke or yellow Iandies.

Take a pottell of stronge ale & one handfull of house
snayles beate them well & put them into the ale and then
set them to boyle & as thay boyle skume them & put into
it a good quantitye of whole cloues & two or thre
blades of mace & a prettye quantitye of saforne &
a good quantitye of hartes horne scraped & so boyle
it one halfe away & then streyne it & in the morning,
first & last let the sicke persons drinke agood drawfte,
therof & by gods healpe it will cure them in shorte time

An excelent good way to make Cakes for
one that is in a Consumption,

Take a good quantitye of yarrow & stampe it very
well in a cleane morter, then streyne it & temper the
iuce therof with some fine wheate flower & make paste
therof, then put in some reed rose water, some fine shuger
& a little sinamon & nutmegge & so make little cakes
therof & backe them in an oven with out burninge & then
vsse to eate them as often in a day as you Can the more
the better & the soner it will healpe you.

A good drinke to be Taken at the springe or
the fall of the leafe to purge away fleamge

Take six vnces of Sceenæ of Alexandria one vuce of

thre vnces of hermadactalus sliced fowr vnces of Aniseeds

A very good medicine to keape the foreheade from
wrinckelinge & to take them away

Take a quarter of a pownd of the purest white virgine
wax one vnce & a halfe of white parmasittye, halfe an
vnce & somwhat more of the whitest deere suet scrape all
thes into a very fayre pewter dishe & Cover it very close
that no smoke get in, then set it over a pott of sethinge
water vntill it be melted then take some very fine holland
& Cut it into peces of the bignes you wowld haue them
then dippe them into the stuffe vntill all be Consumed, then
dreyne them one after the other before a cleare fier vntill
thay looke in all places cleare alike, then smoth them & when
thay be smoth take a peece of deere suett & rubbe them all
over then take a slicke stone & slicke them excedingly on
both sides, when you will vsse them a little warme them &
lay them Close to the places all night. vsed by my la Riche

A good medicines to kill cornes

Take the water of an oyster & seeth in it the second parte
of an oynion untill it be very tender then lay it to the corne
& it will kill it.
olso take houslike & take of the vppermost skine & lay
it to the corne & it will ease in one night.

An excelent medicine for the runninge
of the Reynes.

Take a good handfull of the inward barke of slow bushe
the vppermost blacke barke beinge cleane taken of &
then seeth the same in a quarte of pure ale vntill it do
come to a pinte & lesse, then take it from the fier & streyn
it & drinke the halfe therof fastinge blood warme & the
rest the next day in like sorte & by gods healpe this
will cure you sone without fayle.

An other excelent medicine for the same.

Take a good handfull of neppe & stampe & strayne it
& put the nice therof into a pinte of new milke whote
from the cowe & so drinke it blood warme morninges & even-
inges fastinge & so vsse it two or thre dayes together & it
will by gods grace cure you quickly.

Parasellsus playster good for a nuber of
Diseases heere mentioned in the end of the receipt./

Take thes three gumes of galbanu ij oz; of oppaiax j oz; of amoniaru  ij oz; put them into
an earthen pott that holdeth about 3. pintes, it must be well leaded or glased wthin, then powre vpon
the said gumes a pinte of the best wyne vineger, & couer ^them that nothinge falls in them, let them so
remayne a daye & a night then sett them on the fyer till they be throughly melted, continually
sturringe them, then take a pece of new canvas that is very thinne, & strayne it into the same
pott agayne, & so sett them on a very gentle fier of coales, keeping the fier from the sides of
thes pott, that the gumes may boyle vntill the vinegar be all sodden away cokartv coapara ariae & you maye
spread them like a playster; in the boylinge of it you must continvally sturr it wthout ceasinge
least the gummes be burned & take heed that nothingfalls into it; then take a quarte of the
best oyle olyve & put it into a pott well leaded or glased that holdeth a pottle or more, & set
it on a softe fier of coales, & put into it half apoude of new waxe cut into smale peeces,
& so lett it mel tin the oyle by leysure, & when you are sure the waxe is throughly melted, put
into it jli & di of litharge of gold beaten into very fine powder, sturringe it continvally
wth a sticke or spatula or such like by little & little vntill the thinges be well & throughly
mixed together, let it boyll tell it be grown to a terme substan? or body wich shall ???? by & the matter be of a tawnye coulor, & thicke, then take it of the fyer
& when it hath lefte boylinge a little while take of the gumes that wer first boyled
& wth yor spatula take of them the quantitye of a nvtt & put the same into the said oyle sturringe
it continvally, & so by like quantitye at seuall tymes but in yor gumes till all be putt in & it be
well mixed with other thinges, your gumes wilbe allmost an hower in puttinge in, therfore when
you see it begines to be cold, set it on a few imbers, takinge great heede it be not ou hott,
for then it will all run ou into the fier, for it is very hott of it self: byut yet yf the worste
should happen that it sodaynly begins to run ou that you can not stay it; haue a bowle of water
by you, & quickly put it into the water, then take it out of the water & sett it on the fier agayne
& when thes gumes are well put in, well mixed & melted, put in thes thinges followinge -
vid ij oz of beddliu then take the two kindes of Aristologia rotuda, calaminaris, myrrhe
frankensence, of euy of them one oz. beate them int overy fine powder, & searce them through
a very fine cloth twise, then mingle them together in a paper, & powre them into the said
oyle leysurly, continvally sturringe it, then powre into it j oz of oyle of bayes, & lastly
put therin pure & fine turpentine iiij oz. when you haue put in the turpentine take
it of the fier, & sturr it wth very great diligence, els yor turpentine will cause it run into
the fier, & when you will know whether it be sufficiently sodden, put alittle therof into
cold water, & if it be not so softe that it cleave to yor fingers it is well, but if it
cleaue to your fingers it is to softe, & therfore let it boyle longer till it be enoughe,
then take it of the fier & powre so much of it into a great bason of cold water, as you will
worke into agreat roll, & when it is well could that you may handle it, anoynt yor hands
with oyle of camomill or oyle of roses, & knead it well wth yor hands, & when it is well
wrought make it vp in roules, & when you lay vp this playster lapp it vpp in oyld
pap 0r oyld leathem oyle them once ayeare that you keepe it in, for it will keepe
the better from oumuch dryinge, & lay it out of the sonne & will winde & it will
last about fiftie yeares & be then as good as at the first./

The uertues of this playster:

Paraselsus sayth they are inumerable, it is good for old & new sores, it dryeth; it clenseth;
it breedeth good flesh, it confirmeth, it comforteth, it healeth more in a weeke then any
ither in a moneth; it will not suffer any sore to putrifie or corrupt, or any dead or
euill flesh to growe./ for sinewes cutt, bruised, prickte wth a thorne; or otherwise, it
is most excellent; it draweth out of woudes Iron, wood or leade, & other thinges
if you doe but lape it theron./ it cureth bytinge of venemous beasts: it causeth
all kindes of impostumes & boyles to rypon yf you doe but laye it theron./ it is
most excellent agaynst a fistula or canker, the shyngles, or St Anthonys fier
& also

41
77.
& also a soueraigne & present helpe to assuage all paynes and all kind of aches & all
kinds of woudes. Also it hath bine often tryed by expsrience, that it is a singuler
& present helpe for bones out of Ioynte, so that by layinge one, two th or three playsters
therof at the most it hath healed perfectly & soudly Armes out of Ioynt so sthat the
pties haue had no more payne nor weakenes./ Also for Ioynes & thrusts you must
not tent them, & but only lay this playster ouer it and two playsters at the most comonly
wilbe sufficient to heale it, or any other sore or swellinge./ but if the dead flesh be
on a sore before this playster be lay on, it will not destroy it nor plucke it out,
but if it finde none ther, it will not suffer any to breede. probatu by Tho potter./

Another Speciall good medicine for the Same.

Take mosse of an oke that wch groweth on the boughes not on the

a very good way to make merkury water./

Take one ounce of ^pure Merkury sublimatum, and beate yt exceeding small then
then take a quarte, then take a quarte of pewre Runing water and put
the Mercury therin, then beate and worke them together a long tyme

som blanched Almond beaten in to yt and then straine it./

Another rare and Curryowse oyntment to make
to make the face and & Skin most fayre Cleere
whyte and smooth./

Take som pure Barrowes grace and dres yt and packe yt very fine

somuch as will Cou yt, and every day shifte yt into fresh water and bete

lyke wise in Rose water vj daies, beating yt likewise every day as yow
did before./ yf yow vse yt Rightly yt will looke moste whyte, a earthen
pan is best to put and beate yt in, for a pewter dish be yt never
so Cleane will Color yt, this done if yow will goe to the Cost yow
may putt in som Amber greece yt wilbe somuch the better to increse
bewty for the time ^but not to alay heate but increase yt./ yt will att the
first seeme as yt weare full of Ragg, but more beating will helpe yt:

A very Exellent water for one in a Consumcon

Take a Red Cock and pull him aliue then quarter him and
take owte his bowelles very Cleane and wype him very cleane
wth a Linen Cloth, then put the quarteres in to a pottell of
the best Sack yow Can gette, and put vnto them of Tyme, of
Rosmary of peniroiall, of pimpernell of each one small handfull, of
dates (the stones being taken owte) half a pounde, so lett all lye
and Steepe in the Sack ij howers then distill them in a stillitory
and of the water therof vse ij spoonefulles one howre befor yow
goe to diner, and asmuch before yow go to Supper, and yt will
much Restore the bodie: ashuredly prooved by the Lo Cheefe Iustice
Poppham./

+A good medicine for any scaulding or Burning

Take an onion and bruse owte the Ioice therof and presently after
ani on is greved anoynte the place wth the Ioice of the said onyon &
so Contynew dressing vntill the fier be owte of the greeff the
wch wilbe quickly. Then haue Redie a good quantity of the bark of
the bark of a yong Elme, but not of any owlde Tree or bough, scrape
the vttmost Rinde away, and the second barke take and cutt in to small
peeces and put yt into faire water and sett ^yt over the water fiere
to boyle, but not to fast, and as any skume Riseth in the boyling carfull-
ly take yt of, and Reserve yt in a viall for yor vse, and wth a ffeather
anointe the sore therwth, and lay an owlde peece of launde next vnto yt
and so vse to dress yt and this will heale yow speedily if the sore be not
very deepe if yt bee, then vse som of the powltis heeraffter follow-
ing (the wch is forffellones) the fier beeing first taken owte as is
afforsaid.

A very good powltes to take away the angwish of
a fellon and to breake yt speedyly.

Take som new milke and put therin som cromes of whyt breade
and boyle them together very tender to a powltes then take yt
from the fiere and put therin the yolke of an egg and haue redy
som whyt Lilly Rootes wrapped in a bronne paper and Rosted very tender
first bruse the Rootes to a papp then mix them well wth the other thinges &
so applie yt to the greef a littell warme, when yt is broken vse som of
the healing powltes or Sallue heerafter mencioned and yt will soone
heale the greef. +

Take ij quartes of the best Sallet oyle that is very pewre and sweete and

Turnpentine, then sett yt over the fier and ever sture yt well together

oyle allone as is afforsaide to any greeffe, but yt is best to be vsed wth the salue
or powltes next following, in follio (8) the wch if yow doe, yow may leaue owte the twrpentine
for yowr powltes, and haue still inredines the oyle.

,,For an ache if yt be nine or ten yeares
owlde.

Take twoo peniwoorth of of Redd wax and half a sponeful of peper

For extream ytch in maner of a leprosy.

Take Rye blades Chopp them and make porege therof wthowte Sallte
and eat yt first and last, and yt will helpe.

+ ,,A very good way to Scule any fowle Bone

Take Arras Rootes and seeth beate them very small to fine powlder
then temper the same powlder of the Rootes wth faire water to the
thicknes of paste, and so lay yt to the fowle bone and vse to dress
yt as there is Cause, and yt will Scale away fowle bones:

A spetiall medicine to stay a greeff that yt
shall gangreene no further.

Take a good quantitie of Sorrell and wrapp yt in a whyt or browne
paper and lay yt in the imberes to Roste and when yt it is very
tender take yt owte and make yt into a rowle and put yt very
hott round abowte the place greved not on the sore but neere
vnto yt and so lett yt ly to and Renew yt as there is Cause. this
will not suffer the sore to goe further, and the salue and powtes
afforsaid yow may heale yt quickly

A very good oyle or Balsum for a greene wounde.

Take a quarte of whyt wine iiij pownde of oyle oliue, ij poundes of
Turpentine, the leaues flowres and seedes, of St Iohnes-woorte,
of each ij greate handfulles gently brused, and put them wth all the
other thing in to a greate duble glass very Closse and boyle the
same glass in a Cettle of water, wth som straw in the bottom
wch don straine the liquor from the herbes then put in to the said
Liquor the like quantitie of leaues, flowres, and Seedes, as yow did
before, but no more wine nor oyle and use yt as yow did before. &
so keepe yt for yor use and yow haue a greate secrett.

A very good medisine for the wasting of the back

Take waybrode leaues, parsly, Alexander, Sorrell, violet leves,
of each lyke quantitie, beate them well together and straine them
then take one Spoonefull of the Ioice therof and mix yt wth
iij spoones full of Almond milke. or possett ale and drinke that
quantitie nine morning together and yt will help one although
hee were in a Consumption.

A very good medisine to Comforte and
Clense the back and the kidneyes

Take a pottle of faire spring water, and put therinto half
a pound of Eringoe Rootes as new as yow Can geate them
and meerly of themselues wthowte any Canding or Confection
ony bruse them very well befor you put them in to the pipkin
of water and put therin also iij good Liquoras Stickes brused
Lett all boyle together untill yt be fully Consewmed to a quarte
then take yt from the fier and evry moring drinke a pety
draught therof for sevnteene or eyghteene dayes together:

A very good medicine for a weake body

Take a legg of veale and wash yt very kleane and put in to a
gallon of very faire Spring water, Sett yt on the fier and
and Skeeme yt very kleane, and then put in a quarter of
a pound of the best Red Corant you Can geate and half a pound
of the Rootes of orpine the skin beeing Cleane peeked of, and som
grated bred to thicken yt lett yt boyle softely over the fier
vntill yt Com to a pottell and then put in half an ownce of
of Corall finly beaten and half an ownce of Cinamonde
finly beaten and lett yt be stirred when the spice be put
in and boyle iij or iiij walmes after yt. This you must drink
Thre tymes a day. In the morning and fast ij howres after
yt. and howre before diner and one howr before supper
And yt must be very warme when yow drinke yt.

Another Souaine medicine for a Consumption
in generall.

Take a very fleshy Capon of three yeares owlde, and put him into
a large pott wth att leaste iij Ale quart of faire water, and iij good hand
full of wood Bittony, let them seeth solong together vntill the Capon
be sodd all to peeces, then take yt from the fier and eate as ofte of the
broth as yow Can in a day, wth owte puting  Sawlte or any other
thing into yt and so Continue taking yt for a good space vntill
yow finde health.

An exellent Cullis for a weake body.

Take a Chicken and boyle yt in faire water wth a little mace and
a Crust of whyt Bred and when yt is sodden take yt vpp and flea yt
and take the flesh and Boanes and stampe yt in a mortar wth a few
allmonds then take som of the same broth yt was boyled wthall and straine
yt thorow a fine Cloth wth Rosewater and Sugar and after boyle
yt a wallme, and so take yt of and drinke of yt as often as yow
Can and yow shall find much Comfort in yt.

Many good medicines for the piles and Emirodes.

Take a pinte of the strongest Ale you Can geate and put therin a
good handfull of Red-Rose-Leaues. Boyle them well together vntill
the leaues be tender, then take a litle otmeale finly beaten and put
therin and when yt is boyled lay som of yt to the place greeved as
hott as yow Can endure yt and yt will in short tim aswage the
swelling and take away the paine therof.
Take the papp of an Apple and put therto the yolke of a new laid egg
woorke them together then put in viij Chiues of Saffron small grownd
boyle all these together uppon embers allwaies stirring them then make
plaisteres therof and lay to yor greef. Also take a leaf of mellifolli and
sheepes Tallo beate them together in a mortar very fine and lay
yt to the greef.
Take also Longwoort beate in a mortar very fine, but before yt be
beaten Rost a redd onyon vey tender and beate it therwth  wth a little
safforne and may Butter and so lay yt to the greeff warme
For the emrodes (if they have not as yt weare Tittes). Take well-
Cressis and stampe them and stew them in a pott  wthowte water
then make a playster therof and lay yt to the place greeved and yt
will helpe yow.
For the Emirodes allso take a thick Brick and make yt Redd hott then
lay yt into a Close stoole. th and powre one yt som good malmse
and lett the patient sitt on the stoole that the smoke may goo
in to the bodie and yt will Cure yow
For the pilles. Take a little Hony of Roses and a littell Ballsaumm
and Temper them together and anointe the place greeved.
Take an onyon and Cutt onto the Core and put therin a little
pepper and saulte and so Roste yt very tender then lay yt to
the place greeved as hott as you Can suffer yt: a speciall mede-
sine.
Take mullet Leaues and Elder Leaues and stamp them very fine
wth fresh Butter. Seeth them very well together and straine
yt and so reserve yt for yowr vse. And when yow haue neede
to vse yt put vpp the pilles in to yor body wth som of the said
oyntment and lay a fine Cloth vnto yt som of the said ointment
and let yt so Remayne all night.
Also the ffilth of ones nose or flewme that one spittes so vsed
is very good to ease them and driue them away.
For the pilles before they bleede, Take the strongest oyle of
Lineseede yow Can geate the wch yow shall know to be good by smelling
to yt for yt will fume vpp in to yor head and make your eyes water

Good medicines for the woormes in
Chilldren or owld fowlkes.

Take pewre Steele and file yt as small as yow Can by any meanes
possible, then geeve therof to the party greeved to drinke in any Liquor
beere or wyne and lett them fast after yt. and vse now and then
to geue yt to free them from the woormes.

Also take petrolium that peetrioyl and annointe the stomack and
belly abowte the naule often times therwth, but yt is a more pretious
remedy to drincke ij or iij dro droppes of the said oyle in beere
Ale or wine and yt will presenntly Cure yow, to a small Childe one
or ij droppes may be ssuffitient.

Of these twoo last medicines for the woormes I pray aske
aske advice of Som phyisition before yow admister them all-

thowgh they are Comended and have bine appoved to be
most exellent medicines for woormes in owld folkes or
Children:

Espetiall good for ffistulas Cankeres Sore brestes
and any other malladies.

Take half a pounde of pewre ^Cleere & whyt Turpantine and if yt be not pure
yt will not be whyte, wash yt vntill yt be whyt, and then take ij
or iij yolkes of new laid henn eggs and sturr them well together, then put therin iij
or iiij spoonefulles of wheate meale or flowre and mix them well
together then haue redy the herbes following./ Sage, English Tobacko,
Dittaunder, Planten, Sowthernwood, Saincle, S: Johnswoorte: or any good
healing herb that yow will, of each of them the like quantity (exiep ^ except of
Sage and of that the moste, according to the quantitie of yor other
stuff./ But if the wounde be Cancred or affected to any hott humor
Then to kill that yow must put in some Rew or woormwod, but less of
that then of any of the reste, yow must shred all these herbes very
small, or beate them in a mortar then putt them to the other thing &
put therin a asmuch of the oyl before written ^ in fo: (81) as will make yt like an
oyle a salve or powltes, then spred yt on a Cloth like a powltes and lay
yt to the sores. Changeing yt Twyce a day./ This hath done very
strange Cures for the diseases beforsaide but yow must make yowr
powltes as yow spende yt according to this proportion and whate herbes
yow Canot geate in the winter yow must gether in the somer and dry
them in a Roome where the Son may not Com att them, for that
taketh away the vertw from them beeing once gathered. dittaunder is a
Herbe especiall for the drawing and and Clensing of wowndes./

ffor the heate in the face./ +

Take one ownce of Allome, one ownce of Coppras, one ownce of Brimstone,

+ A drink for the face.

Take iiij handfulles of wylde Tanzy boyle them in ij gallones of
small woorte, then lett yt stand vntill yt be allmost Cowlde, then
put to yt a little Barme and let yt woorke, and when yt is Ripe
put yt in a Cloce vessell, and when yt hath stoode iij or iiij dayes
yow may drinke yt every day twyse all the whyle yow vse the water.

A water to desolue swellin in the bowelles
and superflewitie of fatt./

Take ij gallones of Running water, ijli of Red ffenell, ij ounces of
English Aquauite and a quantitie of Cumine seede, and boyle yt
to one gallon or less of water, and drinke this evry day fasting
for the space of xxti dayes, and the greatnes will waste of the
superflewitie of humores and grosenes./

A water for sores.

Take ij hanfulles of Rosmary, of the leaues of Sage, of wyld
Brier leaues, of woodbine leaues, of lauender fowers, of Elder
leaues, of each ij handfulles, a quarter of a poundof allom, iij qters
of Running water, and boyle yt till yt Com to a qte, wth iij
spoones full of hony, and when yow take yt of the fier put in
one ownce of vnguentum Camfiratun, and stur yt well together,
when yow dress any sore wth yt, yow must shake yt well, and put a little
in a saucer and so warme yt and wash any sore or swelling.

ffor the inflamacon of the Lyver.

Take Sorell, pond yt and straine yt and dipp a good dele of
flax in yt, make yt lyke a playster the length and bredth of
yor hand, then lay yt on yor Right syde, but  not to neere
the stomach for Cooling yt too much./

A water for the Lyver and burning ffeaure

Take Hartstong, Liuer woorte, ^& mayden heare, dist?ill these
together, and vse to drinke therof and yt will purge and pasifie
yor Liuer and quenche the burning of any Agu./

ffor one that Canot brooke his meate

Take woormwoode and mites, and stampe them together wth

ffor watering eyes./

Take the ioyle of Planten and Rose water, and drop the

ffor Conseption

Take Cokele; mirth, wth frankensence, and Safforne, and lett
them Seeth in wyne or water, and if a wooman be anoynted
therwth, yt opneth the narownes of the matrix, and maketh her
apte to Conception./

Make a pessarie of black hellibore. nigella Romana Scamonie

A spetiall medicine for the Rednes of the Face or nose

Tak a pinte of whyte wyne that willnot Change Colowre, a peny
worth wayght of mercury, vj spoonfulles of the ioyse of Lemonds not strained
but Crushed owte, iij peny waight of fine sugar: shake all these
together very well and put in a pewter Cupp, and sett yt in the
imberes xxiiij howeres, then put ^ it into a glass, and shake yt evry day
for a month, add vnto yt one dram of Camphire, and so vse yt
morning and Eving./

For a stich or plurisie in a childe

First take the flowres of dill, mellilote flowres, Colliander seedes,
Anisseede, and Fenell seede, and sithe all this ^in water vntill yt be very
strong, and then put the water in Bladderes, and ty the mowthes
of them Close, and lay yt to the side of the Child, or where
the paine is, as hott as yt may be suffered, and Change yt
fresh and fresh as yow se Cause, and This will help:

A medicine for one that hath a stripe in
the eye or Bruse in the face.

Take the papp of a Black Rotten Apple, and the yolke of an
Egg, and half a spoonefull of Sallett oyle or oyle of Roses,
and put to yt a spvonefull of Red Rose water, and stirr them &
beate them all together, and when yow: haue done, take as
much Croomes of Manchett as will make yt as thick as
yt will spread vppon a Cloth like salue, then lay yt vppon
the sore where the bruse is, if yt be in the eye let them
winke hard, and lett the plaister be laid on the owtside
of the eye.

To Recou eysight.

Take eybright dry yt and beate yt to poulder, and serce
yt fine, then take iij spoonefulles of very Cleane water
and put therto a spoonefull of the powlder and so drinke
yt fasting and fast ij howeres after.

A medicine for the swelling of the Codd

Take a greate Bundle of greene henbane Leaues and
Rost them vnder the imberes, and when they are Rosted,
pound them and draw them throwgh a hearne strainer,
then mingle wth them som oyle of Roses or .........Lay this
powltis to the pained place, and yt shall asswage the swelling
and alay the paine presently.

For the swelling in the belly eyther by

Take ij. or iij hanfulles of Hemlock wash them Cleane then

Rest, then take all these and put them in to iij quart of strong

and anoynt the belly, in the morning all ou befor the fier as

att night. Then when yow goe to bed lay som of the powltis on a

for heate in the fface.

A very good medicin for sore eyes that
Cometh by Rume

Take a pottell of faire Runing water, and put therin as much Camfire
as a frensh Beane, and asmuch whyt Coppras as a little wall nutt, then take ij
Caliminaris stones of a pound waight apece, & heate them very hott
in the fier one after the other, and so quench them ix. or x. times
in the water wth the other thing. after straine yt and Reserv yt
for yor vse, when yow neede therof dropp ^it in to the eye wth a cleane
ffether and ly on yor back for a tyme.

= X To make a washing glister.

Take of Rew, Camomile, malowse, grondsillie, of each on handfull
of Aniseed, fenell seed, brused of each half a handfull, boyle all
in a quart of water to a pinte, put a little Coloquintida tyed hard
in a peece of Linnen Cloth in to the water whyle yt boyleth, then
straine that liquor hard to a pinte, and put to that pint one
spoonefull of Hony, as much Butter or sallett oyle, and a spoone
full of Sawlte. geev yt but warme and no hotter yf yow will
haue yt more purgatiue add vnto yt vjd waight of aloafe in
pouder.

ffor the stay of the pt falling downe

The making of yor broth for a weake back.

Take of the greate ffearne Rootes: of the greene partes therof
brused one handfull, of herb-toopence and medow sweete of each half
a handfull, of Lyones foote half a handfull, of allmond brused a
handfull, of Resones Cut, and of prunes, of each a handfull : boyle all
wth hallf a rack of Muttne Chopped small, or wth a Chick in iij quart
of water to iij pint, then take all the meat herbs and ffrute
owte of the broth and pound them in a wooden platter all together
then put all againe into the pott and lett yt boyle .v. or .vj. howers
warmes more, then take yt of and straine the broth Cleane and keepe
yt to yor vse.
Take of this broth a small mess warmed evry morning and evning ij
howeres before Supper. vse yt now and then att yor plesure

A very good poultis to kill ffellons, venomouse humors, and at the first good
to lay to ould Cacered sores

Take a potle of the dregg of Ale, of the youngest Herb Grace (gethered after the
dew is gone) ij handfulles, of the yongest Sage one handfull, Chopp all small and
boyle them in the dregg of the Ale vntill half therof be consumed then take iij
qters of a pound of sweete Mutton suett very finely minsed and put that to the
rest and so boile them all toger againe and if it be not thick enough to Stick put therein
a spoonefull of wheate bran and so boile them againe and when they ar well boilled strik
it on a Cloth and lay it to the place grived as hott as it may be siffered.

A good powltes to Lay to a sore Brest or on any swelling
or Riseing or any places that bee Red or angry

Take a quantitie of Red Corrant, pick them and rubb them well wth
a Cloth but doe not wash them and then beate them in a mortar to one
substance, and then put to them such a quantitie of the best and sweetest
sallett oyle well tempered together as will amke the Corrantes moiste
and fitt to be spred on a Clothe and so lay som of it on the place greeved
so hot as may be endured

=+ A good powltes to desolue a Swelling orelse to Ripen & breke it

Take a quantitie of New Milke, one handfull of whyt Lilly root clean piked
and wyped but not washed beate them very small beate them very sm and putt them in to the Milk
and so boyle them very well together vntill the root bee like papp cotiually
sturring them for feare of burning and in the boileing put therien ij handfulls
of the flowers of Malouce Cleane piked from the greene huskes and if yow
Canot geate the flowers, then a handfull of the yongest Malow leaves shrid
very small, so lett them boile all together vntill they grow very thick, then
put in som grated bred and so lett them boyle all together vntill it be so thick
that it may be spred on a Cloth and so lay it to the place greeved so warm
as maybe endured Changeing it twice a day

= + A most exellent oyle very sovreigne for a

well proued

Take Foote oyle. 3. pint, yellow wax a quarter of a pinte, May buter
vnwashte one pound, Barowes grece half a pound Aquavite one pinte
an oxe or kowes gall (but in any cace no Bulles gall) Black sope
a quarter of a pound, water-cressis half a handfull. Malouse,
woormwood, and valerian, of each. 2. handfulles. Chamomile. 3. handfulles,
Chickweede, wallwoorte, Strawberg leaues, Rose leaues, Hunny sukles
Shepards pouch, and Rewe of eache. 3. handfulles. dill, Broome toppes,
the firste budding Leaues of Elder (that are of a reddish Colore) of each
4 handfulles: Lauender-cotton, parsly, Groundsill, waybrode, redd croppes
of Brambles, Thime Bay-leaves, of eache one handfull toppes of Rosmary
vnblowne half a handfull. Shrid those herbes small and boyle all on a
gentle fier vntill the ioice of the herbes be boyled owte and they look
dryish then wring them owte harde, and when the oyle is neere Cowld if
there be any dragges in the bottome take the oyle from yt and put toe ye
the same oyle, oyle of Bayes, oy of Camomill, of dill, of populia, of excet
of eache. 2. ownces. Quicksilluer elaid in the pallme of yor hand wth
fasting spittle (ever stiring it wth yor finger vntill it be inkorporate wth
the Spittle) 4 peny woorth one pint more of aqua vite, Stire all
those together and heate yt well and straine yt throw a cloth in to the
vessell yow wowld keepe yt in, the wch must keepe close stopte for  yor  vse.
When yow use yt by a fier wth yor hand anointe and Chafe yt well in
to the place greevid, so bind a linen cloth aboute yt. yf yow make a gret
quantitie to keepe yt long after. 2. years renew yt thus. Take e
black grounds from the pure oyle and put to the oyle mor aquavite
abating in a pinte so much in pporcon as is spent of the oyle warme
yt well together and so put yt vpp againe.

= + A water to heale any wownde that is not mortall
and any owlde vlcer proouid:

Take a quarter of a pound of Bollarmoniak., Camphire an ownce, white
Coppras a quarter of a pound. make it yor Camphire and Coppras in to
fine poulder and put them in an erthen dish well and smoothe leddid
or into a Silluer dish & put that dish on a Chafing dish & Coles.
yow shall see yt presently seethe and pople desoluing as yt were to water
and assoone hardne againe, yow must ever keepe stirring of it wth a spoone
or splatter (whch is better) of Silluer vntill it be so dry that yt may
be made in to fine poulder, then take yt from the fier and make yor
Bollarmoniac into powlder and beate both the Boll and the other in a morter
together into very fine poulder keepe this for yor vse in a bledder or in
a Cloce Box
When yow haue occation to make the water. Take a pottell of faire
Running water putt yt on the fier in a pipkine vntill yt beegin to
boile then take yt from the fier and putt to yt 2. spoones full
of yor powlder stiring yt wth a faire sticke untill yt be could
then put yt in to a glass & stay yt Cloce & shake yt twise or
thrice a weeke to stur the groundes of yt.
The vse of yt is thus, Take according to the wownds gretenes, som of
some of this water when yt is Cleere, warm yt in a Sawser on the
Fier as warme as yow Can suffer yt, bath the greeff well wth

a linen cloth wett in it, then dubble that Cloth vj. or viij times wett

bind yt upp xi. or xii. howres, after dress yt againe, Continue yt so
untill yt be whole/ Take heed yow vse yt not whe yt is trowbled
for if there be any grounds in it, it will breede prowd and dead flesh

A very good medisine for the Gowte

Take viij Eggs and bete them to oyle then temper them wth a pound of
black sope very well that yow may not deserne the one from the other, then
spred yt on lether and bind the place greevid all ouer and keepe yt on
viij dayes wthowte opning it att all.

A good medisine for A rupture.

Take the rootes of the herb Called Solomon seale and pound it and straine
it and straine it wth any liquor yow will and geeve to drink of this when the gref
is vpp in the place it owght to be and bind of the same root powdred to the
greevid parte

A good medisine for the falling sicknes.

Take the after birth of a man Child for wooman and the contrary for
a man dye this and drinke it in any Liquor. yt must bee of the first
Child of a wooman.
Mrs. Winefred Slurton

Take a quantity of Barly meale and beate it very small, then take
asmuch of the Cloues of Garlick and peele them and stamp them very small
and boile both together very well in Clarett wine vineger vntill it Come to
a poultes, then spred it on Lint and lay it to the greef the Cowld taken of
three times att the least and yt will kill the Canker and take away proud
flesh and dead flesh and the Cancer in a woomans brest.

In the Canker water pelitory of Spaine, Salendine, Sinkfoile
are prinsipall herbes wth the rest of the things the pellitory first to bee
brused and after yt is sodden then powr the liquor from the herbes and
take a parte therof and asmuch vineger and boyle them together a while
wth a handfull of Redd rose leaues, then put in some hony and seeth it a
wallme or tow and so applie yt. If the sore be very much festred then
vse Smithes water to make the water in steede of faire water and seeth
therin som Barly to corect the water. if yt be in sore mowthes to Rubb it till
it bleede. The Canker water not to be used vntill the powltes bee
lefte, for the powltes killes the Canker, and the water healith it.
Som healing Sallue to be spredd on a plaister to keepe on the Linte
that hath the powltis.

and straine it w 4. 5. or 6. spoonefulles of Sack and drinke the same blud
warme, and then take the same erbs that is so strained and put therton 2. or 3.
dropes of sallett oyle and spred the same on a blew Cloth and lay it on yor
nauell.

Make a pint of Cleane possett Ale and boile therein a good handfull of potsmother

To strengthen a weake Back

Take a pint of Muskadine wth a handfull of Hzl Hazl Nutt Cernells & blanch
them as yow doe Allmond then stamp them and straine them as yow doe Allmond milke
then take .2. or . 3. Capp dates, stone them and slise them thin and put in one stick
of cinamom and sezn it wth sugar and lett it boyle ou a softe fier the space of a
quarter of an hower continnually stirring it so eate of this in the Morning fastingfasting
and yow shall find of it greate comfort./

A very good Melilote Emplaster

The white Bale.

Take what quantitie of white Chaulke you liste, fower tymes
to much Ceruse, and fower tymes to muche white lead made
all of them into fime powder mingle or mixe them togyther
wth a little freshe hogges licoure and make yt into a rounde Balle
Some put into this myxture, litarge of Silver, some put into
yt the powder of Cristall, or of Alablaster, >somel vse a little
powder of pracipitate in very smale quantitie and that only is
good and the rest are of no value.

for the morphewe

Yf yu take Lytarge of of gould annd Lapis Calaminaris of ech of

one scruple and a halfe, and make them into fyne powder, and

a sponefull of good vinegar and wth oile of roses make yt into
a softe oyntement, yt will helpe that disease very well.

A speciall proved medicine for a pearl pinn and well
flime or other such like sore in the Eyes:

Take Chamberly other wise pisse and put it in a Tubb and when it hath stood so .9.dayes,
Then take a brass Candell-stick mad very cleane especially in the side Then spread
as thick as it will hange on all wthin the inside wth the thickest rawe sweete craime
yow can geate then take the Cadellstick so spred wth Craime and hang it ou the
Chamberly in the tubb so as it may not tuche it, and yet as neere as the Chamberly may
fume vpp to the Craime. And whill the Candlstick doth hang ou the Chamberly yow must
sture it once a day. But so as it doth not  dash on the craime and yow must boath Couer
the tubb wherein the Chamberly is and the Candellstick close to keepe in the ayer
and after the Candelstick hath hanged so .9. dayes the Craime will torn greeneish and
at the .9.th dayes end strike it of the insid of the Candlestik and put it for yor vse in a
Clene glass or gallypott. And when yow vse thereof put a little quantity in the corner
of the eye so as it will run all aboute the patient holding his head accordingly it is
sufficient to use it twice a day.

For the paine in the Head that pseed from could Rewme
yow must make this gargll as followeth

Take a pint of whyt wyne and som 4. or .5.Root of Pellitory of Spaine beeing leane
cleane picked and scraped, Boyle them in the said wyne vntill they bee Consumed and put
thereto a spoonefull of whyt wine vinegar and so reserv it in a glasse for yor vse when
yow find the Rume of yor head offend yow take 3. or .4.spoonefulls and make it blud warm
putting in it one spoonefull of good .........sturring it well together so gargll yor mouthe
wth it spitting the Rume away so fast as it Coms this yow must vse fasting, and when yow doo
vse it Cou yo head ou wth a Sheete solong as yow vse it

To strengthen a weake Back

Take a prety quantity of Amber of the thickest and the yellowest that yow can gete
and beate it to fine poulder and seace it through a peece of fine laune and haue
in a redynes a new laid Egg reare Rosted and put un asmuch of yor searced Amber
as a half nutt sturing it as Sallte and so sup vp the Egg and drink after it a
good draught of Mallmsey wch I hold to be far better then Muskaden this yow
must take only in the morning fasting .3. morning arow./

A very good medcine forth Scuruie

If yow bee not where a hotthowse is sweate in yor bedd by the help of Brick

For the Eyes

Take pellamonten, Time, verbaine, Erbgrace, Red fenell, Red sage, fethersew
Cardus benedittus, Ivy leaves, eybright, whyt thorn leaues in Somer, and
the midell rinde in wintter of each a like quantity and shridd them small, then
put therein a spoonefull of bay salte, more or lesse, anording to the quantity
of yor herbs then stamp them all together and strayne owte the Ioice and keepe
it for yor vse and wth a spoone or feather dropp a little thereof in yor Eyes once
twice, or thrice a day according as the baddnes of yo eyes bee lying vpright
on yor back.

For the flux or Lask:

Take of the Curnalls of Galls that make ynke, of each lyke quantity ^or of the
galles the moste beate them very small and searce them throw a fine peece of
laune then wth hony make pilles of it and so take iij att a tyme iij tymes a
day as bigg as pease and fast after them, so take them  iij times a day vntill
yow bee well or more often if yow can. one gall is enough for one tyme to
make iij pills if yow will yow may drink a litle Milk after the pilles But it is
bett or wthoute.
Long Pepper boyled in Milk is very good and Planten sodd in in possett Ale
and drink nothing else, and so Cinamond, sod in water, also the inice of Plante
and aquavite is very good.

An aproved medicine to kill any Tetter in the Face and
To take away any Reddnes and make the Face cleere

Take a good quantitie of Elder blossoms when they are mose owte and put
to them so much of the best whyt wne as the glasse will Containe that you put
the blossoms in then stopp the glasse Closse and sett it all the Somer after in
the Sonne a sonning and when yow vse it wash yor face therewth every night
when yow goe to bedd and if yow finde it to sharp or eger then put therevnto
a litle whyt wyne as yow vse it so quallifie the sharpnes thereof

To try whether Amber Greece bee good

Throw it hard in to the water if it swime it is good, if it Sinke it is naught
also if yow lay a little of it on a burninge Charrcole if it Consume quit it is
good, but if it leaue any Cole or substace on the Charcole it is then naught

To try Muske and to restore the cent of it

Take a litle thereof and put vppon the end of yor tonge and if it melt & consums
then it is good otherwyse naught and if yow put one dropp of stale vrine in to it or
hang it ou an ould priuie house vaute it will bring the smell againe

To try Bezarstone

Take it and weet it a littl wth a Cleane Cloth and then gently Rubb it, if the shining
of it weare away and fowleth the Cloth it is then naught, otherwise if the Cloth
Continues Cleane it is good also put it in to water if dissolues or breake it is mac
naught other wise good if it hould.

A very good Cwlister that any woman lying in or Childe
may take

Take a quantity of new milke from the Cow and put therein a good quatity
of chamomille but more or lesse as the patiente body is hot or Colld for Caomill
is hott, a quantity of Malous, som Aniseed, fenell seede and ????? seede brused and
parsly seede if the patient be trowbled wth the stone, then boyle all well together
vntill a leaste half bee consumed then take it from the fier and put therein a good
quantity of Comine seed fine beaten and a good quantitie of the blackest sugar
yow can geate and sturr them well together and straine it/ when it is strained
and well wrong that the strength of the Comine seede may ome in to the liquor
(but wthout boyleing) then put in iij or iiij sppoonefulls of the purest sallett oyle
and of yow will haue it weake then better iij spoonefulls of siropp of Red poses
sture it well together and apply it fittly warme

ffor Sore eyes

Take Rose water, ffenell water, Cleane water, of whyt wyne, of each one
spoonefull mix then and dropp it in to yor eyes.







96  A medisine for the Woorms pooved and
especially in yong Chilldren

Take ij or iij handfulls of Alehooffe or otherwise Called hay maids and
byle it in the strongest Crabb verioyse that yow Can geate till the Herbs
bee tender, then Croome in some Croomes of levened wheten Bred
and wringe it hard betweene twoo trenchars and put it into a fine
linnen bagg and binde it first day to the mouth of the stomack as hott
as it canbe Sufferred and the next day so the Navell, and so Continue
it to each place iij or iiij dayes a peece, and as often as it grows colld
it must still be heate anew in the said liquor./

For the Coming oute of the fundament
or the Piles

Take a good handfull of Mullett leaves and boyle them in a quantity
of Milke vntill half be Consumed then take owte the Leavs and wring
owte the Millke wth yor hand, and wth  the Leaves put vpp the fundamt
very gntly and so lett them lye to the place one hower together and
after as often as there is occation doe the like againe and it will in short
time kill the disease, it is also exellent good to Cure & ease the piles to
be vsed in the same Maner. This Mullett is a long leafe a q of a yard
long and a thick horey leafe and of a whytish greene Coloure and
somwhate Rugh it is Called in som places Torchlyght or  Leggtaper

A very good powlder for the throte

Take Roche Allom and put it in a greate oyster shell and sett ^it on very
hott Coles and lett it there seethe thill it leave heaving in the shell
then itt wilbe hard beate it to poulder and put there to a little fine
meale and heaue the pallett of yor Mouth softly wth it beeinge Beetweene your
thumbe, and if there be any vlser in yor mouth or hole eaten wth a
Canker lay of this poulder vppon it. Pbatum est.

For the Laske or loosenes or other fluxe after
losse of a Childe.

Take a handfull of Redd oke bud, one handfull of Planten, wood-
roofes, Sinamond, Pomgranett Rynd, of eatch one handfull, stripe

yo  may  drinke .3. or .4.  spoones full of this at a tyme warme wth
Sugar yow may drinke it wth  muskadine or burnt Clarrett wyne

A very speciall Receipt for a Consumption

Take ij quart of the strongest woort yow Cann make, one quarte of
the best Englishe Hony, sett it ou the fiere in a pott or skillett and
when it byleth skoome it very Cleane, that dune put in to it one
handfull of Lyverwoort, us much of Harts tongue and lett them boile
all together untill ij part there of bee Consumed not forgetting to
stirr it so somtimes in the boyleing. then take it of and putt it in a Glasse
or pott this vse morning and Evening and at other tymes is yow please
pbatum est.

=+A medicine for the Throte

liquor and rubb the place geeved, and then open yor mouth and there
will Rune oute the Cause of yor greeffe.

A medicine to Clense and Comfort the
Rains and inward part of the body in
generall

Take a gallon of faire Runing water and put therein a pownd of
Eringo Root Cleane skraped and the pith taken oute, ij ounces of
Liquoras Clene skraped and brused, a quarter of a pound of Reisons of
the sonn the stones taken oute, ij. ounces of Aniseede Cleane picked
boyle all these together on a soft fier vntill the half bee Consumed
and ever in the boyleing scoome it very Cleane, then take it from
the fier and straine it and for a Costiue body haue redy one ownce of
Sceene and put the same in a cleane Cloth and lay it into the drink
whyle it is hwott and drinke thereof a good draught Cowld morning
fasting and in the after noone ij howers beefore supper. ffor Solible
body vse no Sceene at all.   Mrs E.G

An appoued drinke for the Laske or the Flux

Take a pottell of Milke and make posset drink ther of wth stale Ale or
Beere then take away the Crud very Cleane and then take of Planten
Sheppards pursse or pouch and krott knott grasse of eatch half a good
handfull putt it in the possett drink and lett it boyle after it is in a
good whyle then straine it and put it in to an earthen pott or
Cupp and vse it as yor ordinary drinke exept it be at meate, then take
a Cupp of drinke wth a toste./

The foote Plaister

Take Stonepitch such as is drunke for falles and bruses ij ounces Rosine
and wax of eache one ounce, black Rowles Called Coulmans half on ounce
oyle of Camomyle, of dill, and of whyt Aysell, that is of Elder leaves and

asmuch as a good wallnutt, Cloues and Nutmegg in fine powlder of each
half a spoonefull. Boyle these together at a softe fier and stirr them Con-
tinually till it be Cowld make the plaister according to yor shoo sole, Bathe
the feete in water and som sweete herbes boyled together and weare the
plaister, yt taketh away both hedach and tuthache of Cowld Cawse all stiches
in the syde or in the hippes that pseede of wett or Cowlde and seaseth the
Cowld fitt in all shakeing Agues. yf the plaister bee to thick put more
oyle in it if to thine put lesse oyle in it at yor owne discression

=+ A Vomitt

Take Antimonye and beate it into pouder, Then take twoe pipkins
the toppes beinge both of a bignes And make a furnace in the Corner of
a Chimney, and Ioyne the mouthes of the pipkins togither and with a very
hott fyer of Chearcooles the powder beinge putt into the backce one the
vommitt will Come into the vtter one, and hange on the inside of It
like very fine flower, And alwayes with a feather swepe it into a
paper, then putt in more.

Take the quantitie of seven graines of this vommitt in a penny
pott of white wyne, And lett the patient drinke a good draught
of pottage an hower before he taketh It, and when you would
have It Cease Take a draught of mace ale, proved by Iohn
Bouchier,

=+ Iohn Bouchiers pill good for
the stomacke

Take of masticke halfe an unce, of Agaricke three drammes, of
the best aloes tenne drammes, powder them as fine as may bee
and Searce them, temper them with oximell simplex or melrosarum
make it stiffe Inough to roule vpp in pilles, Take one or twoe
pilles of this masse halfe an hower before supper, but mingle
with them a droppe or two of oyle of tyme.

Iohn Bouchiers makinge oyle
of tartare

Take apound or two of the best tartar, Calcyne ut in a Crucible
then lay it vpon a marble stone, but forst beat it grosly, sett the
stone somewhat a tilt in a Cellar, and lay sand or gravill vnder
the frame wheron you sett your stone with the tartar, and sett
a Receiver vnder It, and the oyle will droppe into It;

=+ A brothe to make one fatt,

Take of Chyna Rootes two ounces slice them thine, and stipe them
in two pyntes of water all night vntill they Colour the water reed
whiche must be donne by settinge it very Close Covered in the hotte
imbers all night, take that infusion, And make your brothe with
a Chicken and a peece of mutton with liverworte, maydenheare
egremoney and time, with a handfull of Reysons of the Sonne
stoned, six dates, and a flake or two of mace, A bottome of a
manchet with out any other thickeninges, when it is boyled
streine It, drinke halfe a pinte early in the morninge and
as much at fower of the Clocke in the afternoone, Continue
it a monthe, It norisheth muche, increaseth good bloud and
openeth obstructions, it is good for all thinge hurtefull to noe
thinge, If you adde to It the Rootes of parcely, fenell and
suckery It is the better, you must not boyle the Chyna Rootes
in the broothe, onely infuse them in the water

The makinge of metheglion

Take fenill Rootes, parcely Rootes, suckery Rootes, saxifrage
rootes, any other rootes as you thinke good, A handfull of Rosmary
to some six gallons of water, make your detoxion strongeof
the Rootes, Then lett it settell after you have streined It

purged It,

Two excellent wayes to amend the skinne
graine of the face well approved.

1 Take a quarte of Cowes milke, the yealke of three egges, and
soe muche finr beane flower as will make a past, of the whiche
make littell loves, And bake them not to much, Then take one
of your loves, And Crumble it into Cowes milke, And lett it
soake all night therin, then in the morninge before you vse It
wipe your face with a drye Clothe, then washe it well with the
milke and bread, The vse wherof will make pier Clear and
white

2 Also limonds sliced and laid in butter milke doth make perfect fayer

But a third whiche is most excellent to make
the face fayer and Cleane vsed by the fayrest
gentelwomen in england is this

Take a good quantity of the marrowe of Swines feete and A
quantity of Cowes milke, a littell Rose water, mingell them
well togither, then breathe on a lynnen Clothe or on a peece
of scarlette, Rubbe your face therwith well, then anoynt your
face therwith luke warme, It will make the face wounderfull
Clear and fayer/

A Cearcloth or maske for the face

Take whitt wax, and washe it well in the distilled water of  Culthfoote
eight or nyne times, then take sperma Ceti washed after the same
manner, then melt them both togither, and washe them againe three or
fower times in rose water, then melte them againe vpon a littell





100
fier in a pewter dishe, and having maskes cutt out of lynnen
Clothe, dippe them in and make Ceare clothes to lyne maskes,
lett the wax and sperna Ceti be equall in quantity

A Receipt for the makinge of an oyle to Cure any
greene wounde made with Sworde dagger knife
or edge toole, wher noe bone is broken as also for
all hurtes by shott, It will also heale an ould vlcer

Take three ponndes and a halfe of the very best oyle olive you Cann
gett for money, and provide three yonge whelpes lyvinge male
or female all is one so they bee vnder nyne days ould , the blacker
the better, then take of ordinary great wormes suche as Creepe
owt in the night a Competent noumber, And putt them into a fine
Seeve, and powre water on them still to make them scowre and
Clense them selves, And when you see them Cast any white
frothe, wipe of the same still, then when you shall find them to
bee very Cleane putt them into a pott or full quarte of white
wine, and ther lett them lye till the next morninge, And then putt
them into a second quarte of white wyne, Ans soe the third
morninge, to the ende all the earthe within them may be Clensed
Then putt the last wine with the wormes, the oyle, and the
whelpes lyvinge into a Kettell, and lett them boyle gently for A
good space over the fier, Then take out the whelpes and mangell
them into gobbettes boanes and all, And putt them into the same
liquor againe, to boyle so longe till you may find the wyne to
be quite Consumed, whiche you shall perceve by puttinge A
smale sticke of fier therin, for if the liquor burne It is A
signe that the oyle holdeth his full power with out checke
Then take of the leaves of nicotiana (Whiche is the leaves of
great tobacco) a good quantity bruse them (and havinge first
strained the former liquor throwe a lynen Clothe) Infuse
the Iuce of the same yerbe therwith beinge in the like sorte
distreined ther into. Then havinge so mingled all the former
ingredientes putt the same into a bottle & therto adde the fower
powders vid pulvis thuris, pulvis masis, pulvis sarcacolla
and pulvis Aloes, And when you have occasion to vse the
same for a shotte, Take onely a lynen Ragge and out of the
bottle lett droppe a little of the oyle into A saucer and make
the same warme, And dippe your Ragge therin, and lay
the same vpon the the wounde with out any tente or other
thinge therin vsed, save onely a thyne plaister to keepe It
fast and soe binding It vppe dresse it once a day.

for a wound by sworde or any other edge toole take a peece of
blacke wooll pulled from the backe, necke or ratherer the Coddes
of a blacke Ramme onely carded without washinge, And
dippe in the same oyle and lay it onely on the wounde and
dresse It oute in fower and twenty howers with a thinne Cleaving
plaister as Basilicon or the like to keepe the woolle on, And
were the wounde through the body so as none of the noble partes
bee offended, the party shall (by gods grace) Recover in nyne
dayes, If you feare any inflamacion aplye a cataplasmus
of Coolinge hearbes, now you must know that if the hurte bee
in the head they never vse this oyle,

This oyle will keepe tenne yeres and the elder the better, and
the woolle dipped and laid by is the better, the newer the oyle
is the longer the wonnde is in curinge.

A proved medicen for the Greene sicknes
and helpeth the Survey.

Take mace one ouce nutmegges halfe an ounce, cloves halfe
an ounce, the Cernels of Ashe keyes fortie , Amber white one
dramme, Corall one drame, pearle a quarter of an ounce
Tarttar two drams, Beate all theis into fine powder then adde to
It steele that is filed very smale or steele prepared one Ounce and
a halfe, fine suger halfe a ponnde or more as you like the
taste , take the wight of six pence in the morninge fastinge
and fast two or three howers after It, the like quantity at night
last to beddeward, and presently after the takinge to walke or
doe some exercise, and to absteine from all frutes, nuttes
Cheese, milke, fishe, and salte meates.

To Cuer pine and webbe
or peale in the eye

Take burnt allam, the finest white suger you Cann gett
take of theis two a little quantity, make them in as fine powder
as may bee , it is best to beat them severally, and mixt them
well togither, It must be made freshe every three or fower
dayes, and kept very drye, The vse of it is to have the
partie greeved to sitt downe low and hould his eye open
and putt in asumuche as you may resonably take vppe bewene
your finger and thumbe

A most excellent and precious oyle or
balsam whiche Cureth all woundes in a very
shorte time, It is also good for all paines
for the Crampe, stiffe members, shrunke sinowes
and many more deseases of mans body
it is approved to bee good

Take of freshe and sweete oyle of olives tenne ponndes, white
wyne one ponnde boyle theis togither till the wine bee Consumed
lett it Coole, and putt it into a stone potte that is bigg enough.
then adde vnto It of the flowers of Rosemary one ponnd and
a halfe, of lignum aloes three Ounces, of olibanum, of Bdellium
of eche five ounces, then stoppe your potte well with Corcke.
pitche and bledders, and burye it in the earthe about the beginninge
of august, there lett it Remayne about halfe a yere then take
it owte and putt in the followinge thinges,
Take of sage, Rosemary, Betoney, Rue, yarow, of the Rootes
of Consolida maior, of the hearbes of viticella or ballsamina
mas Called in englishe Balsam apple is an Italian plante and
rare in any other Country if beareth a Red frute, here is ment
the leafe but in defect of this I should Councell to take of
Balsamite saemina in english madelyn, of the flowers of tapsus
barbatus of eche three handfuls, of gallinga, Cloves Nutmegges
Spica nardi, saffron of eche one ounce, of Aloes
epatica, Rozine of the pine of eche fower ounces, of Calophonia
halfe a pound, of the toppes with the Seedes of Hipericum or St
Iohn woorte one pound, of muske halfe a drame, of yelowe
waxe, of Hogges greace of eche nyne ounces, but in steede
of the two last it is better to take oyle of wax and mans greace

The hearbes you shall Cutt small and stampe them, powder the
rest of the thinges, and putt them all vnto the oyle, mingell them well
and sett them all the Somer in the Sonne, then boyle them till the
Hearbes be drye, And straine the oyle out strongely and putt in





Some of the Artifitiall balsam the quantity of tenne Ounces, And
in the monthe of september putt in of the frute of Balsamina
when it is Red a pound, And then is your balsam endid, keepe
it Close stopt for your vse

This balsamina is the viticella or balsam apte before before spoken
of,  here you must take the fruit when it is Ripe and Redde,
in defect of this I thinke it were not amisse to take of the madeline
with the flowers and seedes,

In my opinion it were not a misse to adde vnto this balsam either
the Gume Elemin, or else the oyle of Gume Elemin distilled, If
the Gume it must bee putt in with the other gumes to the quantity
of fower Ounces, If the oyle it must  bee putt in when you putt
in your artificiall balsam to the quantity of two ounces, This
Gume Elemin or the oyle is most precious in Curinge of woundes
and against all the former Resited deseases.

This is the composision of the artificiall balsam

Take of venis turpentine a pound of perfect oyle of bayes
fewer ounces, of Galbanum three Ounces, of Gume Arabick
fower ounces, of lignum aloes, Galinga, Cloves, Consolida
maior, Cinamon, nutmegges, hedoaria, ginger, diptanum album
album of eche one ounce, of olibanum, myrra elected, Gume hedera
of eche thre ounces, of the best muske and amber greete of eche
one drame of rectified Aqua Vite six poundes putt them altogither
in a pott, and lett them stand seven dayes well stopped then distill
them in a glasse Retort in Sand, the first that Cometh over is A
white water mingled with oyle, and thus Contniue your fier
smale so longe as It distilleth, then increase your fier, and ther
will Come a blacke oyle, then putt to your Retort one other
Receiver, increasinge your fier according to arte vntill It
droppe noe more, then seperat the blacke oyle from the blacke
water, and the white oyle from the white water whiche Came
first over, The blacke oyle is the artificiall balsam wiche is
good for the aforesaid disease aforenamed.

The first water is good to Cleene the eye sight and preserve
them, the face beinge washed therwith maketh It fayre and
younge

The white oyle breaketh the gravell in the kidnes, provoketh vrine
beinge druncke, It Cureth all kind of woundes a clothe beinge
putt therin wetted, It Cureth also sciatica beinge anointed ther-

with,
The blacke water whiche is Called the mother of Balsam Cureth
skabbes in a short time beinge washed therwith, also leprosies
and all sorte of ulcers,

A most excellent balsam for all swellinges bruses
woundes or any other accident of the body

Take of the best Civill oyle one pint make if very warme
on the fier then infuse therin the flowers of St Iohns worte
beinge but budded, the yong buddes of Red roses the nayles or
white endes beinge Cutt, of the flowers of rosemary, the flowers
or younge leaves of wodde bittony of eche of  thos two handfulls
alkenet Rootes brused one Ounce, lett  all theis bee infused
in a large viall well stopped Close, and standinge in the
sonne the Cheefest two monthes of Somer, then streine or
presse very harde the balsam from the simples; putt it in
a vyall Close stopt till you have occasion to vse It, Soe have
you the best balsam in the worlde.

A good medicine for A wenne

Take A wodden ladell & heate it to the fyer & with the
Iuce or froth that Cometh therof annoynt the wenne with
a feather thre times a day betwen the sonne risinge to the
sonne settinge & after it is annoynted gaue in redines
the Crume of Cleane barlye breade well heate & moystened
in vineger & so lay it to powltes wise as whott as you
Can well suffer it & thre mondays or frydayes one after the other in the morninges only, you must quiddle the wenne
in & out with your thumbe & it will weare away.

Severall good medicins to stoppe the fluxe
or any loosnes.

Take a qiantitye of milke from the Cow & boyle therin
some pomegranatt pills & drinke a drawft therof as often
as neade requires.
also take ginger & slice it & Cast it into a Chafinge dishe
of Coales & settit onto a Close stoole & sitt over it &
take the fume into the bodye, this hath often helped when
no other medicine & then the milke neades not.
also take a good quantitye of Cinamon slickes to the
weyght of an vnce & boyle it well in a quarte of fayre
water& drinke often therof & no other drinke.
also take a good lappfull of knott grasse & boyle it
exeedinge well in the Smithes black water that they
heate ther Iron in & when it is well boyled, putt it in
to a bowle or panne & sett it into a Close stoole as whott
as may be & then Sitt vppon the stoole to keape in the
steame so that it may goe vpp into the bodye & so Con-
tinue it as longe as you find any heate & do this
fowre or five dayes together & it will healpe you.
All these medicins haue bine proved.

+= An excelent powltes to kyll a fellon or
to draw out any venemous humor

Take a quarte of the dregges or lyes of Ale, then take
on or two good handfulls of yearbe grace (otherwise
called Rew) gathered after the dew is of it, halfe a
handfull of red sage, Choppe them very smale & putt
them into the dregges of Ale & sett them over a sowfte
fyer & so lett them boyle vntill the halfe be almost Con-
sumed, then take halfe a pownd of motten suett fynely
minced & putt iy thervnto & so lett it boyle vntill it be
so thick fitt for a powltes alwayes sturringe it in the
boylinge, then apply it thicke on a Cloth as warme as
Conveniently Can be endured & keape the greasse warme
with Clothes & vsse it as often as you see Cause.

An Electuarye very good for the weaknes
of the liver stomack or other partes

Take of the Conserues of wormwood Romaine, of red roses
of damasee roses, of Cowslippes, of Violetts, of Rosemarye
flowers, of Ieliflowers, of each of these one vnce, mixe
them all well together, then take the pills of one ourenge
and one lemon & mince them as smale as possible
then mixe them with the Conserues: but of the pills only
take the yellow & very thinne sliced putt therin halfe a
nutmegge of this electuarye eate as much as a nutt-
megge going to bed & risinge in the morninge & a
little before dinner & Supper. Mis Elizabeth Gorges.

To make Sweet wasshinge Balles

Take Castell sope & Cutt into smale peeces, the whiche
melte in a Skillett & putt in a stone mater & lett it
Coole. Then beate it very well & putt therin of storex
of Beniamin of each like quantitye beaten to fine po-
wder & a little muske: olso some rose water & the iuce
of some sweet hearbes; as of margerum, lime, & Isope
with some spices fynely beaten & searced, as Cinamon
nutmegges & Cloves, all these thinges must be added acco-
rdinge to the smell or likinge of the partye that makes
them, when they be well beaten & incorporated together
make them vpp in balles, And if you will you may putt
in some Camphire & some doe vsse Venice sope, which
is whiter & sowfter then Castell sope.

= X Two spetiall proved Waters for the
stone, or a bloudy Water Coming therby

1. When the beane Coddes are greene take out the beanes, And then still
thes greene Coddes, And of that Water so stilled, take six sponefulls
at a time, And slice a littell nutmegge into It, And drincke yt
in the morninge, and as muche more in the afternoone at fower a
Clocke, And when you find the paine, then beginne to vse It, or
when you doute It to prevent It, And vse It a weeke togither,

2. Take hawses when they beginne to turne Redde, And stille them
And when you find paine to beginne, of that water drincke
morninge, and in the afternoone six sponefulls at a time, And
use It two or three dayes togither or as you find your payne to
Continue.

An other very good medicen for the stone

Take Raddishe rootes at the first Comynge and slice them, and
putt them into a double glasse then putt them to them a good
quantity of fine barbary suger, And stoppe the mouthe of the
glasse very Close and hange It in Runnynge water vntill it bee att
disolved into water, then powre it out, And keepe it for your
vse, takinge a sponefull of it at one time in the morninge, And soe
vse to take It as you have occasion,

A receipte to Cure sore eyes that are
trobled with a hotte flux of Rume wherby
many are muche bleare eyed,

Take a pinte of white wine halfe a pint of Redde rose water and
halfe a pinte of Smithes water, putt them in a Chafer, and then
putt therto halfe an Ounce of shumathe beinge brused, two penny
worthe of tutty powder, a handfull of house leeke picked and
washed very Cleane, two handfulls of yellowe docke leaves Rootes
Rose leaves, stirre them all togither, And sett them upon a softe
fier, Then take a very Cleane sticke, and sett It upright therin
And notche it in three severall places that you may knowe when
the third parte is Consumed away, then streine it into a bason
throughe a thinne Clothe, And when it is Coulde Colde putt
it into a glasse groundes and all, When you have Cause to
vse it, power a little out into a sawcer and with a Cleane Ragge
washe your eyes, And if they be very hotte, wett your Clothe
well and lay upon them When you goe to sleepe, And it will
both Coole and Close uppe the Corners and take away the rednes,
If you have occasion to make this in the winter you make may
take dried redde rose leaves,

for the paine in the backe and kidnies

Take a quarte of muscadine, a handfull of the toppes of rosemary
a handfull of the toppes of mother time, some bittoney, some Comfery
a great nutmegge ^grated two of the largest maces, six Cloves boyle them
altogither, and when they are boyled, streyne them twice to the
ende it should bee the Clearer, Then take the yolkes of twoe
new laid egges, and make a Caudell of It, puttinge therto A
quarter of a pound of Refined suger, and halfe a pinte of the
best Rose water, And drinke five sponefulls of It morninge
and eveninge,

for fleame and winde.

Take scabias, bittony, fennell, parsely Rootes, Isoppe, maydenheare

sweete fenell seedes, of eche one ounce, An onyon pilled, Reysons

and boyle them in a quarte of water till the halfe bee
Consumed, and then streine it out very hard and putt vnto It

scumme It as any Scumme doth Rise, and putt vnto It two drames
of safforne grosely beaten a quarter of an hower after it hath
boyled, And soe drincke therof when you find your selfe ill

A good medicine for the Consumption of the
longes or shortnes of breath

Take A Tost of good fine breade & tost it not over browne
& then spred it on both sides with pure honney. then putt
the sayd tost into a drawfte of good beare not over stronge
& eate the tost & then drinke the beare & doe this everye
(or most) morninges & it will restore much breath & Cure the
Consumption, if it be not to far past.

The vertues of Oyle of Amber

It may be Called the most forceable European Balsam
because by the vertue therof it expelleth all other red remidyes
in Curinge the Apoplexie & fallinge sicknes, of old it was
Called the holye oyle & that by the Admirable & secrett force
therof.

In the plauge or peste, the nostrells beinge annoynted with
one droppe at morninge & night, hindereth all venim from
infectinge the harte. If any be alreadye infected let them take
one scruple therof at atime in the water of Cardus Benedictus
It greatly healpeth thos who feareth any dangerous diseases
of the heade, such as is the Apoplexie, palsey, & fallinge
sicknes. ffor if any feareth the former diseases & taketh ther-
of every morninge one droppe in the appropriatt waters, as
Bittonye, Tillia (or linden tree), lavender, black Cheryes,
and such like, he shalbe free from all such infirmityes,
of the like effect or little tabletts of Sugar mixt with
some of this oyle, And if any be trobled with the Apoplexie
fallinge sicknes or palsey, scarse Can ther be founde a
more soveraigne remidye then to vsse some droppes of
this oyle made of white Amber given in an epilepticall
spiritt. It shall olso much avayle to annoynt the neck
& nostrells with the same oyle & for certayne the discease
shalbe removed & they who shall vsse it shall sencibly re-
cover sence & motion: The Epilepticall speritt is made
of lavender & marigowld flowers, infused in the speritt
of wine.

The veynes, neves, sinowes that suffers Contractions Spasmes
& the like are Cured by annoyntinge this oyle theron, olso
mixinge some therof with such oyntments as you vsse to
strenthen the same partes withall.

One or two droppes mixed with parsley water & inwardly
taken expells the stone & other superfluityes of the urinall
passages.

olso halfe or one whole scruple therof mixed with the water
of vervin, muggworte or malmsey and given to a woman
in Childbirth advanceth the birth. olso thre or fowr droppes
given in Cinamon water maketh her labour come forthwth
vnto her. olso if the matrix be misplaced & fume vpp
to the heade annoynt the nostrells & tempells therwith &
it will make it sturr: but if it showld not then stamen
settle in the right place annoynt the navell therwith & it
will settle it: but if it should fall to low at the first
annoynt the upper parte of the matrix & it will rayse
it agayne & settle it.

56 107
It healpeth much Cawld defluxtions of the heade & by the vsse
therof they Consume away & the brayne is restored.
Annoynte the nostrells with some droppes & over the harte
& breast to thos woeme that ar trobled with suffocations, per-
cipitations & strangulations of the mother It doth healpe
the motion of the mother: of the like effect is the tabletts
made afforesayd.

It is very profitable olso in sowndinge beatinge & pantinge
of the harte.

It not only Comforteth the facultye of the vitall vertue of
the harte: but olso of the animale vertue of the brayne &
likewise the naturall vertue of the liver, Therfore in
Concoction & digestion it much avayles.

before the fitt & sweatinge thervppon drives away the

In stoppinge of the water thre or four droppes taken in

It is profytable in dryinge vpp of Catarhes

It Cureth the paynes of the teeth which ariseth from

In the Suffocation of the mother, you must take it in

In stoppinge of the monthly Cowrses seaven or eyght

In Contractions A certayne person was Cured in a

In vomitinge of Blood the droppes given in Cowltsfoote

It Cures the vertigoe or giddines of the heade.
It marvelously removes the stupiditye of the brayne.
It Comfortes the sight with fenell water.
It is very profitable in the stitch of the side. The quan-
