A good meanes to helpe and Cure the
eatinge or gurmontizinge evill

ffirst take and purge the stomacke excedinge well with a fittinge
and good vomite (for the disease Cometh and procedeth by a Raw
sower humor feedinge the stomacke) then make all the meate
the partie eateth with Currantes and good store, and for the most
parte if most of his foode were Currantes onely It were the
better, for It will the better, and soner Cuer him, but Currants
is the principall means , for they are hard of disgestion and
the disease Cannot worke on them or gett mastery, and the
nutrament they breed is sweete and tourneth the sower humor
to his Contrary, soe in time this Course may Cure himm whiche
disease is difficult. Sir Walter Rauly

for a bytinge with a madde dogge many
other, besides the bytinge of any other beast

Take a good quantity of the heare of the dogge and frye it in
vnwasht butter, And soe lay It to the soore, and It will drawe
out the venome of the dogges teethe howmuche soever, then apply
Mr stallenge his salve or powltes and It will sone Cure It.

for any Cutte or greene wounde

Take Isoppe and stampe it well and with the yearbe soe stamped
and Cheefely the Iuce therof, and mixe it with fine suger and
dresse the wounde well therwith and fille vppe the place, and
it will sone heale,

To kill any tetter

Take first oyle ^of and wheate ^& temper it with fastinge spittle of a howldsome bodye
well therwith and that will kill the humor, then take rosomary
and Isoppe, and in may strippe the leaves downeward, and
boyle them well in may butter and Rosen, And when it is
well boyled make a playstertherof stroke on lynnen Clothe
and lay It to the sore, and that will skine heale and dry it,

A good vomyt and famylier

Take Arabacca three of the leaves, and lay it in steepe in
good white wine all night and drincke It,

A good medicen to avoyde fleame
and winde and also a good Restorative

Take a pinte of good Aquavita and a quarte of stale Ale
of Elicompane Rootes beaten and finely searsed, of sinomond
so vsed likewise of eache one sponefull, of refined suger
halfe a pound beaten, mixt them well togither, and putt
all into a glasse bottle, and when you Intend to vse any of
It, first shake the bottle well, then take a sponefull or two
at a time, when you find your selfe ill at ease, Mr Bownsalls

lynnen bagge, and putt it into the said ale, soe that It may

Take Alehowffe (or in devon Called haymaydens) and boyle

A bathe to be vsed with bathinge
tubbe to swette by

Take Rosemary halfw a handfull, as muche sage, as muche
garden tansey, Camamell one handfull burrage, mallowes
elders leaves Cheafely, Cumfery, bayes, of eche halfe A handfull, boyle all this in fayre water, Then when you
are redy to goe into the bathinge tubbe, you must putt in
a pint of vineger and a pint of Claret wine by the pipe
and soe Cover your potte Close that the streme may runne
into the pipe to the bathinge tubbe, and then putt in Coles
and soe swete as longe as you Canne endure It This bathe
will serve six times swetinge, beinge every time renewed wt fresh wine & vineger

An approved medicen for the dropsey
vsed by Mr Iohn smarte/

Take the Iuce of Sea scurvy grasse and drinke therof A
good drawfte Continually morninges and eveninges with
white wine for a good space togither, and when the vse
therof maketh it lothsome drincke some suger therwith
Also take water Cresses, and brookelime and stampe them
with good ale, and streine them and therwith washe the armes
thighes legges and other limmes once in two dayes, and by
thus vsinge theis thinges, all the dropsey humor will Runne
and swett from out the body of the patient and it will by
gods helpe perfectly Cuer/

A good medicine for the palsey

Take oyle of Amber Could & annoynt by the fyer any
limme, Sinowes, or ioyntes that is affected to the

A very good medicine for Swellinge or
Aches in the Ioyntes

Take A white stone that is to be had in barran heathy
growndes (or other dryinge stones may be as good) heate
them very whott, then putt them in the partyes owne water
& when by so often doinge it is made very whott, howld
the greaved parte over the steeme of the sayd vrin & Cover
all so close that the parte greaved may swette & so
Continue it still renewinge the vrin with hott stones a
good space, after you thinke the parte hath swett a good
spanne well, Cloth it vpp warme & goe to bedd, olso if
you will you may only heate the stones & putt them in
warme Clothes & lay them as nere to the partes greaved
as you Can suffer them as you lye in your bed, therby
to procure the parte to swett & so by Changinge & ren-
deringe them enforce it to swett excedingly & so vsse
it as longe as you thinke fitt & after keape the parte
very warme: both wayes ar very good, but what doth doe
good to one may misse in an other. The stones in the
Cowntye of devon ar Called white Aker stones.

A very good medicine for the payne in the
knees by Swellinge or otherwise.

Take a Sheepes heade newly killed & slitt it in to & put
it into six quartes of runninge water & putt therwith of
plantan, of Sage, of Camomill, of Rosemarye, of each one
good handfull, slippe them all downeward & boyle them
all well together vntill the heade be very tender, then
pull out the bones very cleane & choppe all the fleshe
& hearbes well together, then putt in a good handfull of
greate otemeale & so boyle it agayne vntill the otemeal
be very tender, then lay it powltes wayes to the knees as
whott as you Can suffer it & let it lye to at discretion
& vsse it as you see Cause.

An other for the same.

Take a quarte of stronge ale & thre handfulls of Sma-
ledge (alias march) and one quarter of a li of vnwasht butter
& boyle all these together vntill halfe be Consumed & then let
the patient vsse it at discretion & no dowbt: but they shall find
ease.

A very good medicine for A bruse or Swellinge
in the legges or any other parte

Take new Cow donge (and esspetially in May) & boyle it
well with new milke & some sallett oyle & spread it very
thick on a linnen Cloth & lay it whott to the greaffe & re-
new it as you see Cause & vsse it for a fornights space
together. Olso Cow donge & unwasht butter boyled together
is excelent good.

A good medicine for A Surbated & fyreye
heate or Swellinge in the ffeete.

Take & howld your feete in a Runninge streame of
water from the Sonne risinge to the sonne settinge or for
xij howers at the least & it will Cure that maladye.

A good medicine for A soore on the Eye
Called A wert growinge on the Eye lidd

Take a goosberye bushe newly gathered & with one of the
prickes therof make as if you wowld prick the soore place
and so thrust at it severall times but doe not hitt it by
any meanes: but Come as nere it as you Can without
hittinge it & doe this the morninges together & it wil weare
away.

A good medicine ffor A wenne

Take the hand of deade bodye & stick the wenne therwith
thre times & it will weare away. Olso weare a plate of leade
as thinne as twopence vppon the wenne, as bigg as it is, tyed
on as hard as you can suffer it, by holes made in the leade
& if you first rubb the leade with quicksilver very well
killed & then layd on, it wilbe the more effectuall, but wth-
out it, it hath Cured many. Olso take fatt egges & roste
them & with the swett ^that will arise of the egges annoynt the wenne well
therwith & if it gathers to a heade prick it with a goosberye
bushe & it will weare away.

A very good Approved water for the stone.

Take the wild parsneppes that comonly growes in ditches
in some places Called bellowes & in other places Called
water psley, distill the hearbe therof (without the roote)
or if you will have it more stronge & effectuall, stampe
it before you still it, & only still the Iuce therof (or the
hearbe as afforesayd as you see Cause And the wather
water therof dinke every morninge one, two or thre sponefulls
at the most with so much white wine & in vsinge it con-
tinually (as it must be without any Ceasinge, it will make
one voyd stones & gravell in abundance without payne
& keape one free that doth vsse it.

An other medicine for the same

An excelent medicine for the mother

Take the shredes of shomakers leather & burne them

close that none of the steme may goe into your mouth
or nostrells, for that may be hurtfull for you.

A good medicine for the runninge of the Reynes

A very good medicine for the migrume

Take one hand full of Angle ditches or red wormes
a quantitye of Comin seed, some bole arminack & the
white of an egge, beate all together savinge the wormes
whiche beinge alive bruse them a little, then take &
putt them altogether & make a playster to the bignes
of the foreheade & lay it on & lett it lye thre dayes,
then renew it agayne & so Continue it nine dayes
together, renewinge it every thre dayes.

To make a womans disease Come in
right Cowrse.

Take a quantitye of greynes & some safforne & as much
madder as of greynes & beate them all very well & then
drinke some of the sayd powder fastinge, in white wine
sacke or a little warme brothe.

=+ An oyntment for any Aches paynes or
Swellings in any parte of the body eyther
Could or whote Causes/ or for any Takinge

Take an handfull of parsly, one handfull of Chickweed, one
handfull of Sage, one handfull of hearb Robert (that wch hath the
red stalkes is best) halfe a handfull of peny leaves, halfe a
handfull of fetherfew, halfe a handfull of grownsell, a few violet
leaves, a little quantytye of hemlocke, wash all the hearbes very
cleane & dry them well, then shred them very small & beate
them very well wth halfe a pownde of vnwasht butter (wthout salt)
then boyle them vntill the hearbes be so parched or dried in the
boylinge that one may Crush them to peyces betweene ones fingers,
then streyne it & vse the oyntment twise or thrise a day to the
place greeved, it beinge melted in a sawcer first by the fyer,
& Chaffe it well in wth a warme hand, still keepinge the
oyely ch cloth vnto it (for cleane clothes are not so good), if it chancd
chance to swell then boyle all the leaves so mutch the lesse &
a little crush them from the butter & so lay them to powltesse
wise, & it will take away the swellinge/ Mrs Elizabeth Kinge

The hearbe Robert vseth to grow in hedges, in wet mossy
grownd, it hath a leafe like a three leaved grasse, but
groweth in branches & higher, & beareth a little red flower,
& a little sprigge or spill in the midest, & the hearb & flower smelleth
sweete/. If yow lay the hearbs too, powltesswise it
will draw the payne so mutch the sooner from the bone, though it
doe not swell at all./

=+ A good Electuary for the Stomacke

Take 3 ownces of Romane wormewood & beate it very small, then
to every ownce of wormewood take 3 ownces of shugar & beate it very
well together, then take one ownce of greene ginger cut in small
peyces, & halfe an ownce of a preserved pomeciteran peele
& a quarter of an ownce of preserved orange peeles, cut all
thesse small & beate them well together, then take a grayne
of muske & a grayne of ambergreece & grinde it with a little sugar
very small, & sett your stuffe vpon a chaffingdishe of coles, and
give it a little drijnge & then put in your muske & stirr it well
together & put it when it is could into a gally pot or a glasse
take every day before dinner an hower or too as mutch as ^a wal-
nut, and as mutch before supper & then drinke a cup of wine
sutch as you like best wth some nutmeggs & sugar in it, and
if it like you white wine is best

A good medicine for a Wenn or Wart

You must take a peece of fresh beefe and rubb him exceedingly for a quarter
of an hower together & you must doe it fiue or sixe tymes in a daye & at euery
rubbing you must vse a fresh peece of beefe & not vse one peece twise and this
you must doe for a fortnight togeather & soe the wenn will were awaye./

Take the powders following ^beinge all very finely beaten & searced through fine sa.. of the raggs of Pearle. Of red Corall
of Crabbs Eies. Of Hartes horne. Of white Amber. Of each the like quantitie
Then take as much of the black tippet of Crabbs Clawes in powder.
as all the other powders, for it is the materiall Agent in this receipt
Then you must make it vp in Balles wth a Jellye made wth hartes horne
wherevnto you must infuse some Safforne and when they are all fully
decocted & the balles hard Vse to take it from three ^five graines to tenn graines
in powder in some good liquor every daye or three tymes a weeke or
more often if there be cause, when you take it you must fast after it
an hower. It wilbe best to vse it for a weeke together eyther in the morning
fasting, or last at night.

To cause a woemans disease, come vppon her.

Take some Angleditches or redd wormes and splitt them, then
wash them first cleane with water, and after wards with good
white wine, vntill they be very pure and cleane, then sett them in
an oven when the bredd is taken out, and when they be well
dried beate them into powder and take one spoonefull of that
powder, & puttit to three or fower spoonefulls of good white
wine, the wine must be first made bloodd warme before you putt it in
then let the patient drinke it fasting for three morninges in the change
of the moone. Allsoe take the best mirre & temper into Pills wth redd
rose water, & take twoe pills therof fasting, as bigg as greate ffrench
Peason, this is allsoe as good a medicine as the other./

A very good salue or ointment for a sore brest
or any age bruise or strayne or the like maladie

Take of the Sotherne=woodd Self=heale, Smaledge, Sage, Herbe bennet
Planten, Ribbworte, Hearbe grace, Orpine, Howsegreene, Parseley,
Marigols, Wormewood, Mercury, Byttonny, Scabias, Valerian,
frett worth Settworth, Cumpferye, Lionstoothe, Buckshorne, of euery one of these
one handfull wash them very well and putt them in a Seeue to run drie
and when they be run frye chopp them very smale then putt vnto them
a quarte of maye butter, that is vnwasht and clarified in the Sunn
and the whaye taken cleane awaye, then boyle all the hearbes in the
butter vntill one parte be boyled awaye, then one parte of it must be
st^raynd to make oyntement salue of, and putt into a pott for yor vse
and after that is done, putt into the other twoe partes that is left
as much pure Rossen & waxe as the quantity of twoe eggs to make
it somewhat stiff to make playster salue and then boyle all togeather
a good while, and soe strayne it and keepe it for yor vse in potts
and as you haue cause vse the one or other as is moste fitting the
greefe, this medecine must be made in the middest of Maye./

To make Ielly of harts horne very good for thos
in a Consumption or in any weaknes.

Take three half pintes of fayre Rininge water & half a pinte of white
wyne & two es of harts horne scrapt, lett it boyle softly in a
pypkin close coverd vntill it come to a pinte, then whilst it is hott
strayne it & put to it a little slist ginger & Synamond sticks, the next
day take of the topp of it & leave the bottom of it that you may haue the
clearest, & putt it into a skillett, & putt to it some fine suger, & a topp
of sweet margerom, half a pinte of Redd Rose water a litle Iuce of a
lemon & of an orange, & so lett it stand a little while vppon the fyer
& then lett it Ronne through a Ielly bagg wth a little Rosemary sprige
in the bottom of the bagge, this Ielly you may eu eate cold & for the
moore speedy remedy f of the consumption melt it & drinke it
warme.

For the making of Spirrit of Roses.

Take half a score bushells of roses more or lesse; beat them
in a stonnen morter with a woodden pessell untill they be
cloggy and heng togeather, then put them in an oken fearkin
press them well togeather cover them and let them stand in
a seller for 8 or 9 dayes or longer untill they smell sower.
Then take a 6 part of these roses put them in a glassen ?
set a head upon it, lute it well and lute a large recever
unto it and so still it in Balneo as long as it will runne
When it runneth no more then let all coole before yow
open it. When it is sufficiently could then take the dry
rose leafs out of the body and put an other 6 part of
yowr fermented roses in it, pore over it the rose  water
which yow have drawen, still if again luted as before
Reiterate this operation taking still a 6 part of your ro=
ses and poring on all the rose water yow have drawen
by the former distillations untill yow have no more roses
left; Then take all the rose water which yow have
drawen from all yowr roses and put into a glassen bod=
dy lute a helme unto it and a large receaver well
luted to the head; and draw in balneo the 12 part of this
rose water, and so let it all coole, and keepe this by
it selfe for the spirrit of roses, for it is so spiri=
tuous that it will burne like aqua vitae, it is a good
cordiall in all passions of the heart, half a spoonefull of
it taken will rid one of the fitt as soone as anny thing I
know. This liquor must be kept stopt close for else it will
all breathe away in hot weather.

For to Draw Chymicall Oyles out of anny hott
herbe.

A sketch of a closed vessel with tubes attached to a receiver

Take of Sage, Wormwood, Rosmary or anny other hotte
herbe wher yow meane to draw the chymicall oyle of
half a dozen of bushells more or lesse according to the
quantity of the oyles yow meane to darwe; Gather them before they
have anny flowers, and let them dry thoroughly in a
shady place, When yowr herbes are well dryed put a good
quantity of them according to the bigness of yowr brassen
vessell with a refrigeratory wherin it must be made both
for proffit and expedition, there must be one third part of

A sketch of a tundish, or funnel

the still empty; Pore on the herbes as much running water as will
cover them and so let them steepe for a night, next day lute it
well wit brown bread dow tempered with a good quantity of salte
Ad so sett it the fewer under it and with the water the oyle, will

To make Pommatom

Take of mutton seuett of the hardest of the calle a pound, and putt it in
a cleane napkine, and beatt it with a rowling pinne, then picke out all the
stringes and vels, and soe tie it up in a cleane cloth louse and laye then
the cloth, and all in water, and change it three tymes a daye for
fiue dayes, then take your pound of suet, a quarter of a pound
of permasitie of the whitest you can gett, and as much stoveckes
and benjamin, and the wait of a shillinge, and take a pepin, and
par him, and cutt it in 4 quarters and sticke in himme sixe cloues
then take it, and putt it in a silver bassone, or in a cleane erthen
panne, and cover it closse, and sett it on a pot of seatinghe water
some foure of or fiue howres, and allsoe putt into the bassonne
some foure or fiue spoone full of read rose water, then when
it is melted you must powr it softlye ricpinge out of the bottoum
throught a cleane cloth, then when it is a daye oweld shaue it with
a sharpe knife verie fine, and then leat one that hath a cleane
hand wrock it as you doe worke butter often dipinge their hands in
read rose water, and leat heir worke it tell it be verye white, then
leaue it in a cleane hancheker for two dayes, and then putt it in a
gallipote, then working it in youer hand, you maye youes it as
knowe howe.

ffor the Spleene approved

twelue ownces of wax on pownde of sheepes suett of white wyne
on pynte and melt it altogether in an earthen pott then lett them
stand seaven dayes and after lett them be pfectly boyled tyll it lookes
greene then strayne it and soe vse it Spreade vpon a plaster and
layd to the syde

A Reciept to make Sirropp of Roses
the Best

Take two quarts of Dammaske Rose water or two quart
of fayer water sett it on the fyer in a postnett then haue
Ready of a bushell of Damaske Roses pickt and newly gatherd
then put as many as wilbe Coued into the water let
them boyle Coued till they looke white and the vertue
be decockted then Cast them into a Collender then Ringe
them out ^very harde wth your hande savinge the licor Carefully –
and sett it on the fyer agayne and put in as many more
Roses agayne and lett it soe boyle as the other Coued till the
vertue be out of the Roses and soe Ringe them as before
Doe this as often till all your Roses be boyled then take
the lycoure and way it and take as much Shuger as it
doth way and boyle it vpp to a Sirropp then haue Ready
an ownce of sliced Rubarbe and put it into it while it
is whott yf you Wringe your Roses harde wth your
hand and keepe it Close coued in the boylinge and the
fyer from the sides of your postnett you shall haue a pynte
of Sirropp of the two quarts of Water you must take
three or fower sponfules of this Sirrop in a little possett
Drinke the Roses are the better yf they have a little dew
vpon them when they are gathered

ffor the Burning wth Gunpowder

A hanfull of growne sell a dozen heades of houselick stampe and strayne

Continually stiringe it for feare of Burninge and when it is boyled

the sore for three Dressings

ffor shortnes of Breath and fatnes of the body

of the Rosmary and strew the blossomes very thick upon the hunny and
Breade and soe eate it fastinge for a fortnight together

A very good medicine for the Plauge

First when you find your sealfe ill goe presently to bed
and vsse forthwith the Cowrse followinge: Take thre spoonfulls
of fayer water, as much of white wine vineger, one
sponefull of good Iane Treacle and a dryed Settwell
Roote as bigg as ones thumbe grated mingle all those
together & drinke it luke warme & sweate after it
in your bed six or Seaven howers, if an hower after
the takinge it you doe not Cast at all, you take some
possett drinke made with smale ale luke warme: but
in no wise drinke more Cowld, And after the first dau
drawght you may drinke more warme as then it canbe
or you shall like: Butt if you cast, then before yow drinke
any possett ale, drinke an other draught of the foresayd
medicine and if after that you Caste take an other &
doe so vntill you leaue Castinge, and after you haue done
then an hower & a halfe after drinke possett ale as before.
The old lo of Arrundell

An approved drinke to prevent the plauge

Take thre good pintes of the best malmesey & boyle them
of Sage, of Rue, of each one handfull vntill one pinte be

63 121

wasted, then streyne it & putt it over the fyer agayne, then
putt therin a peniworth of longe pepper, halfe an vnce of
Ginger, a qter of an vnce of nuttmeggs all beaten together,
then lett it boyle a little and putt thervnto fower peniworth
of metridate, tooe peniworth of Treacle, a quarter of a
pinte of Aquavitae, sturr them well together & keape this
as liffe aboue all worldly treasure. Take it always warme
both morninges & eveninges if you be alredy deceased, if
not but once a day is sufficient Trust to this for certayne
that in all the plauge time ther was no man, woman or childe
that this drinke deceaued if the harte were not Cleane
mortifyed with the same discease before. This is not only
good for the Comon plauge but also for the plauge which is
called the sicknes of the Smale pox, the meazells, Surfeyts
& divers other kind of deceases.
Mrs Eliza Reade

An excelent playster to Cuer the Sciatica

Take a toste of Cleane Rye bread, stick it full of good
cloves & toste it by the fyer as you would doe any other toste
you lay the toste in a whott pewter dishe & spread it over
with venice Treacle as you would spread it with butter
you putt the toste into an old linnen Clothe & make it
whott & so applye it to the parte greaved.

A very good Receyt to Cuer madnes
or any other Infirmitiyes

Take Sceenae of Alexandrina fower vnces, of Sarsaperilla
five vnces, of licoras tooe vnces, & tooe drames of Anyseeds
tooe vnces of Raysons of the Sonne (the stones taken out) one
pound of Epithamu of duble Camomill flowers, of stecados,
of Hermadactalus, of each halfe an vnce, lett the sarsaperilla
licoras, & anyseed be beaten & putt severally by them sealves
Then take six quartes of white wine & putt all these toe
ther (except the licoras, anyseed and flowers of camomill) into
the wine & stoppe the vessell closse & so lett them steepe all
night you take a greate kettell & fill it with water &
into that water putt the vessell wherin the wine & Simples
ar, it beinge stopped very Closse when the water boyles, lett
it so boyle one hower & a halfe, then putt in your other
thinges before reserved & so lett it boyle one hower & a halfe
more, Then take it of & streyne it through an indiferent
fine Cloth & putt that liquor by it sealfe, then to the substance
remayninge in the streyner putt six quartes of good beare or






122
Ale & lett it be stopped agayne & boyle thre howers, then
take it of & streyne it.
The first drinke must be vsed thre or fower times a day
fower or five sponfills at one time, the second drinke must
be vsed at all times when the patient is thirstye, without
drinkinge any other drinke whilest it lasteth.
Mr Breedon

A Spetiall Cuer for the french poxe

Take of Sarsapeerilla six vnces, of Sarsafras thinne sliced
thre drames, of Egremonye one handfull, of Sinamond grossly
brused halfe an vnce, Scrape the Sarsapeerilla & devide
in the midest & bruse it a littel in a morter, then infuse it
in twelue pintes of fowntayne water twelve howers with
the Sarsafras & egremonye, then boyle in a dubble vessel
otherwise called Balnea maria or neptuni untill the one
parte of thre be Consumed, In the end of the decoction
add the Cinamon, and then strayne it and vsse it for
your ordinarye drinke for thre weakes together drinking
in all that time no other drinke at all.

A spetiall approved medicine for the bloddye
fflux, and to make one avoyd fleame & wind
and very good for Consumption & a good Restorative

Take two vnces of Sinamon, two vnces of mace, two
vnces of Elicompane Rootes dryed, halfe a pound of
refined Shuger beate all very well & serce all but
the shuger and then putt them all into a pinte of good
Aquavitae & a quarte of stronge ale and so shake them all
for your vsse. And when you have occation to vsse any
of it first shake the glasse that all therin may be very well
mixte together then take tooe sponfulls therof in the morninge
fastinge, & as much at the eveninge last & so Continue
takinge it vntill you find healpe, iff the flux be very
stronge take fower sponfulls at the times afforesayd.
Mr Bownsall

A very good Iulope to Coole ones heate or
Thirste in A Burninge feavor

Take french Barlye & washe it cleane in severall waters
then sett it to boyle in fayer water after it hath a little boy-
led change the water so longe as it Cullers it & when
the water is cleane then lett it boyle & take a quarte of
the sayd water & dissolve therin an vnce of Conserue of Red
Roses if the patients bodye be sollible, otherwise ^so much Conserve of
Damaske Roses, then streyne it & putt therin so much white
Shuger Candye as will sweeten it to your taste & a little
Iuce of lemmon & so lett the patient drinke therof Continuallye.

An approved medicine for A Consumption

Take eyght Snayles such as haue shells on the backes and
of they vsse to feede or Crawle on rosemarye or Camomill
they ar so much the better, take out the sayd Snayles out
of ther shells & putt them into fayer runninge water &
then lett them score of ther slyme, wch they will doe of them
sealues, & as you see the water slymye, Change them in to
other fayer water vntill you perceave they be cleane
then putt them for a little time into some white wine, thus
they may be the more cleane, after washe the wine of with
fayer water, for the wine will make the milke Curde, wheh
when you haue so Scored & washed them, then boyle them
in thre pintes or two peniworth of new milke, of A Red
Cowe only, vntill the one halfe be consumed, then take it
from the fyer & take out the Snayles & keepe the milke
for your vsse, And morninges fastinge & late at night
when you goe to bedd, take a Sawcerfull of the sayd
milke & heate it (for it wilbe thick like creame) when
it is warme, putt to it some shuger of Roses to sweeten
it & so drinke it of, and in this manner vsse to take it
as often in a weake or as longe time together as you
have occation & vsse to Carye shuger of Roses aboute
with you & eate therof as often as you thinke fitt, so
it be not at the times you drinke the milke. This hath
preserved (if not Cured) manye in very greate Con-
sumptions. Mr Will Mills

An excelent approved oyntment for any
Scaldinge or Burninge whatsoever

Take tooe good handfulls of greene Sage and as much
Called hearbe Barbery or winter cresses Cassa bulla (or bullen) a hearbe that growes Comon in
Devonshier, shred those hearbs very smale, then wey
them & putt to them the like weyght of unwasht butter
then putt it on a moderate fyer & then let it ientlye
boyle now & than sturringe it untill the hearbs waxe
Crispye or parched then take it from the fyer & streyne
it & so keape it in potts for your vsse: Accordinge to
these proportions you may make what quantitye of
this oyntment at one time that you please to serue
you all the yeare And when you have vsse of the same
annoynte the places greaved exceedinge well then with
with a Cleane feather, and lay to the soores, ether hartes-
tonge, plantan, marigould, or pennye leaues to keap
them from stickinge to the linnen cCothes. This hath Cured
in very shorte time greate Burninges & Scaldinges.
Mrs Elaz: Kinge

A Very good Receyte for to perfume

Take of muske, of Ambergreece, of Civett, of each qter
of an vnce, of orenge flowers one vnce, grind all these
thinges on a marble stone, then mingle them well together
with halfe an vnce of oyle of beane or for wante therof
one vnce of oyle of sweet almonds, and so applye it to the
leather or other vsse you will putt it vnto.

A Receypt of an other excelent perfume and
the manner how to vsse it

Take first the leather that you meane to perfume & Cutt
it out lardge enough to the proportion you meane to vsse
or that you thinke fitt it should be of, then lay it in rosewater
thre or fower dayes or untill it be through wett, then take it
forth & hange it a dryinge, when it is drye if it be not
sweete laye it agayne in rosewater untill it be sweete, then
take it ??? out & hange it vpp & lett it drye well, then it
must be rubbed one peece agaynst the other & made verye
lithe & ientle. Then take of Ambergreece, of muske, of each one
quarter of an vnce, of orenge flowers one vnce, which must
be beaten very fine in a morter or after searced through
a searce or a fine peece of lawne, then putt them altogether
vppon a marble stone & with an other marble stone grind
them very smale as may be fower or five howers together, And
still in the grindinge puttinge in a little rosewater only to
moysten the stuffe withall, when it is made very fine by grindinge
& done as afforesayd then take halfe an vnce of oyle of beane
and the one while with a little of that & another while with
a little rosewater (accordinge as you shall see neade) mo-
ysten the stuffe afforesayd a little at a time, still grindinge it
without ceasinge untill the greatest parte of the oyle be spent
the oyle must be used sparingly for it will asoe five or six
howers at least Continuall grindinge after the first grindinge
and besides some of the oyle must be reserved for the last
grindinge & finishinge the perfume, And when the afforesayd
stuffes ar well ground like to butter, then rubb some of it with
your fingers all over the foresayd leather very thinne that it may
be all alike, when the leather is so dressed lay it in a close
thinge altogether from the ayer for the space of two dayes &
the rest of the sayd stuffe remayninge putt also into a glasse Close
stopped, And at the two dayes end take forth the stuffe grind
it anew agayne on the marble stone puttinge unto it a qter
of an vnce of Civett & then grinde them altogether for the space
of thre or fower howers in the grindinge puttinge in little by
little all the oyle of beane that was lefte & rosewater as before





126
afforesayd and with that stuffe new dresse your sayd leather
after the afforesayd maner. This stuffe is enough to pfum
a Ierkin at the least; If you Can gett no oyle of beane, then
take in steed therof one vnce of sweet almonds newly dra-
wne, After all this is so done laye it very Close altogether
for a weake & the perfume will therby Continue the longer
& better: what Culler soever you will haue your leather
on, you must gett some Colleringe of that Culler & temper
it with rosewater & therwith rubb your leather very well
all over & then dye it very well & after vsse the perfume
as afforesayd.

A very good receyte for the makinge of Manus
Christes good for the wind Collick.

Take of parsley seed, of ffennell seed, of Caraway seed, of
Time seed, of longe pepper, of white pepper, of blacke pe-

seeds & spices one drame: but your Coliander seed must

by the fyer, The. beate altogether very fine to powder,
then take a pownds of fine shuger & beate it also & putt
it into a possenett & putt thervnto six or eyght sponfulls
of red rose water, then sett it on the fyer vntill the shuger
be mowlten, then putt into the sayd shuger & rose water
all the seeds & spices & sturr them all well together & then putt
them over the fyer & doe then as you doe other manus Christes

An other good medicine & a present
remidye for the wind Collick.

Take a pinte of good malmsey, & one handfull of the
toppes of mother time & boyle it welltogether, then streyn
it & so drinke it very warme & it is a present remidye.

To make the best pumatom

Take the leafe of a hogge & peele him pick it Cleane & poole him in
Smale peeces & lay it in waeter ixen dayes Chandgeinge of it
euy day then put it into a Iugg or an Earthen pott & put the pott
into a pott of water that is boylinge & let it  melt & in the Meltinge
putt in the Ioyse of two or three leamons & an ownce of parmisitie
when it is all Melted then put in halfe & an ownce of Oyle of bitter
Almons as much oyle of white lillis a dram of Camfyer pownded smale
then strayne all this into Rose waeter & beate it with a Spoone till it

A Receyte to Cure (allmoste)
all Deceases./

Take {Synamon the beste. i ounce and halfe
{Ginger                         i Drame
{White pepper              halfe a drame
{Graynes of Paradice}
{Nuttmegge                 } each a drame
{Cloues                         }
{Scananthum                halfe a scruple
{Spike                              eight graynes

Bruise all theise grossely together, and put into twenty ounces of speritt of wine: Lett
them steepe therein 4 dayes, stirre them euery daye twice, then streine, an put it
into a glasse close stopped./

Then take 4 ounces of white sugar, and two punces of the before mentioned infusio
and put them into a pinte and a halfe of white wine, and mingel it well together.

Of this latter composicon take euery day or euery other daye once or twice a
spoonefull, or two, or an ounce, or halfe an ounce: Drinke it in the moringe fastinge
or after a little brothe taken, or betweene meales, or a little before./

This is most Souereigne medicyne, l life-giuinge nectar, and is rightly called
the milke of old age; for the often, and moderate vse thereof, makes the mortall
and fraile habitacon, of the mortall soule (as much as in this life can be obteined
or performed) to attayne to a kinde of mortality./

For it renewes the naturall heate, and spiritts, stirres vp all the senses, both
internall and externall, and begetts as it wearea fresh youth, in old men of
cold, humidde, and weake natures, and by reason of its Antidoticall quallety
it is excellent for the preuencon (of allmoste) all diseases.

It stayes and digestes those noxious vapors, which arismoe from the stomacke
cause the swiminge and dissines of the heade, conulsions, the stiffe crampe
of the Necke, fallinge sicknes, Apoplexie, Palsie, or resolycon of the sinnewes
which take away feelinge or motion, Ouercastinge or dimnes of the eyes, and
soundinge in the eares, as also ci=onstant Catarrhes, or Rhumes arisinge out of
the cold cause, or the like distempef.

It cures fluxes fallinge to the eyes, eares, chappes, the windepipe
brest and lunges, it cutts and deminisheth Phlegm clinginge to the lunges
takes away the payne of breathinge, and pursiues and mittigates houpsenes
caused by thicke phlegme.

It purgeth the mouth of the stomacke from rawe humors, and taketh away
the stinkinge of the breath and mouth:

It helpes the weake degestion of the Belly, sower and sharpe belchinges
loathinge of meate not cuminge of a hotte cause swellinge or risinge of
the stomacke, and stretching forth of the belly, cruell paine of the harte
and





128
and tormentinge sighings, a fluxe or colde laske, whereby the meate passeth tho-
roughe, without digestion, for it concocteth, the rawe humors, of the stomacke
and freieth them from corruption, it strenghteneh the concoction of
the belly, and makes it perfect.

It delivereth the liuer, spleene, and meseraicke venies, from all
obstructions and swellings.

It bannisheth the yellowe Iandize dropsey, and all euill disposicon of
the body, and reduceth the habitt thereof to a better state.

It is excellent againste the Collicke and tone, for it breakes in peeces
the stone of the kidnies, and washeth away the gravell and gritt there
of, and cutts a sunder the filth and slime in the kidnies, which else
would turne to a stonie matter.

It helpes a colde bladder, or which hath sucked into it filthynes, is
infeebled, or gapeth with the surchardge of phleagme.

It excellently helpeth a cold and moist Matrix, abolisheth the Rumaticke
diseases of the wombe, takes away the obstruccons and windynes thereof
and suffocations processinge thereupon, and giues vnto those partes
strength and stability.

It procures ablenes, force, and soundenes to old men whose handes shake
and tremble, whose thighes waxe and sleepe, and growe nume, and whose
knees by reason of the excessiue moyster of the sinnewes vse to goe
out of ioynte.

But this is not good for sharpe, or ther agues, an hecticke
feauer, for those that are leane, or haue the consumption
of the lunges

And because it is of a quallety somwhat dryinge, it is better
for full, then leane bodies, for cold then hott, for the auncient
then younge, for the phleagmaticke then Collericke.

How to vse Scuruy grasse
in Ale

Of the iuice of scuruy grasse one pinte, of the iuice of water Cresses
halfe a pinte of the iuice of brookelime halfe a pinte. Take Sage,
Scabious and Egrimonie, of each of theise a handfull, Mace and
Nuttmegs of eather halfe and ounce, put the iuices in five gallons
of new ale, and chop the other hearbes and spices, and put them
in a boulter bag with a peice of leade or pebble stone, after
five or sixe dayes drinke of it.

For winde in the stomache.

Take 2 spoonefulls of brome water, and put to it 3 spoonefulls of woormewoode
water, and drinke it when the paine doth troble you.


67 129
For winde in the belly.

Take burr seedes and drye them, and beate them to powder, and put a spoonefull
of it, into 5 or 6 spoonefulle of beare, and drinke it when the payne doth holde you.

For the strangurie.

Take the pappe of a rosted apple, & mingle it with springe water, as thick as you
canne drinke it, doe this as ofte as you haue any payne.

For the vlcer in the bladder.

Take 7 or 8 spoonefulls of sallet oyle, and drinke it in the morninge, and when you goe
to bedd, if it be offensiue to youre stomacke, you maye mingle with it a smale
quantitie of muskett wine.

For the fallinge of the Vfula.

Burne the powder of white amber, and sucke the smoake of it into youre mouth
through a tunnell.

For an ague.

Take a pinte of redd rose water, put as much suger to it as will make it pleasant
to drinke, & drinke it 3 howers before the fitts take it 3 times.

For the stone.

Take radish rootes, at theire first cominge, and slice them, and put them in a
bis dubble glasse, then put to them, a good quantitie of barbary suger, & stoppe it
close and hange it in runinge water till it be all dissolved into water then
power it out and keepe it to youre vse, takinge a spoonefull of it at a time in
the morninge, as you haue occasion.

An other for the stone.

Take conserves of violetts, of maiden haire, of marsh mallowes, of ech a little
quantitie, mingle it together, & take as much as a nuttmegge fastinge in the
morninge.

An other for the stone

Take the rinde of the roote of Ellder and shaue it thinne, and laye it in
steepe in ale, a so drinke it in the morninge.

A medicen for any ache

Take of redd leade, and white leade, of ech one pownde, Cassell Soape xij vnces, oyle
olliue 4 pownde, lett the redd and white leade be pownded, and sarched, and
the sope scraped in thinne shauinges, then boyle it in the oyle till it looketh
blackish, and hange not to youre fingers, when it is coole, then it is ready
Frame rowles of it and make playsters for aches.

For a ouerfloweinge of them.

Take a quarte of coulde milke, a handfull of redd rose leaues dried, one vnce
of Cinnamon, boyle it to a pinte, straine it: Lett the party take of it firste
and laste, 5 or 6 spoonefulls as longe as shee needeth.

An aproued medicen for the smale Poxe in the throate.

Take a quarte of smale ale & 3 Iewes eares & 3 or 4 figges boyle these together from a
quarte to a pinte. then take it from the fire, & when it is colde put to it 3 penniworth
of white suger Candy, & let the patient drinke of this some 3 spoonefulls at a time, as
often as you will.

For a Coffe or a colde weake & windey stomacke

Take half an vnce of the best oyle of mace, a quarter of an vnce of oyle of Quince, a quarter
of an vnce of oyle of mintes, halfe a drame of oyle of nuttmegges kimycally taken: Anoynte
youre stomacke heerwith morninge and eueninge, keepinge the same cloth next youre
body which you first vse without changinge it.

Good for those to drinke which are trobled
with rhume in theire eyes.

Boyle in youre Ale, sage, bettony, & eyebright, & wash youre eyes with white wine,
& fenell water mingled together, in the morninge and eueninge when you rise
and when you goe to bedd.

A medicen good for all manner of stiches.

Take linseede & boyle it in newe milke, till it be thicke enough, & make a
powltes and lay it to youre paine.

A medicen for to bringe downe

Take a handfull of vnsett Isop, a handfull of garmandeue, a handfull of vnsett leekes
a handfull of mother of time, & as much of markary, and a flowerdeluze roote
pared & sliced, boyle all these in a quarte of white wine, till it come to a pinte
then straine out these herbes from youre wine, & let it boyle a waume or
two, & then put in a quantity of safforn, & sandurs.

A medicen for a stoppinge
You shall take Cardus powder, angellica powder, alicampane powder, licoras
powder, & anieseede powder, you may haue what quantitie you will, you muste
compounde them with the pureste combe honey you can gett, you may haue an
vnce of ech or what you will, but you must take twice asmuch of youre
cardus, as of any of youre other, this you may take in the morninge fastinge
suckinge of it by little & little.

A medicen good for the plage or one that
is poysoned.

Take halfe a pinte of muskadine, & a handfull of herbegrace, a little Camamell
& let it lie an hower in steepe, then set it on the fire & let it boyle vp,
then take it of the fire, & put in some good sallet oyle & aquavitae into it
and so let the patient drinke it, in theire extremity, at any time of the daye,

A medicen to helpe the pox on ones eye

Take 2 or three droppes of the ioyse of dasey rootes, in some hore hownd water, with a
little liue honey in it, and so beinge mingled well together, droppe some of it in the
eyes of those that haue the pox in them, & vse this medicen to htem, till theire
eyes be well

To make almon water

Take a quarter of a pownde of bitter almons, blanch them in colde water, then pownde
them, and straine them with a full quarte of runninge water, then take halfe an vnce
of mercury supplimatum well killed, & put into youre glasse firste, then put youre
quarte of almon milke, & one vnce of oyle of Tartar, & 4 drames of Campheere made
in fine powder, & so shake it well together, till you vse them, for 4 dayes together
& euery time you vse them, you must shake it and vse it euery morninge as
you knowe howe.

A receipte for the drenchinge and
lettinge of pigges bloude.

Take bowle armonicke, red leade, vinecricke, turmericke, & sneesinge wort, of ech the quanti-
tie of a smale nutt, & beate them all together, then take almoste a spoonefull of treacle
& temper it all together, & so put it all together into some whey, or growndes of drinke
and giye to ech pigge such a quantitie as this aboue written:
But the day before you drench them, you must lett them bloude in the roofe of the
mouth at the springe, & vnder the roote of the tonge at the fall.

Doctor Owens water for the Plauge.

Take Rue, Egremoney, wormewoode, Sellendine, Anhgelica, Scabias, Sage, Balme, Rose-
mary as of anie of the other herbes, and you must haue of like weight, then
soake them in the best whote wine you can get, three dayes & nightes, and after
wringe the herbes from the wine, and distill the herbes by themselues, & after
distill the wine, and that water is good for Agues, but put it not to the water
of the herbes, for that with a littletreacle or Metredatum, shall driue away
any sicknes from the harte.

Heerto you may adde 4 vnces of fine Metridatum, & halfe a pownde of Venice
treacle, or treacle of Ieane, to infuse with the rest, & so distill it, then will it
be moste excellent for the Plauge & Agues: This is the addition
of a Doctor of Phisicke.

A present remedy for the wormes.

Receaue 3 cloue of Garlicke, the seedes of wormewoode, 2 penniworth of Alloes, 2 or three
droppes of vineger, beate all these together, to a salue, & then spreade them on a peece
of leather, & laye it to the nauell of the partie that hath worms & the wormes shall
auoyde presentlye.

A medicen for ones belly that is sweld & harde.

Take water, and boyle some Camamell in it a good while, & when it is boyled enough
put some fresh liquor into it, & bath the diseased bellie with it morninge and
eeueninge with this as warme as as the partie can suffer it.

=+ A drinke for one that spits bloode; or hath a
Cough or a Consumption in the lunges

Take one potle of stronge ale, one handfull of vnsett Isop and pennyriall together a
quarter of a pound of figges, a quarter of a pounde of reysons of the sunne, two
vnces of dates, one vnce of licoras, one vnce of Anniseedes, slice the figges, reysons
and dates, then boyle them all together till it comes to three pintes; then straine
it and put it in a close glasse, then put two vnces of white suger Candey
beaten into it, & let the diseased partey drinke of it a prittie quantitie warme
first and last, morninge and eueninge.

=+ Mr Smiths Electuary for the cough of the Lunges.

Take rootes of marshmallowes, rootes of Elecampane each 4 vnces, boyle them beinge well
clensed, and brused in water till they be very tender & the water neere spent, then
straine out the pulpe through an haire strainer, adde to it of the pulpe of quinces
boyled & strained 8 vnces, & 2 powndes of clarefied honey, & boyle all till the
waterines be spent, then add of quicke brimstone 2 vnces, of powder of licoras
one vnce, of Cynamon halfe an vnce, and of saffron 2 drachmes all in fine powder
mixe it well and reserue it as a marmalade, or electuary: take in the morninge
of it fastinge, asmuch as a nuttmegge or a wallnutt, & before meales and at
night as you find occasion.

Oile of Earthwormes

Take earthwormes washt and prepared halfe a pound, white wine two ounces; sweete oile two
pound; Boile them to e consumption of e wine, and e washing of e wormes, then straine e oile out
It mollifies with a mederate heate, asswages paine, very conuenient for such as be bruised, and
haue paine in e ioints. To prepare e wormes, lay them in oke mosse.

A good remedye for the stone in A horse

Take of parseley seed two handfulls, of Burr seed one handfull
of black Sope as much as an egge, of Madder one vnce
giue this in one pinte & a halfe of white wine & some oyle,
If it be a horse washe his yarde with white wine & blacke
Sope, iI it be a mare with fole, leaue out the sope in the
drinke, and to a mare you may rake & giue her with A
glister pipe for the purpose the Iuce of leekes & Sallett
oyle.

A good medicine for many disceases in a horse

Take of wheate meale six pounds or as much as will
bringe all the Simples followinge to a stiffe paste: ffirst
of Anyseed two vnces, of Commin seed six drames, of cow
Corthamus (of Carthamirs) one drames & a halfe, of venecrick
seede one vnce & a halfe, of brimstone one vnce & a halfe,
of Sallett oyle one pinte & tooe vnces, of honey one pownd
& a halfe, of white wine two quartes, All these thinges must
be made into a very stiffe paste, the hard thinges being
beaten very fine & searced, Then you must keape it in
a cleane Clothe for your vsse, And when you have any
occation to vsse it, take therof so much as will make a ball
as bigge as a mans fiste, and this balle you myst Con-
sume by Continuall washinge & lardringe in a gallon or
two of fayer Runninge water, and so give it to the horse
to drinke, It may be at the first he will be Coye or
vnwillinge to drinke it, therfore at the first you must
give it him in the darke & keape him from all other water
vntill he will willingly drinke it. This drinke kills the
wormes, prevents the yallowes & the staggers it healpes
all Cowlds, it is esspetiall good for all surfeyts, it is very
excelent good after travell & longe jorneyes & it makes
a horse fatt & by lettinge him blood twice a yeare it
keapes him from all disceases.

The way to make the powder Called
diapente good for the disceases followinge

Take of Gentian, of Axistolochia, of bay beryes, of mirre,
of Iverye of each one vnce, Tooe sponfulls of these pow-
ders with one peniworth of saforne, and a pinte of Sack
& some oyle & Suger, kills all wormes, distroyes the
yallowes, healps all Cowlds & Coughs. I Cowld wishe none
to be without this powder in redines.





Good Medicins to be given to horses in ther
provender to keape them from diseases

Take polipodium of the oke & drye it & beate it into
powder, Choppe wheaten straw very smale, Boxe leaues
greene or drye, Savin dryed, Elicompane rootes beaten
to fine powder, Severallye given to horses in ther provender

A very good medicine for A gawld horse
backe, to drawe it, skine ,heare, & heale it.

Take growndsall & stampe it, then putt the Iuce therof vnto
a little wheate flower & some sutt of wood fyer finely
sifted, and one sponfull of honey & a yolke of an egge
temper all together & make a salue therwith, and
so applye it to the soore.

A good medicine for A fowndered horse in
the ffeete.

Take a quarter of a pownd of honey; of wax, of Rozen
of mutten Suett, of each a like quantitye, beate the
Rozen, Cutt the wax, & shreed the suett, then putt them all
together in a dishe or a Chafingdishe & Coles Continually
sturringe them, and when they boyle putt the sayd stuffe
into a bowl of fayer water & so make it into Rolles
for your vsse, when you applye it you must first pair
& open the horse foote very well, then shooe him easye
& putt some leather between the foot & the shooe to keape
in the sayd stuffe, then putt in some tarre & okam into
the tooe & after stoppe in some of the stuffe aforesayd
& then putt in more tarre & okam to the heele to howld
in the stuffe & so you may ride him presently & dresse
his foote but once in tooe monthes.

A good medicine for a horse that is presentlye
ffowndered.

Take a quarte of good Sacke & putt therin some of a
mans order (or merde) & sturr it well together & giue
it the horse to drinke & lett him fast after it.

A good medicine to Cuer A horse with A
Coughe or glanders & to make him rune at
the nosse.

Take a pinte of good wine vineger & putt therin fower
sponfulls of good Sallett oyle & so giue to the horse Cowld
or but a little warmed, the lett him be ridd easilye & set
vpp warme, and a day or tooe after giue him an other

82
of the same & so a third time , and after these thre times lett
him haue a sweete Comfortinge drinke, If the horse be
not farr gone lett him not haue the drinke thre times.

A good medicine to purge A horse of all kind
of Glanders or the mourninge of the Chyne.

Take horse Radishe rootes & mince a good quantitye of
them smale & giue it him Continuallye in his provander
& it will Cuer him & he will desire to eate it.

An excelent medicine for the Cough of A horse

Take one dramme of the flower of Brimstone & putt to it
into the yolke of an egge & so giue it the horse downe
the throte, and doe the like thre morninges together lettinge
the horse faste after it & it will Cuer any Coughe.

Another good drincke for A horse that hath
a very soore Coughe.

Take two quartes of good Ale, one pownd of Reysons
of the Sonne, two heads of Garloke, some Elicompane Rootes
some toopes of Rosemarye, two handfulls of growndsall,
shread & stampe the hearbs, then putt in two vnces of Ros
black shuger Candye, halfe a quarter of a pownd of licoras
made into powder, boyle all these in the Ale vntill the
halfe be Consumed then streyne it & putt therin halfe a
pinte of good Sallett oyle & halfe a pownd of sweet butter
& two sponfulls of Tarr & so giue it the horse warme.

A present remidye for the Botts in a horse
or a barren mare.

Take the horse & first lett him blood in the mouth, then
take of Gentian, of mirre, of black Sope, of each one spon -
full, then putt them into a quarte of Raw milke & giue
it the horse with a horse lettinge him fs faste after it.
This is a remidye if all other fayles

A present remidye for A mare with fole
that hath the Bottes.

Take of Brimstone, of Sutte, of the Iuce of Rue & putt
them into some milke in a redines, then take thre egges
& make holes in them that th emeate may Come out, then
fill vpp the egges with some of the heare of the mares foore -
topp beinge shreed very small & also some Sutte withall
and so take out her tonge & betwen a handfull of the
sayde drinke giue her one of the sayd egges whole downe
her throte & so vntill she hath swallowed them all: but if
she be so sick that she is not able to stand then giue her
some of the excremente of a man, as it Comes whotte from him







A good medicine to kill the farcion or farcis
in any horse

Take & open the skin on the breast on the in side wher
the disease is most & with a horne make it hollow
& then haue redye some arsneck & some Ratts bane
in equall portions in powder & with a quill filled
full therof & a stick into the quill thrust out the powder
into the opened place & so Closing the skin together
let it Continue so thre dayes, in which time it will
gather all the Corruption of the farcis to that place
& make it swell excedinge bigg & after of it sealfe
it will rune & fall out, then apply ordinary meanes
to Cure the soore places & the horse wilbe well for
a longe time.

To Cure any scratches, scurffe or mange
in any horse or Beast what soever, & olso
good to take away wartes.

Take of Trayne of stale vrin, of Tarr, of bay salte
of Brimstone, of Sutt of the Chimney, of black sope, of
each like quantitye except the vrin, boyle them all well
together & with the sayd liquor wash the horse very
well & Chaffe it in excedingly & lye him from meate
thre howers & keape him in the house thre dayes, for
if you torne him abrood, when he lyes downe, the gras
will not grow for that yeare, in once well dressinge
or twise at the most, this will Cure any the diseses
afforesayd

ffor the stone in a horse or mane & to
make them make water freely.

Take of Castell sope & of black sope of each like
quantitye & some psley seedes brused, boyle them
all in white wine or ale if you haue no wine
& giue it the beast warme.

A good medicine to kill the farcis or any
Itch in A horse.

Take allome, greene Coperis, brimstone, of each a like
quantitye mingle them together with a little Creame or
freashe greace, then lett your horse be very ^well Curried & the-

with annoynt the soore places & rubb it in very well & lett
him stand tyed vpp halfe the day after.
